# 4. Store build and Shopify

## Platform choice, Shopify plan, trial and account setup

### Why Shopify

- Shopify is the platform this handbook uses for every store, because it is built only for e-commerce and covers domain setup, storefront design, product management, inventory tracking, payment processing, checkout, analytics, email marketing, live chat, and built-in connections to Google and Facebook sales channels [L0069.004, L1062.005, L1304.001].
- Shopify's all-in-one scope means separate tools are not needed for store design, product management, payments, inventory, email, order tracking, live chat and analytics [L1304.001].
- Shopify is the recommended primary platform for dropshipping. It has superior integrations and technology built for e-commerce fulfillment, and it allows unlimited product uploads on day one [L0280.007].
- Shopify is recommended for dropshipping stores because of its supplier-friendly integrations and app ecosystem. Other site builders are not recommended for stores that will run paid ads. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0665.132]
- Shopify was chosen over newer builders for its maturity and feature depth. The newer alternatives were considered too young for a serious store at the time of the source. [L0941.038]
- The course recommends Shopify as the single platform for every store. It is described as the market leader for about ten years, with the best platform and support. Using one account for all tools is preferred over combining several tools. [L1304.002]
- Shopify is the managed platform. The merchant does not host, secure or maintain the software. [L1283.107]
- Shopify has rarely been down for stores. Custom setups add hosting latency and maintenance work. A custom build would need separate APIs, such as PayPal and Stripe, to replicate the checkout, supplier connections and order back end that Shopify includes. [L0788.028]
- Shopify handles the frontend, which is the storefront customers see, and the backend, which runs ordering, checkout, and product and order management. The merchant works in the backend every day, and customers only see the frontend. [L1314.004]
- A hosted platform already includes ordering, checkout, order management and product management, so a beginner only adds design and content. This avoids a custom website, which typically costs tens of thousands of dollars. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.001]
- A first business should not begin with a large upfront website development cost, because that money is hard to recover if the business fails. The priority is to launch quickly on a platform that is easy to use. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.002]
- Shopify is recommended for beginners because it combines ease of use, easy daily management and cost-effectiveness. BigCommerce, Magento, WooCommerce and WordPress can also work, but they are harder for newcomers. An experienced builder can use another platform if they already know it well. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.003]
- Shopify is used to build an online store in minutes or hours without coding or web development knowledge, using free themes and apps. [L0760.003]
- The build is done step by step and does not require prior website experience. [L1060.006]
- Shopify is for beginners with little or no coding experience. It is mobile responsive and handles security topics such as data encryption, fraud prevention and PCI compliance. [L1313.005]
- Shopify can be used for both physical and digital products. [L1313.008]
- Full-time support is available by chat, plus an active community. Answers to most common questions can be found through Google or the Shopify support pages. [L1313.007]
- Large consumer brands such as Kylie Cosmetics, Tesla Motors and Gym Shark run their e-commerce on Shopify, which shows the platform works at very large scale. [L1313.009]
- Many of the largest e-commerce stores still run on Shopify, and a store can grow on Shopify for a long time before a platform change is needed. *(Older, 2019.)* [L0960.074]
- Shopify is suited to beginners starting a new business from scratch, and it is the platform used to build the high-ticket store in this course. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1481.018]
- The coaching team supports only Shopify stores and does not work with WooCommerce. Feedback on a non-Shopify site will be limited. [L1090.146]
- Build the store directly on Shopify rather than building elsewhere and importing it, because imported stores often need a lot of rework. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0671.136, L0671.271]
- A Shopify store stays on the Shopify platform. Moving to another e-commerce platform later requires an app-based migration and substantial rework. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0671.137, L0671.272]
- Moving a Shopify store to a self-hosted platform is not seamless. A developer can model the existing store on a new platform, since Shopify's structure is well established and widely understood. *(Older, 2019.)* [L0960.075]
- Shopify takes a percentage of each sale, so the platform's cost depends on sales volume (see the plan section below). [L0694.024]
- Shopify is a managed platform that is recommended over self-hosted alternatives, which require the merchant to handle hosting, security and customer data protection. [L1283.107]
- Marketplaces such as eBay and Facebook Marketplace bring organic traffic because users visit with purchase intent. Google search traffic requires strong SEO or paid ads. Shopify has harder initial traffic acquisition, but it enables better long-term retargeting and scaling. [L0280.010]

### Alternatives and when they fit

- WooCommerce is self-hosted and needs separate hosting and plug-ins. Shopify keeps hosting, security and support in one platform. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0247.091]
- WooCommerce plug-ins and add-ons are often paid. Compare the total add-on cost with Shopify before choosing WooCommerce. [L1283.107]
- WooCommerce is not recommended for dropshipping. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0803.057]
- Magento only makes sense for stores with around 50,000 to 100,000 or more products. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0803.057]
- Do not mix WordPress and Shopify for new stores. Shopify's blogging system and SEO capabilities are excellent. Combine WordPress with Shopify only if you already own an established WordPress site with significant history and traffic, such as a blog subdomain. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0249.029]
- Use WordPress for standalone blogs or review sites that earn affiliate commissions. Use Shopify for stores that sell products directly. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1279.041]
- WordPress and BigCommerce are alternatives with similar ecosystems to Shopify. [L0788.028]
- ClickFunnels creates single-page checkout funnels. Shopify product pages achieve the same conversion goals at lower cost, with better e-commerce integrations. Shopify is preferred for dropshipping because it was designed for e-commerce fulfillment, not generic funnel building. [L0280.045]
- Shopify is the only store platform used for these stores. Funnel builders such as ClickFunnels are not used in place of Shopify. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0429.069]
- Shopify gives the seller another sales channel and lets them send traffic from Google and Bing to their own products. A separate store also avoids losing buyers to other products on Amazon, because visitors who land on Amazon often buy something else. [L1153.059]
- A Shopify store makes retargeting, upsells and email capture easier than Amazon does. It keeps the focus on your own products, which is hard to do with Amazon's search results. [L1153.060]
- Compare the conversion rate of a digital product on Shopify with PayHip before choosing a platform. Test both with the same offer to see which converts better. [L1216.028]
- PayHip allows PayPal and Stripe to be connected, the two most important payment options for digital products. Check the platform fee per purchase before choosing it, since the lesson does not state the fee. [L1216.029]

**Moving an existing Wix store to Shopify**

- Move from Wix to Shopify for an e-commerce store, because Shopify is built only for e-commerce and has more apps and built-in tools. Keep existing logos, images and written content when migrating. [L0359.042]
- Migration procedure *(older, 2021)*:
  1. Look for a dedicated importer app. None was found for Wix at the time of the source. [L0962.035]
  2. Export the products from Wix as a CSV file. [L0962.035]
  3. Import the CSV into Shopify. [L0962.035]
  4. Recreate the collections, pages and text manually in Shopify. [L0962.035]

### Plans and pricing

**Plan comparison as listed in the 2021 source** (prices from the 2021 plan page; verify against current pricing before relying on them) [L0370.016]

| Plan | Price | Transaction fee | Listed features |
|---|---|---|---|
| Basic Shopify | USD 29/month when billed every 30 days | Highest of the three plans. The text gives 2.0% (see below), but the table entry was not legible. | Online store, sales channels, gift cards, Shop app for in-person selling |
| Shopify | USD 79/month | 1.0% | All basic features plus retail hardware and analytics; POS Lite included |
| Advanced Shopify | USD 299/month | 0.5% | All Shopify features plus data modelling, professional analytics and calculated shipping rates; POS Lite included |

- Add-ons: POS Pro upgrade USD 89/month per location; Remove online store (sell via Buy Button and in-person Point of Sale) USD 9/month. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0370.016]
- The Basic plan carries a transaction fee of 2.0% on sales when processing does not go through Shopify Payments. The Shopify plan has 1.0% and the Advanced Shopify plan 0.5%. These are the 2021 figures and must be verified against current Shopify pricing. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0370.012]
- The Shopify pricing page compares shipping discounts and credit card fees. It has a separate section for third-party transaction fees that apply when Shopify Payments is not used. [L0805.035]
- The Basic plan is the one used for most new stores. [L0805.035]

**Monthly price across sources (conflicting figures)**

- Basic at USD 29 per month was the figure in 2020 and 2021 sources. In 2020 it was described as the recommended tier for most stores and as enough functionality for about 99% of stores. *(Older, 2020-21.)* [L0852.084, L1111.056, L1314.005, L0759.011]
- Basic at 29 USD per month when paid for a year in advance, or 39 USD per month when paid monthly. *(2023.)* [L0964.084]
- Basic listed at about USD 39 per month plus tax after the introductory period. *(2026.)* [L1323.001, L1323.003]
- One source gives about 79 USD per month for Basic and almost 300 USD per month for Advanced. The 2021 table lists 79 USD for the middle Shopify tier and 29 USD for Basic. The two sources disagree; verify on the current plan page. [L0390.052, L0370.016]

**Shopify Plus thresholds (conflicting figures; each applies to a different claim)**

- Upgrade to Plus only if processing fee savings exceed a minimum of 5,000 USD per month. *(Older, 2020.)* [L0268.017]
- Custom Checkout is available only on the 2,300 USD per month Plus plan. Standard plans do not include custom checkout. *(2024.)* [L0276.017]
- Plus is required for full control over checkout design and costs at least 2,000 USD per month, which makes it unsuitable for most new stores. Checkout apps that inject code are fragile and often stop working over time. [L0390.045]
- Plus only makes sense for stores at nine-figure or mid eight-figure revenue. Below that level it is not worth the extra cost. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0963.044]
- Verify the current Plus price and the revenue condition before deciding. The sources in this set do not agree on the minimum. [L0268.017, L0276.017, L0390.045, L0963.044]

**Plan restrictions that change the choice**

- The Shopify 39 USD per month plan does not include staff accounts. Agencies or partners use a collaborator invitation instead, since partner access does not require a staff account. [L0667.039]
- Basic plan changed on 24 January without notice, so it can only sell in free markets and cannot run multi-market setups. Check plan limits regularly, because a plan change can break an existing store configuration. [L0694.023]

### Choosing and switching a plan

- Start a new Shopify store on the Basic plan, not on Shopify or Advanced Shopify. Higher plans are worth it only once a store has more staff or a large volume of sales. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0370.011]
- The switch point to a higher plan is approximately 30,000 to 40,000 USD monthly revenue, according to the 2021-22 lesson. Verify this against current pricing. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0370.011]
- Higher plans charge a lower transaction percentage per sale, so a higher plan such as Advanced can pay off at high monthly sales, because the lower percentage adds up across many orders. [L0694.024]
- On Shopify Advanced, the transaction percentage was about 1 percentage point lower than on the plan below it. Advanced was cheaper when billed yearly rather than monthly. [L0694.046]
- Billing yearly was cheaper than monthly for Advanced. [L0694.024]
- Shopify offers a discounted annual price when paying up front. Committing to a multi-year term is not advisable at the start. Monthly billing keeps the cost flexible while the store is unproven. The annual option can be considered once the store's sales and staffing needs are clear. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0370.018]
- A plan upgrade can come with a temporary free period, such as three free months on Shopify Advanced. After it ends, the store may revert to Basic without notice. Note the expiry date, because a reversion can cancel multi-market settings that depend on the higher plan. [L0694.045]

### Trial offers and how to verify them

**Standard trial**

- Shopify offers a free 14-day trial that does not require a credit card at sign-up. Paid plans start from about USD 29 per month after the trial. [L1314.005]
- The standard trial was 14 days in the 2020-2022 sources. *(Older, 2020-22.)* [L1277.052, L0771.033]
- A credit card is still required to start a plan. [L0370.017]
- The trial period must be paid for only after you have chosen a plan before the trial ends. [L1304.021]
- During the free trial no payment is due, but the `Select a plan` button in the backend must be used to choose a paid plan before the trial ends. Nothing needs to be selected during the first days of the trial. [L1314.015]
- Start the 14-day free trial before choosing a plan. In the lesson, selecting a plan first was said to lose the trial. Do not pay for the Shopify plan during the trial unless the product is already making sales. [L1444.040]
- A new store can be built and worked on during the 3-day free trial without selecting a plan. The plan and the 1 USD first-month offer only need to be chosen before the trial ends, when Shopify prompts for a subscription. [L1304.021]

**Promotional offers by date**

| Date (source) | Offer as stated | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| 2020 *(older)* | 90-day free trial for new stores during COVID instead of 14 days; annual payment said to include two months free | L0852.084 |
| 2020 *(older)* | 14-day free trial; domain name about 14 USD per year | L1277.052 |
| Oct 2021 *(older)* | 14-day free trial plus 1 USD for the first month | L1102.001 |
| Nov 2021 *(older)* | 14-day free trial plus 1 USD for the first month, announced on a coaching call | L1089.001 |
| 2022 *(older)* | 14-day trial, then 1 USD for the first month instead of 29 USD per month, via partner link | L0969.085 |
| 2022 *(older)* | 3 months for 1 USD as a promotion | L1444.042 |
| Undated | Extended trial: 3 days free, then the first three months at 1 USD per month | L0069.006, L1062.011 |
| 2024 (past promotion) | 3 days free, then 3 months at 1 USD per month | L0471.026 |
| 2024 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 1 USD per month on select plans; accessed via course link; no credit card needed for the trial | L0806.042 |
| Oct 2024 | 3 days free, then 1 month at 1 USD | L1105.008 |
| Nov 2024 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L1095.001 |
| Dec 2024 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L0431.001 |
| Feb 2025 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L0675.001 |
| Mar 2025 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L0985.001 |
| Jun 2025 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L0851.006 |
| Sep 2025 | 3 days free, then 6 months at 6 USD | L1286.006 |
| Nov 2025 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD, from a coaching call | L1096.001 |
| Q4 2025 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD; time-limited | L1106.001 |
| 2026 | 3 days free, then 3 months at 3 USD | L0817.002 |
| Source D 2026 | Introductory rate as low as 1 USD per month for a set number of days; Basic listed at about 39 USD per month plus tax afterwards | L1323.003 |

Offers are dated. Check the current offer on the Shopify checkout page before planning around any of them.

**Verification rules**

- Confirm the trial terms before you rely on them. Promotions change, and the same offer may not be shown to everyone. Check the current price and offer on the Shopify checkout page before subscribing. [L1323.003]
- Promotional offers reached through a referral or affiliate link can differ from the public offer. Compare the price on the official Shopify site before paying for a plan. [L1323.005]
- The 3-month-for-1-USD offer was not shown to everyone and appeared to depend on the user's IP address. To claim it, open the link and create the account right away, because the offer can disappear once the IP is recognised. [L0372.022]
- The trial shows at the top of the new Shopify admin. The store must be on a paid plan before launching. [L1292.027]
- If a trial has ended, a trial extension can be requested from Shopify support. It gives 14 additional days. Support may refuse if the offer is only for qualifying participants. *(Older, 2022 reference.)* [L0372.023]
- Each new account can ask Shopify support for an extra two weeks, extending a 14-day trial to 28 days. This can be done regardless of how far into the trial the account is. [L1380.034]
- Reported in 2022: several stores under one account may each get a 14-day trial. Verify this in Shopify before relying on it. [L1444.041]

**Cancelling during the trial**

- The plan selection screen allows a plan to be cancelled before the trial ends without any charge. Choose the plan and note the trial end date so that a cancellation can be made in time. Remaining free trial days are kept before the first payment. [L0370.017]
- Forgetting to cancel before a free trial ends results in the full monthly plan price being charged. The checkout states the cancellation deadline, so note the date when you sign up. [L1323.004]

### One account per store

- Each separate store needs its own Shopify account and bill. A single Google account can manage several stores, but keep them separate to make a later sale of one store easier. [L0359.044]
- Each Shopify store needs its own Shopify account. An unused second account should be cancelled to avoid paying for it. [L0471.025]
- One Shopify account equals one store, regardless of the plan. A single account cannot host several separate niche stores. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0853.082]
- Each separate store needs its own account, because one account supports one store. The name chosen at signup becomes the permanent myshopify.com address to which custom domains are attached. *(Older, 2019.)* [L1002.018]
- Each new store requires a new account. The same email address can be used to switch between accounts after logging in. *(Older, 2020.)* [L1277.051]
- Several accounts can share one login email using single sign-on, but one merchant account number cannot be used across several websites. [L0934.018]
- Related stores can share a legal entity, but each unrelated group of stores should get its own entity. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1279.040]
- Use a new email address for each new Shopify account to keep stores organised. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1444.041]
- When creating a new account, use an email address that is the same as or closely similar to the TikTok account's email. This makes it easy to match each store to its accounts. [L1444.072]
- The sources differ on whether one login can manage several stores. The Google login and single sign-on statements allow several accounts under one login, while the one-account-per-store statements keep each store separate. Keep one account per store, and verify trial counts in Shopify before relying on them. [L0359.044, L0934.018, L0853.082, L1002.018]

### When to open the account

Two positions appear in the set. Use the one that fits your stage.

- Do not open the Shopify account early. Open it when you start building the website, because early setup mistakes must otherwise be fixed later. The 14-day free trial is wasted if it runs before the build. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0458.033]
- The 14-day free trial starts at signup, so do not open the account before the website build stage. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0771.033]
- Opening the account before the website stage starts the trial too early, and early setup mistakes must then be fixed. Wait until the store build step, when you have a verified market and are ready to build. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0771.063]
- Do not activate the Shopify plan before you build the store. [L0458.031]
- Confirm Modules 1 and 2 are fully completed before using the Shopify extended trial link. [L0069.008]
- A Shopify account is required whether you build the demo store yourself or the done-for-you team builds it, so it should be created first. [L1062.010]
- Do not build a full store until the product is getting views and is near about 1,000 TikTok followers. A store is worth building once you see comments asking to buy, or once an Instagram profile with a link is driving buyers. [L1444.038]

### Business email and signup details

**Email address**

- Use a business email address for the Shopify account and all business communication, rather than a personal inbox. The verification email must be opened and confirmed within about 24 hours. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.014]
- A personal email address can be used, including one from a personal account, because it can be changed later. [L1062.010]
- A business email is not required. A regular personal email such as Gmail works. [L1313.001]
- In the sign-up page, type the business email address into the Email address field. [L1314.008]

**Verification**

- If the verification email from Shopify does not arrive, the Verify your email address card on the dashboard has a Resend verification email button. Clicking it sends the confirmation link again to the email address, so the account can be verified within the 24-hour window. [L1314.021]

**Business details**

- The business entity name does not need to relate to your domain name or brand. Simple names such as ER Stores LLC or Online Stores LLC are acceptable. The entity name appears on bank accounts and tax documents; the domain is your public-facing store name. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0249.027]
- A US address is not required to open a Shopify account. It is optional for business setup, and a virtual or physical US address can be added later if needed. *(2024.)* [L0360.021]
- If you have no company yet, pick your home country as the business location (for example, the United States, or the country you live in). [L1313.001]
- Onboarding Step 2 of 2 is titled Add an address so you can get paid. Its fields are First name, Last name, Address, Suburb, Country/Region, Phone, State/territory, Postcode and the optional Business or personal website. Below them is a checkbox labelled This store is a registered business, followed by the Enter my store button. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.009]
- Onboarding Step 1 of 2 offers sales-channel choices. Under Online Store: Online Store, Social media and Online marketplaces. Under In person: At markets and fairs, At pop-ups and In brick and mortar retail stores. Selecting any in-person option leads to point-of-sale offers. The question Are you setting up a store for a client? is for agencies building stores for others. A solo online-only merchant should select only Online Store. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.018]

**Signup procedures (each source kept in full)**

Procedure A, Source D 2026:
1. Enter your email on the sign-up page and click Next. [L1323.001]
2. Enter a password. [L1323.001]
3. Choose the Basic plan in the plan selection and click Try basic. [L1323.001]
4. In the checkout, check the trial end, the renewal date, the plan price (about $39/month plus tax) and the introductory $1/month rate, then click Subscribe. [L1323.001]
5. Dismiss the upsell offer with Not now. [L1323.001]
6. On the 'What can we help you do?' screen, select Sell online and Dropshipping, then click Continue. [L1323.001]
7. Enter a store name (the default is 'My Store' and can be changed later) and click Continue, or choose I'll do this later. [L1323.001]

Procedure B, Source C:
1. Go to shopify.com, enter your email address and click 'Start free trial'. [L1313.001]
2. A business email is not required; a regular personal email such as Gmail works. [L1313.001]
3. On the first question, choose 'I'm just starting an online store' and select dropshipping products as the product type. [L1313.001]
4. If you have no company yet, pick your home country as the business location (for example, United States, or the country you live in) and click Next. [L1313.001]
5. Choose 'Continue with email', set a password and click 'Create Shopify ID'. [L1313.001]
6. Once the account exists, open the settings options and fill in the business details: store name, time zone and main currency. [L1313.001, L1313.002]
7. Under Domains you can buy a custom domain, which becomes the professional domain later. [L1313.001, L1313.002]
8. If sign-up asks for a domain name, Shopify provides a default one ending in.myshopify.com. Any name works because this default domain is not used for promotion. [L1313.001, L1313.002]
9. Open the Online Store section and then Themes, where Shopify's free themes can be selected. [L1313.001]

Procedure C, Source A extended trial link:
1. Open the Shopify extended trial offer link provided with the lesson. [L1062.012]
2. Create a Shopify account through that page (3 days free, then the first three months at $1 per month). [L1062.012]
3. Enter any email address, personal ones included, since it can be changed later. [L1062.012]
4. After the account is set up, either continue building the demo store yourself or submit the done-for-you web design form. [L1062.012]

Procedure D, Source A module flow:
1. Confirm Modules 1 and 2 are fully completed. [L0069.008]
2. Use the Shopify extended trial link to create your account. [L0069.008]
3. Decide whether to build yourself or use Done-For-You service. [L0069.008]
4. If using Done-For-You, submit the web design form. [L0069.008]
5. If building yourself, proceed to the next lesson. [L0069.008]
6. Mark this lesson complete. [L0069.008]

Procedure E, Source B sign-up page:
- Open the Shopify sign-up page and type the business email address into the Email address field. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.008]

**Store name and address**

- The name chosen at signup becomes the permanent myshopify.com address to which custom domains are attached. *(Older, 2019.)* [L1002.018]
- The default store name is 'My Store' and can be changed later. [L1323.001]
- The default domain is a free placeholder that Shopify assigns during sign-up. [L1313.002]

### Admin orientation

- The Shopify admin is the back end where storefront design, product upload, product page design and inventory tracking are managed. The front end of the store is designed inside this admin. [L1304.017]
- The left-hand navigation contains Home, Orders, Products, Customers, Analytics, Marketing, Discounts, Apps, Sales channels, Online Store and Settings at the bottom. Orders and products are managed daily here. Analytics and sales data start to fill in only after the store goes live. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.006]
- Until the store is launched, the storefront is protected by a password, so the public cannot see it. A new store shows only a basic home page with no design or images until the theme is customised. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.007]
- Practise Shopify edits on the free trial, which was 14 to 21 days at the time of recording, or on a copy of a store in progress. This prevents accidental deletions from damaging the live store. [L0921.012]
- The dashboard card titled Your trial just started lists four first setup tasks: Add your first product, Customize theme, Add domain and Set up payments. Working through these four items in that order gives a new store its basic setup after account creation. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.020]
- The Shopify admin home page shows a setup checklist for a new store with Add product, Customize theme, Add domain and Set up payments. These mark the main steps before launch. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1292.029]
- Initial setup checklist on the Shopify admin Home page, in this order:
  1. Add store name. [L1323.002]
  2. Discover products to sell: install a recommended supplier app or add a product manually. [L1323.002]
  3. Set up Shopify Payments: click Activate. [L1323.002]
  4. Customize your online store: choose or generate a theme, then add logo, colors and images. [L1323.002]
  5. Review your shipping rates. [L1323.002]
  6. Customize domain: the store starts on a default myshopify.com address and a custom domain can be added. [L1323.002]
- Settings > Plan shows the current plan, the trial terms (in the example, 3 months at $1 per month), the date monthly billing starts, and the payment method. Plan changes and switching to annual billing are made on this page. [L1305.007]
- Settings > Billing shows the stored payment method, billing currency, active subscriptions including paid apps with their next bill, and previous charges. Use it to track what is paid to Shopify. [L1305.008]
- Settings > Users shows who has access. The Pending tab shows requests from the agency. [L0472.037]
- Shopify login problems can come from being logged into a different Shopify account. Use the admin URL (yourstore.com/admin) to log in to the correct store, or try an incognito window or a different browser. *(2025.)* [L0472.036]
- To deactivate a Shopify store you created by mistake:
  1. Open the Shopify admin. [L1113.031]
  2. Click Settings. [L1113.031]
  3. Open Plan. [L1113.031]
  4. Click Deactivate. [L1113.031]
  Shopify may put the store on hold for a while in case you change your mind. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1113.031]

### Collaborator and staff accounts

**Staff accounts**

- Shopify Staff Accounts let the store owner give team members limited access to the store instead of sharing the main login. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1089.003]
- Create staff accounts for contractors and designers. Set permissions to restrict access to customer data, payment settings and financial information. Each staff member can have different permission levels. [L0276.030]
- When bringing in a VA or helper, use staff permissions so they only get the access they need, such as editing store pages, and not financial or other sensitive areas. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1090.042]
- Permissions to grant: Products, Catalogs, Online store and Store settings. Do not grant access to Orders, Draft orders or Customers. *(2024.)* [L1285.029]
- Settings > Users and permissions > Add staff: invite a person (for example, a hired store builder) by name and email, and choose which sections of the store they can access. This gives them a separate login instead of sharing your username and password. [L1305.009]

**Collaborator accounts**

- Shopify collaborator accounts give a staff member or outside partner access to a store without sharing your main login. Use them instead of sharing credentials. [L0675.002]
- The collaborator request arrives by email once assets are ready. [L0472.037]
- For agencies or partners on the 39 USD per month plan, use a collaborator invitation, since partner access does not require a staff account. [L0667.039]
- Give a private supplier access by going to shopify.partners and inviting them as a partner with partnership access to your Shopify store. The supplier provides the step-by-step instructions, and the lesson does not name the exact menu labels. [L1156.010]

### Cost of running the store

**Fixed costs**

- Shopify is a fixed cost that must be paid every month while the store is running. Stopping payment can lead to the store being shut down. [L0759.011]
- Shopify costs 29 USD per month when paid for a year in advance, or 39 USD per month when paid monthly. *(2023.)* [L0964.084]
- The Shopify subscription at 29 USD per month is small compared with the cost of a physical retail store, which needs a lease, a rent deposit, build-out and fixtures. A store that makes only one 1,000 USD sale per month still covers the subscription cost. [L0759.033]
- Shopify costs in the 2021 setting: a 14-day free trial, then 29 USD per month. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0771.033]

**Startup costs before launch** *(older, 2021-22)*

| Item | Cost | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Domain name | $5 to $20 | one-off |
| Shopify | 14-day free trial, then $29 per month | do not activate before you build |
| Branding and logo on Fiverr | about $20 to $30 | logo and branding |
| Total to launch | about $100 | time not counted |

[L0458.031]

- A premium Shopify theme can sell for around $300 when bought separately. Stores are built on a theme that handles the background code. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0458.032]
- A hosted platform avoids the cost and time of a custom website, which typically runs to tens of thousands of dollars. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1314.001]
- A domain name cost about 14 USD per year. *(Older, 2020.)* [L1277.052]

**Transaction and add-on costs**

- Transaction fees per sale by plan are listed in the plans section: Basic 2.0%, Shopify 1.0%, Advanced 0.5% in the 2021 source. [L0370.012, L0370.016]
- Add-on prices: POS Pro USD 89/month per location; Remove online store USD 9/month. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0370.016]
- Paid apps appear on the Billing page with their next bill. [L1305.008]
- For plans that cost more, Advanced was cheaper when billed yearly rather than monthly. [L0694.046]

**Keeping the cost under control**

- Do not commit to a multi-year term at the start. Pay monthly while the store is unproven. [L0370.018]
- Note the expiry date of any free period, because a reversion may cancel multi-market settings. [L0694.045]
- Cancel any unused second account to avoid paying for it. [L0471.025]
- Check plan limits regularly, since a plan change can break an existing store configuration. [L0694.023]

#### Further details

- Shopify is the platform used to build the online store, and a ready-made Shopify theme is used during the store build stage. [L0506.005]

- Shopify is an ecommerce platform for building online stores. Signing up through a dedicated trial link can give an extended free trial period instead of the standard one. [L0518.001]

- All stores in this model are built on the Shopify platform. Shopify is used to design the store, upload products, and manage orders. [L0759.008]

- Shopify offers a 14-day free trial before the monthly fee starts. [L0759.012]

- Shopify is the e-commerce platform recommended for building the dropshipping store. [L1061.007]

- A Shopify free trial is the starting point for building a new store, and it can be used to test the platform before paying for a plan. Store content pages can be built faster with ready-made content page templates. [L1093.007]

## Store name, domain purchase and domain connection

### Naming the store and the domain

- A domain is the web address for your online store and how customers reach it. Every store needs its own unique domain, for example the source's own site [L0079.001]
- The domain is the root web address people type to reach the store. Check that the name is available and affordable before you commit to it. [L0336.003]
- Choose the domain before you invest in the store build or the logo. Check availability first, because the name you want is often already taken. [L0520.001]
- Check availability before you finalise brand or logo work. The.com version of a good name is often unavailable, so try variations of your product or brand name until you find one that is free and fits the brand. [L0520.008]
- Business name and domain: choose the business name first, then check whether the matching domain is free before you commit. Brainstorm five to ten name ideas so a backup is ready if the favourite domain is taken. [L0522.001]
- Keep the store name and the domain consistent. A business name does not have to match the domain, because the business name can stay broad enough for several businesses. Even so, keep the domain and the store as consistent as possible, ideally the same. [L0964.088, L0964.089]
- A custom domain should match the Shopify store name so customers can find and trust it. [L1250.018, L1250.019]
- The niche keyword is optional. Include it if it is available and inexpensive. A creative brand name that fits the theme is equally acceptable. [L0941.033, L0934.162]
- A store name does not need to list every category. Buyers who search for a specific product will still buy from a store whose name does not mention it. [L0801.106]
- The meaning of the domain matters little, as long as visitors can remember it. [L1325.026]
- Choose a simple, memorable name. The domain can be kept while the brand name changes. One example reused a basic name after a niche rename. [L0661.053]
- For testing, start with a broad, neutral brand name and only a few basic branding elements, because the offer and the creatives decide the first sales more than the logo does. For unrelated products, a general name such as a female first name works for testing. Add full branding once a product has its first sales. [L1428.026]

**Practical name rules**

- Use.com, no dashes or hyphens, three words or fewer, and a name that is easy to remember and type. [L0941.033]
- Do not use numbers or dashes, because they make the name harder to remember and to tell to others. [L0504.006]
- Keep the name short and easy to remember, with no hyphens. [L1111.054]
- Avoid unusual hyphens. Naming is free-form, but the name should be something customers remember. [L0964.086]
- If the.com is unavailable, avoid a shortcut name with an odd ending. A clear form such as shop.brandname.com or brandname.com is better. [L0530.017]
- Check that the name reads the same when spoken aloud. [L0468.058]
- Use a generic company or LLC name rather than one tied to a single store, so several related stores in the same niche can share it. [L1090.046]
- Google requires that an LLC's name is not used as a website. Build one website per sales store. The LLC itself needs no website. [L0934.163]

**Naming procedure (Source B, 2021-22, older)**

1. Brainstorm 5 to 10 business name ideas that identify the market and stay within three words.
2. Check each idea against Google searches for your product keywords to confirm it is unique in the market and not a close variant of a competitor's name.
3. Pick the idea that resonates with you and whose matching domain is available.
4. Confirm the domain is affordable, then buy it so the business name and domain match exactly. [L0336.014]

**Domain-selection procedure (Source A, 2020, older)**

1. Choose a short, memorable domain with no hyphens that ends in.com.
2. Check that the name reads the same when spoken aloud.
3. Check the US trademark registry for conflicts before buying.
4. Buy the domain through the Shopify admin, or through an outside registrar such as Namecheap at about $9 per year.
5. Link the domain to the store before contacting suppliers. [L0468.058]

### Domain extension

| Market or case | Extension | Source |
|---|---|---|
| US store |.com | L0504.004, L0522.003, L0968.037, L1111.054 |
| Australia |.com.au (preferred) | L0504.004, L0522.003 |
| UK |.co.uk | L0504.004, L0521.003 |
| Canada, new store |.com, even when selling in Canada (older, 2020) | L1277.053 |
| Single country, other than US or Australia | local country-code extension | L0522.002 (older, 2021-22), L0520.010 |

- Match the top-level domain to the market where the customers are. [L0521.003, L0504.004]
- Google gives preference to domains that carry the local country designator. [L0522.002] (older, 2021-22)
- A local extension is trusted more by local buyers than a.com. Example:.ch in Switzerland or.com.au in Australia. [L0549.038]
- A country-specific extension can be chosen instead of.com when you target one specific country. [L0520.010]
-.com is the first choice for a store domain because it is the most widely accepted..co is the second most common extension for e-commerce stores. [L0520.009]
- A.com is recommended over.store or similar extensions, because customers type.com by default. [L0964.085, L0838.001]
- For a US store, do not use.net,.store or.org. Customers who come back later will type.com out of habit and land on another site or none at all. [L0504.005]
- For sales to US customers, do not use a.co.uk domain. Shoppers may hesitate to order from a store that appears not to ship to them. [L0967.101] (older, 2020)
- In the US, only a.com is needed. In Australia,.com.au is preferred. Buying both.com and.com.au is possible but optional. [L0522.003] (older, 2021-22)
- Use.com even when you sell in only one country, because shoppers remember and type.com by default. A.us domain is not recommended. [L0968.037] (older, 2021)
- Meta Ad Library shows the domain to viewers. A.us or.store domain can make a pre-lander or advertorial look like a direct sales page, which can reduce trust. [L1327.045]
- Example: a store that sources and sells only within one country used that country's extension,.com.au for Australia, as in an early home lighting store. [L1014.020] (older, 2021-22)

**When the preferred name is taken**

- When the local extension of a name is taken, the search also suggests.net,.org,.online or.us. For a store aimed at one country, keep the local extension and change the name instead of switching to a generic extension. [L0522.018] (older, 2021-22)
- When the.com is already taken, do not switch to a different-looking extension. Change the store name, or buy the.com if the owner is willing. A.shop address causes customers to mistrust email from a mismatched domain. [L0468.059] (older, 2020)

### Checking availability, trademarks and history

**Availability**

- Search the name in the store's domain search or at the registrar, with the correct local extension. If the result says available, add it to the cart. [L0522.004] (older, 2021-22)
- In Shopify's Buy new domain search, the default results are suggestions. You can type a specific name you already chose straight into the search field. Delete any "http://" prefix and trailing slash before searching, then confirm the result says the domain is available. [L0521.018]
- If a candidate domain's website cannot be reached, that usually means nobody is using it. Still confirm availability in the store's own domain search, because an unreachable site does not prove the name can be bought. [L0521.012]
- A message that the domain is taken means someone already owns it, and it cannot be bought for that name. Do not force that name. Search the next backup name the same way and repeat until one shows as available with the correct extension. [L0522.005, L0522.006] (older, 2021-22)
- AI chat tools cannot check real-time domain availability. Their suggestions may already be registered. Taken names often belong to someone else's site or are parked as premium domains for sale. [L0521.009]
- Avoid buying domains that other people hold to sell at high prices. Choose a name that is actually available after brainstorming. [L0964.087]
- Do not buy domain names for resale as a business idea. Many are held for thousands of dollars, and this is unnecessary. [L1111.055]

**Trademark**

- Before buying, search the US trademark registry for conflicts. [L0468.058]
- If a store name or domain may infringe a trademark, search the US trademark registry first. If it does, pick a new domain and link it to the existing Shopify store, which keeps all products and settings. [L0468.060] (older, 2020)

**History checks (procedure)**

1. Pick the available domain you like best.
2. Google "site:" followed by the domain name. Zero results are normal for a new name.
3. Open the Google Transparency Report and enter the domain in the check site status field. "No data available" is normal.
4. Open the Wayback Machine at archive.org and enter the domain to view its browse history.
5. Only if all three checks show no problems, buy the domain. [L0504.014]

- An old site: string search that returns old pages is acceptable if they are relevant to the niche. Spammy or scammy old pages are a red flag, and you should pick a different name. [L0504.015]
- A domain can be available and still have a history of penalties, so the history checks are needed even when the name is free. A blacklisted domain, for example one that once carried a virus, is rare, roughly one in 500, but it can damage a store built on it. [L0504.016]
- If the Wayback Machine has archived a domain, open its old snapshots to check for spam or scam content. Then search Google for the domain's past reviews. If there is no archive, the domain is effectively new, and that is fine. [L0504.017]

### Price limits and premium domains

- Do not pay more than $20 per year for a domain name. Premium domains are often offered for $500, $1,000, $5,000 or more, and buying one at that price is not worth it for a new store. [L0521.010]
- Each store needs its own domain. Do not buy premium or parked domains at high prices. A similar domain can work just as well. [L0801.093] (older, 2021)
- Avoid parked domains listed for resale, since they are often overpriced. Choose an open domain instead. [L0934.096] (2023 price: about $14 to $15 per year)
- Keep the domain cost low: spend no more than about $14 per year on a domain name. Choose a generic name or one that contains your niche keyword. [L1090.046, L1090.045] (older, 2021)

**Price reference (values differ by source, date and registrar)**

| Source | Registrar or channel | Price per year | Date |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A | Shopify, buy inside Shopify | about $14 | 2019 [L0356.027] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify, buy inside Shopify | about $15 | 2020 [L0468.057] (older) |
| Source A | Namecheap | about $9 | 2020 [L0468.058, L0852.083] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify Domains | about $13 to $15 at the time of the call | 2020 [L0852.083] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify | about $12 (for a new domain on a reused store) | 2021 [L0834.042] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify | about $15 | 2021 [L0801.104] (older) |
| Source A | external registrar | about $14 typical | 2021 [L0247.087] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify | about $14 | 2022 [L0665.030] (older) |
| Source A | Shopify | about $14 | 2023 [L0804.043] |
| Source A | Shopify | about $15 per year | 2023 [L0964.085] |
| Source A | additional domain, Shopify | about $14 per year, each extra domain has its own fee | 2023 [L1283.092] |
| Source A | Shopify Domains | $16 per year (price shown in the search) | undated [L0521.011, L0521.016] |
| Source A | Shopify, example | $15 per year (available.com via Shopify checkout) | undated [L0504.012] |
| Source D | Shopify,.com example | about $16.00 USD per year. Some names have a higher first-year price. | 2026 [L1327.044] |
| Source C | Shopify | about $15 to $30 per year, depending on name and extension | undated [L0520.011] |
| Source C | external registrars (Namecheap, GoDaddy) | about $6 to $10 | undated [L0520.012] |
| Source C | reported cost of a product brand domain | about $10 per year | undated [L1428.025] |
| Source B | GoDaddy | roughly $1 to around $20 per year. Introductory first-year prices can be far below the renewal rate. | 2021-22 [L0522.008] (older) |

- Example: a domain that costs $9.58 at Namecheap on regular pricing can drop to about $5.98 with a discount code. The same name bought through Shopify would cost around $15. Always compare prices across registrars and look for discount codes before buying. [L0520.014]
- Source A's 2023 guidance: the domain costs about $14 to $15 per year through Shopify, and you should buy a self-made name, not a squatted one. [L0804.043, L0934.096]

### Buying a domain in Shopify

**Why Shopify**

- Shopify is the recommended place to buy a domain for a Shopify store. Common third-party registrars are GoDaddy, Namecheap and Google Domains. [L0520.002]
- A domain bought through Shopify is live immediately, with no waiting for SSL and no DNS changes. This makes it the simplest option for a beginner. [L0520.003]
- Buying in Shopify is the intended way to get a low-cost name for a new store. [L0521.011]
- The Shopify purchase is quick and needs only a few clicks. [L0941.102]
- Source A's 2019 advice was to buy the domain directly in Shopify for about $14 per year, rather than through an external registrar such as GoDaddy, which was advised against. Namecheap is another common registrar. (older, 2019) [L0356.027]
- Buying in Shopify was used for ease of integration. [L0852.083] (older, 2020)
- A domain bought in Shopify is added to the store's account and billed yearly at the price shown during purchase, for example $16 per year. The buyer owns the domain, so visitors who type that address reach the store. [L0521.017]
- A domain bought through Shopify can be used in the same Shopify account and set as the primary domain. [L1382.060]

**Limits of a Shopify-bought domain**

- A domain bought from Shopify cannot be moved to other platforms such as WooCommerce or ClickFunnels. The lesson's view is that this rarely matters if you stay on Shopify. [L0520.005]
- A domain bought through Shopify can be transferred to another registrar such as Namecheap or GoDaddy. Every domain registrar allows transfers. [L1094.064] (older, 2023 source; conflicts with the platform limit above, which concerns platforms, not registrars)

**Procedure A (Source A, undated)**

1. Log in to your Shopify admin.
2. Click Settings.
3. Click Domains.
4. Ignore the default myshopify.com domain.
5. Click Buy new domain.
6. Enter your chosen domain name.
7. Confirm availability and $16 per year pricing.
8. Click Buy.
9. The domain is added to your Shopify account.
10. Shopify automatically assigns it as your primary domain. [L0079.009]

**Procedure B (Source C, undated)**

1. In the Shopify admin, click Settings in the bottom left corner.
2. Open Domains.
3. Click Buy new domain.
4. Type your desired name and check whether it is available.
5. If the name is taken, adjust it and check again. Test several options.
6. Select an extension, such as.com,.co, or a country-specific extension.
7. Review the details and the price, then click Buy domain.
8. Once purchased, the domain appears in the Domains section. You can set it as the primary domain so that visitors land on it. [L0520.007]

**Procedure C (Source A, undated)**

1. Log in to the Shopify admin.
2. Click Settings.
3. Click Domains in the left navigation.
4. Click "Buy new domain" at the top.
5. Delete the "http://" prefix and the trailing slash from the entered name, then search for the domain.
6. Check that the search result says the domain is available.
7. Click "Buy" to add it to the account.

The domain is billed yearly, and it becomes the primary domain once purchased. [L0521.015]

**Procedure D (Source A, 2024 path)**

- Open the Shopify admin, then go to Settings > Domains in the left navigation to view and buy domains for the store. [L0521.013]
- Once the purchase is made, the domain shows in the list. Source A's 2024 lesson states that a new domain can be bought through Shopify (recommended for simplicity) or elsewhere, then linked under Settings > Domains, where it takes effect immediately. [L0971.026]
- A new store starts with a default address made of random characters followed by.myshopify.com, which redirects to the domain you choose. [L0504.011]

### Buying a domain at an external registrar

**Cost and reasons to choose it**

- External registrars such as Namecheap and GoDaddy typically cost about $6 to $10 per year, which is considerably cheaper than buying through Shopify. [L0520.012]
- External registrars are the better choice when budget matters, or when the domain may later be used on other platforms such as WooCommerce or Wix. The trade-off is that they do not include free email forwarding, so a separate paid email service is needed for a professional address. [L0520.013]
- A domain held at an outside registrar is easier to move later and makes business email simpler to set up. [L0989.019] (Source E, 2025-26; this conflicts with the Shopify recommendation above. Both apply: Shopify for the simplest launch, an outside registrar for cheaper, portable and email-ready domains.)
- Source A's 2023 lesson: domains can be purchased from any registrar and connected to a Shopify store. You do not have to buy them through Shopify. [L0934.095]
- Buying a domain through GoDaddy and linking it to Shopify avoids TikTok warnings about links in the bio. A professional business email from the domain can be used for the store's social accounts. [L1381.058]

**Procedure E (Source C, undated)**

1. Open the registrar's website.
2. Search for the desired domain and confirm it is available.
3. Compare the price and look for a discount code.
4. Add the domain to the cart and complete the purchase. [L0520.015]

**GoDaddy purchase (Source B, 2021-22, older)**

1. Search the domain on the domain search page with the correct local extension.
2. If the result says available, click Add to Cart.
3. Check out and pay by credit card. Checkout creates a GoDaddy login account for the buyer. [L0522.004, L0522.007]

- Do not add any extra products or add-ons at GoDaddy checkout. Buy only the domain itself. [L0522.009]
- GoDaddy's domain search lists.com.au results with a "Restrictions apply" note, which signals eligibility rules for the registrant. Check that the buyer qualifies for the local extension before adding it to the cart. [L0522.014]
- GoDaddy may display prices in a currency that does not match the target market, such as euros for an Australian or US store. Confirm the currency and the final amount in the cart and at checkout before paying. [L0522.015]
- The cheapest first-year prices at GoDaddy are often tied to a two-year registration. For example, about €0.99 for the first year applies only when registering for two years. Compare the renewal price for each period before choosing the term. [L0522.016]
- GoDaddy's domain search may offer a broker service for a domain that is taken. Treat this as a last resort and move to a backup business name first, because restrictions apply. [L0522.013]
- GoDaddy is a domain registrar used to search availability, register domains and manage the account. Other registrars can be used instead if an account already exists there. [L0522.012]

**Namecheap purchase (Source E, 2025-26)**

1. Go to Namecheap, search for the domain, and add it to the cart.
2. Check out with domain privacy kept on.
3. Leave auto-renew off for now.
4. Click Confirm Order.
5. Create a username, enter your details and click Continue.
6. Add a payment card and click Pay Now.
7. Open the domain and click Manage. [L0989.021]

**Comparison of the two routes**

| Point | Buy in Shopify | Buy at an external registrar |
|---|---|---|
| Typical price | about $15 to $30 per year (Source C), about $16 (Source A) [L0520.011, L0521.011] | about $6 to $10 (Source C) [L0520.012]; about $9 at Namecheap (2020, older) [L0468.058] |
| Ready for the store | live immediately, no DNS or SSL wait [L0520.003] | DNS and SSL changes needed, with a waiting period [L0520.016, L0520.017] |
| Email | not covered by the purchase; a separate email service is needed [L0520.013] | no free email forwarding; a separate paid email service is needed [L0520.013] |
| Moving later | cannot be moved to other platforms such as WooCommerce or ClickFunnels [L0520.005] | easier to move, per Source E [L0989.019] |

### Connecting an external domain to Shopify (DNS, TXT and SSL)

**Procedure F (Source C, undated)**

1. In the Shopify admin, click Settings in the bottom left corner.
2. Open Domains.
3. Click Connect existing domain instead of Buy new domain.
4. Type the exact domain name you bought from the external provider.
5. Click Next. If the domain was bought externally, Shopify shows the option to continue.
6. Follow the DNS settings Shopify shows, and make the changes at your external registrar. Shopify's official guide describes the exact DNS settings to use.
7. Expect a waiting period for the DNS changes and SSL before the domain works with the store. [L0520.016]

- Use the connect existing domain option only for a domain already bought elsewhere, such as at Namecheap or GoDaddy. Shopify has documentation for the connection steps. [L0504.013]
- A domain registered elsewhere can still be connected to Shopify, but it needs more setup than one bought in Shopify. [L0941.102]

**Procedure G (Source E, 2025-26, Namecheap)**

1. In the Shopify admin, go to Settings > Domains.
2. Click Connect Existing Domain and enter your domain.
3. Shopify reports that the DNS records do not point to Shopify yet and shows the records to change.
4. Log in to Namecheap and open the domain's Advanced DNS tab. [L0989.023]

| Record | Name / host | Current value | Change to |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | as listed by Shopify | 162.255.119.46 | 23.227.38.65 |
| CNAME | www | parkingpage.namecheap.com | shops.myshopify.com |

[L0989.024]

1. In Namecheap's Advanced DNS, update the existing A record from 162.255.119.46 to 23.227.38.65.
2. If no A record is listed, use Add New Record and choose A Record with the value 23.227.38.65.
3. Update the www CNAME record from parkingpage.namecheap.com to shops.myshopify.com.
4. Click Save All Changes.
5. Return to Shopify and click I updated it.
6. Wait a while for DNS propagation and the TLS certificate to complete before assuming the connection failed. [L0989.025]

- Right after DNS changes, Shopify shows DNS propagation at 0 and the TLS certificate as not provisioned. These statuses are normal during the waiting period, so do not change the records again straight away. [L0989.026]
- After you connect a custom domain under Settings > Domains, the connected domain may show as redirecting to the store's default myshopify.com address until the DNS records are correct. Expect this status during setup, and do not treat it as a final result. [L0989.032]
- Expecting the external domain to work immediately after connecting it is a mistake. DNS changes and SSL setup take time, so the store will not load on the new domain right away. [L0520.017]
- Source A's 2024 lesson says a domain linked under Settings > Domains takes effect immediately. The DNS wait described above applies to external domains. Treat the wait as the rule when the domain was bought elsewhere. [L0971.026, L0520.016]

**TXT records (domain verification)**

- For a domain bought through Shopify: go to Sales channels > Online store > Domains, click the main store domain and then Manage, then open DNS settings. Click Add custom record, choose TXT, keep the Name as @, paste the verification value into Text value and click Confirm. [L1025.015]
- In Shopify's Domain settings > Add custom record, enter @ in the name or host field so the record applies to the whole domain. Paste the verification value from Search Console into the value field and confirm. [L0735.013]
- For a domain bought from a registrar other than Shopify, add the TXT record in the DNS settings of the site where the domain is hosted. [L1025.016]
- Always use the main domain, not the myshopify.com address, when editing DNS records. [L1025.016]

**SSL**

- The SSL certificate on Shopify is free. Enable it through Shopify's documentation to show the HTTPS lock and secure connection badge to customers. (2022, older) [L0249.020]
- An SSL certificate is included with Shopify, so buying one separately is unnecessary. [L0934.097, L0934.160] (2023)
- Shopify's 2022 advice is to enable SSL through its documentation. The 2023 advice says it is included. Both describe the same certificate: it is free, and you do not buy it separately. [L0249.020, L0934.097]
- A newly added custom domain can show the status Provisioning TLS while its SSL certificate is still being issued. Wait until this status clears before treating the domain as ready for visitors. [L1330.026]
- A domain bought through Shopify does not wait for SSL. [L0520.003]

### Primary domain, redirects and www

**Setting the primary domain**

- After buying a domain, open Settings > Domains, find the new domain in the list and click Set as primary. From then on, anyone who types that domain lands on the storefront, the public side of the store. [L0504.018]
- Connect the domain and set it as the primary domain before launch. [L1145.017]
- The purchased domain must be set as the primary domain. Additional domains can be connected or transferred without creating a new Shopify account. [L1305.037]
- Admin path for a primary change: Online Store > Domains > Change primary domain. Select the custom domain and click Save. Traffic from all domains then redirects to the primary domain. [L1292.018] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Under Primary for Online Store you can switch the primary domain. [L1250.018] (Source D, 2026)
- After a custom domain is bought, check the Domains list under Settings to confirm it is marked as the primary domain. The default.myshopify.com address should no longer be the one shown to customers. [L0521.019]
- Leaving the default Shopify-assigned domain as your public address is a mistake. Use a professional domain that represents your brand and set it as the primary domain. [L0520.018]

**Auto-assigned or manual primary (sources differ)**

- Shopify automatically assigns a newly bought domain as the primary domain. [L0079.009 step 10]
- Once bought, the domain becomes the primary domain. [L0521.015]
- Shopify's Settings say no additional configuration is required after purchase. [L0079.010]
- Other sources say the purchased domain must be set as primary manually, from Settings > Domains. [L0504.018, L1305.037]
- Check the Domains list after purchase, whichever way it was set. [L0521.019]

**Redirects, www and non-www**

- If traffic from the other domains is not redirected to the primary domain, the page shows an "Enable redirection" link. After the primary domain is changed, the page shows "Traffic from all your domains redirects to this primary domain" with a Disable redirection option. [L1292.035]
- When a custom domain is connected, Shopify also adds the www version as a third-party domain with its own SSL status. The www and non-www addresses redirect to each other, so visitors reach the store through either one once the primary domain is set. [L1292.036]
- Use the non-www version of the custom domain as the primary domain, because www and non-www redirect to each other and the non-www form is simpler. [L1292.019] (2021-22, older)
- Check that the www version is connected to the store and set to redirect to the root domain, so the store has one main address. [L1327.063] (Source D, 2026)
- In Settings > Domains, the www variant of a custom domain can show "Redirects to the myshopify.com address" during setup. [L1330.027]
- Two domains pointing to the same Shopify store show identical content, which splits brand authority. Choose one domain and build its authority. Redirects are possible but not recommended. [L1088.032] (2020, older)

### The myshopify.com address

- Every Shopify store keeps an automatically generated myshopify.com domain that cannot be deleted. It should remain as the backend, while the custom domain is the primary public domain. [L1292.017]
- The admin default domain on Shopify cannot be removed, so the custom domain must be set as primary to avoid customers reaching the myshopify.com address. [L1292.026]
- On the Domains page, the myshopify.com address is listed under Shopify-managed domains with the note "Never expires". It cannot be removed. [L1292.037]
- The myshopify.com entry is labelled Primary for Online Store in the Domains list until a custom domain is set as primary. Use the myshopify.com entry for the Shopify URL field in the Shrine whitelist. [L1330.027]
- A new Shopify account starts with a default domain made of random letters and numbers under.myshopify.com. Customers should not see this address, so replace it with a purchased custom domain as the primary domain. [L0521.014]
- The default myshopify.com domain is only a technical placeholder. Choose any name for it, because the store is later connected to a purchased custom domain used for promotion. [L1313.004]
- Using a store on a default myshopify.com address is a weak sign for a business and can appear with unprofessional branding. Use a custom domain name. [L0806.041] (2024)
- A store on a myshopify.com URL looks unprofessional to customers and suppliers. Keep the myshopify.com URL only for demo stores. [L0852.082] (2020, older)
- The myshopify.com subdomain is not visible to customers, so it does not matter much. The store name in the admin (name.myshopify.com) is not visible to customers either. [L0358.037, L0359.043]
- Buying a domain in the admin and linking it hides the myshopify.com address from customers, with one click. [L0468.057] (2020, older)
- Before a custom domain is connected, the store is reachable at a temporary address in the form store-name.myshopify.com, which is also the address shown in the admin header. The store is not visible to the public at that address while it is password-protected. [L1314.019] (2021-22, older)
- A new Shopify store starts with a temporary myshopify.com address. The real domain is connected before the store goes live. The domain choice does not have to be made during signup, and there is no deadline for it. [L1114.026] (2023)
- Register the business name and domain before creating the Shopify account, because the store is set up under that name. The domain is connected to the store later through the Add domain task on the dashboard. [L1314.017] (2021-22, older; this conflicts with the no-deadline point above. Both apply: the domain is not required at signup, but registering the name first is a sound order.)

### Renewals and privacy

**Auto-renew (sources differ)**

- To renew a Shopify domain, go to Settings > Domains in the Shopify admin. The domain is usually set to auto-renew, so check that setting before the renewal date. [L0962.032] (2021, older)
- Turn on auto-renew every year. [L1327.044 step 5] (Source D, 2026)
- At purchase, leave auto-renew off and keep domain privacy on. Once the business is working, go back and turn auto-renew on so the domain does not expire. [L0989.022, L0989.021 step 3] (Source E, 2025-26)
- The two approaches differ at the start: the Source D and Source A sources keep auto-renew on from the start, while the Source E source turns it off at purchase and on later, once the business works. [L1327.044, L0962.032, L0989.022]

**Registration term and renewal price (GoDaddy, 2021-22, older)**

- A domain can be registered for one or two years at checkout. It renews automatically each year (or each registration period) at the price originally paid. A one-year term saves money now, while a two-year term is equally valid. [L0522.010]
- Introductory prices can be far below the renewal rate, so check the renewal price before buying. [L0522.008, L0522.016]

**Privacy**

- Keep domain privacy on when registering the domain. [L0989.021 step 2, L0989.022]
- GoDaddy states that domains include Basic Privacy Protection, which hides the owner's contact details in public registration records. Keep this default on when buying the domain for a business. [L0522.017]

**Stability**

- Once a domain is bought and set up on the store, it should not be changed later. Treat the choice as long-term and do not rush it. [L0522.011]

### Changing, transferring and moving domains

**Whether to change at all**

- Changing a domain for SEO is usually unnecessary, since products, brands and SKU numbers are what rank, not the niche keyword in the domain. Keep an existing domain unless there is a strong reason to replace it. [L0941.034]
- If the store is still new, changing its domain name is fine. Link the new domain to the existing Shopify account rather than building a new store. [L1088.031] (2020, older)
- To move an existing store to a new domain: in the Shopify admin, remove the current domain and connect the new one. Products, categories and settings carry over, and no new Shopify account is needed. [L1002.019] (2019, older)

**Safety rules for a change**

- Making changes to the primary domain, contact email and payment method in one session led Shopify to suspend the store, and support did not explain the reason at first. Make such changes one at a time and check the store after each one. [L1131.061]
- Keep a saved copy of the working theme before editing, so the store can be restored quickly. [L1131.061]
- Changing a store's domain name can break existing Google accounts (Workspace, Analytics, Merchant Center) that were set up on the old domain. If a domain change is needed, create new Google accounts for the new domain instead of reusing the old ones. [L0472.035] (2025)

**Procedure H: change a store domain (Source A, undated)**

1. Buy the new domain and link it in the Shopify admin.
2. Update your store emails to use the new domain.
3. Update the domain in Google Merchant Center.
4. Create a new product feed, since all URLs change.
5. Relink the new URL to Google Analytics, Merchant Center, Facebook and Bing. [L0941.035]

**Procedure I: rename a store domain without damaging ads (Source A, 2021, older)**

1. Turn off all Google Shopping ads.
2. Delete the product feeds from Google Merchant Center, Microsoft Ads and Facebook shop feeds.
3. Buy or link the new domain in Shopify so it loads the store.
4. Resubmit the products from the new domain and expect a few days of downtime while they are approved again. [L0968.032]

**Procedure J: switch to a new domain in the same Shopify store (Source C, undated)**

1. Buy a new domain in the same Shopify account.
2. In the TikTok channel app in Shopify, disconnect and reconnect the channel, and select the same pixel so the historical data is kept.
3. Set the new domain as primary in Shopify.
4. Add the new URL to the new campaigns, because campaigns change every two to three days. [L1436.070]

**Transfers**

- A domain bought through Shopify can be transferred to another registrar, and a domain bought elsewhere can be connected to Shopify. Every domain registrar allows transfers. [L1094.064] (2023)
- Transferring a domain to Shopify is a separate procedure from building the store and needs to be handled as its own step. [L0851.005] (2025)
- Shopify's Domains page offers Transfer domain alongside Connect existing domain and Buy new domain. Transfer domain moves a domain registered elsewhere into Shopify, while Buy new domain registers a new domain through Shopify. [L1292.034] (2021-22, older)
- Shopify's Connect existing domain links a domain without moving its registration. Transfer domain moves the registration itself. [L1292.034, L0520.016]

**Procedure K: move a domain bought through Shopify to a new store (Source A, 2025)**

1. Create the new Shopify account.
2. Follow Shopify's knowledge base documentation to transfer the domain from the old account to the new one.
3. Close the old account once the domain has moved.

Linking the domain through DNS settings is not enough; the domain itself must be transferred. [L0857.050]

### Subdomains and multiple stores

- Each store needs its own domain. A domain should not be bought for every niche before the first store proves itself. A new store should have its own URL rather than a subdomain of another store. [L0807.005] (2025)
- Use a store on its own domain rather than a subdomain such as store.yourdomain.com. Separate memorable domains bring more returning customers and are better for a second store. A domain costs about $14 per year. [L0665.030] (2022, older)
- A store can run on a subdomain, such as store.yourwebsite.com, linked from the header menu of the existing website. Alternatively, the existing website content can be moved to a new Shopify store, and the main URL can point to it. [L1068.044] (2019, older)
- Shopify can host a blog on a subdomain: install the store on a subdomain such as store.yourdomain.com while the main domain keeps the blog. The store then has full Shopify functionality and the rest of the site remains unchanged. [L0357.052] (2019, older)
- Build a separate.com store for each other market you sell into. [L0967.101] (2020, older)
- Each extra domain costs about $14 per year, and each one has its own fee. [L1283.092] (2023)

### Reusing a store for a new niche or domain

**Reuse decisions**

- If the domain and company name are generic, reuse the existing store for a new niche by changing the tagline and branding in the backend. Starting from scratch is not necessary. [L0469.038] (2021, older)
- An existing Shopify store can be reused for a new niche by editing the store and optionally buying a new domain (about $12 a year) and linking it. [L0834.042] (2021, older)
- To add a new domain to an existing Shopify store, link it in the Shopify admin panel. The 2021 source says this creates a new store on the same account while the old one continues to exist. [L0469.039] (2021, older; later sources describe the same store with a new domain, see the next point)
- Linking a new domain to the existing Shopify account, rather than building a new store, is the approach in later sources. [L1088.031] (2020, older), [L1305.037] (undated)
- A new domain can be bought in the same Shopify account, then set as the primary domain. The downside of keeping one store is that other products cannot be tested in that same store without mixing their results. [L1382.060]
- If one product is clearly working, keep it in the same store: buy a new domain, make it the primary domain, and keep the apps. The downside is that other products cannot be tested in the same store. [L1436.071]

**Moving a winning product to its own brand (Source C, undated)**

1. Buy a new domain that fits the winning product. A reported cost is about $10 per year.
2. Create a new logo for the product brand.
3. Create a new Facebook page for the product's ads.
4. Point the new domain to the Shopify store's base URL.
5. Switch the store and the ads to the new logo, domain and Facebook page. [L1428.025]

**Store name checks when reusing**

- The store name in the Shopify admin is not visible to customers, and the URL customers see can be changed as often as needed by changing the domain connected to the store. [L0359.043]
- If the.com is already taken for a reused store, the domain may change while the store stays the same. Keep the domain and store consistent. [L0964.089]
- Before buying a domain for a new niche, check whether the existing domain and company name are generic enough to reuse. [L0469.038]

#### Further details

- Adding a new domain to Shopify is simple: buy the domain through the Shopify backend, then select which domain is primary for your store from the settings. [L0249.028]

- Do not use.myshopify.com extensions for your store URL. Always use a custom domain name (e.g., chandelier.com, not yourstore.myshopify.com) for professional appearance. [L0253.034]

- Shopify domain path: in the Shopify admin, open Settings (bottom left), then Domains, where the Buy new domain option is shown. The default Shopify-assigned domain shown on a new account is not a professional domain. [L0520.006]

- Domain names can be bought from any registrar and linked to Shopify. Avoid expensive parked resale domains, and choose an open domain available for about $14 to $15 per year. [L0934.161]

- Domain names can be bought directly through Shopify and are linked to the store, so the domain does not have to be bought from a separate registrar. [L1304.003]

- Under Settings > Domains the Shopify domain and any purchased custom domain are listed. Both are visible there once the domain has been bought. [L1306.022]

## Business email, phone number and contact identity

### Which addresses and roles you need

- Set up three business addresses on your domain: sales@yourdomain for supplier outreach, support@yourdomain or returns@yourdomain for customer inquiries, and yourfirstname@yourdomain for personal business use. [L0119.002]
- Alternatively, set up sales@yourstore.com for customer inquiries, returns@yourstore.com for managing returns, and yourname@yourstore.com for personal business use. The two sources differ on the inquiry address: L0119.002 suggests support@ or returns@, and L0125.001 uses sales@. [L0125.001, L0119.002]
- Set up business addresses such as info, support and sales at your domain, and use them for customer service and supplier contact. [L0962.033] (2021, older)
- Use the same role address (info@ or sales@ on your domain) for Shopify, apps and social profiles so logins stay consistent. Do not use a no-reply address for newsletters, because replies from customers are valuable. [L0665.031] (2022, older)
- Use the store domain for customer-facing emails, such as orders@yourstore.com, because customers expect order confirmations from the store. Use the company domain for corporate communication, bank links and similar business accounts. [L1090.044] (2021, older)
- Enter a store email on the store's own domain (for example info@yourdomain) under Settings > General > Store contact details. Do not use a Gmail address as the store email, because a domain-based address looks more professional and is more trusted. [L1327.002]
- Use a separate business email address, not a personal one, across all Google platforms: Gmail, Google Analytics, Search Console, Google Ads and Merchant Center. This keeps data in one place and makes handing over the business easier later. [L1277.059] (2020, older)
- A new Shopify store defaults its store email and phone number to the ones used at sign-up. You can swap these for dedicated business email and phone later, once they exist. [L1306.003]

### Company name and store domain

- The owning entity can use a generic company name, such as an LLC or S corporation, that owns several stores in related niches. The entity name does not need to match a store domain, and changing an existing store domain is unnecessary. [L0807.006] (2025)
- An older rule (2021) says the store domain and company name should not match. Name the company something generic (e.g. LJ Stores LLC) so that additional stores can be opened under it without confusion. [L0834.040] (2021, older)
- Enter your business information rather than your personal name in the Shopify store information and settings. Your personal name can stay as well, but the business information should be the primary entry. [L0835.071] (2023)

### Business address

- Do not put a home address on the website. The options are no address, or a UPS Store address, which costs about $200 to $250 per year and works as a business address. [L1068.042] (2019, older)
- A UPS Store address shows a suite number instead of a PO box, which looks more professional in an email signature and on a website. A post office PO box is a cheaper alternative but looks less professional. [L1068.043] (2019, older)
- Use a professional business address (UPS Store with suite number) rather than a USPS PO Box on business documents and supplier agreements. It conveys greater professionalism and legitimacy. [L0251.014] (2023)
- Use a business address such as a UPS Store mailbox instead of your home address on the website. [L0852.049] (2020, older)
