- Use a commercial address such as a UPS Store address rather than a home address or a PO box. This is the practice used across the owner's businesses, though the reason for the policy is not confirmed. [L1111.068] (2020, older)
- Enter the business address in the Shopify store settings alongside your other business details. [L0696.011]
- Adding a physical business address to the contact page is optional, but it adds a level of trust for shoppers who want to send mail. [L0696.012]
- The business address on the Contact Us page is another contact method. It is useful for Google and not required, but it must be a business address, not your home address. [L0835.070] (2023)
- Some competitor stores list a private home address on their website, which exposes the owner's personal information. Do not copy that practice. If you list an address at all, use a business address on the contact page. [L0696.017]

### Personal details and second phone line

- Entering your personal phone number in a Shopify store or SMS sign-up can lead customers to call you directly. A second, dedicated number is safer for store contact fields. [L0820.047]

### Google Workspace sign-up

1. Go to the Google Workspace sign-up page. [L0125.003]
2. Scroll down to locate the Business Starter Plan ($7/month). [L0125.003]
3. Click 'Get Started' under the $7/month option. [L0125.003]
4. Complete account setup. [L0125.003]
- Google Workspace can be set up through a meta tag or through DNS records to verify the domain. The meta tag method is the first choice. [L1094.067] (2023)
- Enter the domain without https:// and without www, only the name and extension such as yourbrand.com. If the domain was bought through Shopify, keep Shopify as domain host so the DNS can be edited there. For a domain bought elsewhere, choose that registrar from the list and edit its DNS there instead. [L1196.011]
- Domain verification may not be required if Shopify automatically recognises the domain. [L0125.004]

### Connecting Google Workspace to a Shopify domain

The procedure below combines three versions of the same connection. Each step keeps the details of all sources that describe it.

1. On the Google Workspace domain host screen, keep Shopify as the domain host if the domain was bought through Shopify; otherwise select the actual registrar from the list. Click Continue. [L1197.009, L1196.011]
2. In Google Workspace, hover over the clipboard icon next to the Text value field and click it to copy the unique verification code. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
3. In Shopify Admin, click the Settings icon in the bottom left corner, then click Domains. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
4. Click the primary domain of the online store. It must be the same domain added to Google Workspace. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
5. Scroll down past Email forwarding, which should not be used, to the section Third-party email hosting, and click Switch to email hosting. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
6. Leave the email hosting provider as G Suite. This is the old name for Google Workspace, which Shopify still shows. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
7. Paste the copied code into the Text value field and click Save. The provider now shows G Suite. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
8. At the top of the page, click Domain settings, then click Edit DNS settings. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
9. Click Add custom record and select MX record. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
10. Set Name to @ (the at symbol), set Points to to smtp.google.com, and set Priority to 1. Click Confirm. [L1197.009, L1196.009, L0119.007]
11. Scroll down and check that the MX record list shows smtp.google.com with priority 1. [L1197.009, L1196.009]
12. Back in Google Workspace, tick the box to confirm the code was updated on the domain host, click Confirm, and leave the page open while DNS verification runs. Verification can complete almost instantly. [L1197.009, L1196.009]
13. When verification finishes, Gmail is ready for the domain. Confirm it under Confirm verification in Google Workspace and wait for confirmation. [L0119.007, L1197.009]

- The MX record for Google Workspace must be Type MX, Name @, Points to smtp.google.com, Priority 1. Without this record, incoming email is not routed to Gmail. [L1197.010]
- Short version (2022, older): open the DNS settings of the domain, add the MX records that Google Workspace provides for the domain, and wait for the change to take effect before expecting email to work. [L1281.062] (2022, older)

### Mistake to avoid: forwarding instead of email hosting

- Choosing Email forwarding instead of Third-party email hosting in Shopify does not connect Google Workspace. Use the Switch to email hosting option for the Google connection. [L1197.011, L1196.010]
- The "Create a forwarding email" option under Email forwarding only forwards to an existing inbox. Use the Third-party email hosting section so Google receives and sends mail for the domain, and paste the verification code into its Text value field. [L1196.010]
- Shopify's free forwarding can still serve as a receiving address. A contact address on the store's domain can be forwarded to your private inbox, so customers see a branded address while you read mail in your usual account. [L0520.004]
- Example: a contact address such as contact@yourstorename.com can be forwarded to a private inbox with Shopify's free forwarding. [L0520.019]

### Source D procedure: test the domain email

The Source D procedure uses the menu Settings > Domains > Email forwarding and hosting.

1. Choose Third-party email hosting. [L1327.046]
2. Connect a Google Workspace account. [L1327.046]
3. Follow the Google Workspace steps to add the DNS records to the domain. [L1327.046]
4. Check that the domain's email works by sending a test email from it. Shopify's domain settings page shows the DNS records and nameservers used for this. [L1327.046]

### Authentication: DMARC, DKIM and TXT records

- Shopify requires DMARC authentication so that outgoing emails do not go to spam. You must authenticate the domain and add a DMARC record. [L0255.036] (2025)
- To add DMARC on Namecheap:
  1. Go to Namecheap DNS records. [L0255.037]
  2. Get the DMARC code from Shopify: log into your account and go to settings. [L0255.037]
  3. If the code is not visible, contact Shopify support via live chat for the DNS record code. [L0255.037]
  4. Add the code as a DNS record in Namecheap. [L0255.037]
  5. Shopify will verify, and emails will no longer show the Shopify domain in the 'from' line. [L0255.037]
- When transferring a Google Domain to Shopify, configure the TXT records properly, including a DKIM record in the DNS settings. Without this, order confirmation emails fail to send to customers even though they are triggered in Shopify. [L0298.052]

### Switching the store to business email

- After the professional addresses are working, replace older personal email addresses in the Shopify store settings and anywhere else they appear with the new business addresses. This keeps store notifications and customer contact consistent with the domain. Do this once the Google Workspace setup is complete. [L1196.017, L1197.022]
- Set up the mailbox before the store goes live. A support email in the footer only works once a real mailbox for that address exists. [L1018.011]
- Shopify contact form messages are delivered by email to the address set in the store's Shopify account. Check that address in the Shopify settings to make sure inquiries reach you. [L0470.056] (2023)

### Replacing placeholders in policies and admin

1. In Shopify Admin, replace all placeholder emails with your professional business email addresses. [L0119.011]
2. Update your store footer to display your professional email. [L0119.011]
3. Update the privacy policy, terms of service and returns policy pages with your business email. [L0119.011]
4. Update your contact page with your professional email address. [L0119.011]
- Terms of service and demo store placeholder details (such as phone numbers) are not required before supplier contact. Placeholder data such as '555' numbers looks unprofessional and should be replaced with a real phone number or an email address. [L0971.035] (2024)
- Use the same store and business contact details everywhere: the Shopify account, Google Merchant Center and the website's contact page. Mismatched details can cause account reviews or suspensions. [L1111.076] (2020, older)

### Getting a business phone number

- Secure a business phone number before building the website, so the number can be placed on the site from the start. Setting it up early keeps the contact details consistent across the store and supplier communication. [L1198.012] (2021-22, older)
- Add the business phone number to the store only after the phone app signup is finished, because the number is issued at the end of that signup. Before that, there is no number to enter in the Shopify header, footer or contact page. [L0700.015]
- The phone number can come from Google Voice or OpenPhone. [L0700.009]
- Use a dedicated business phone number, such as a toll-free 800 number in the US or a 1300 number in Australia. [L1371.040] (2021-22, older)

### Voicemail

1. Set up a phone number on the store. [L1111.069] (2020, older)
2. Route calls to voicemail after two rings. [L1111.069] (2020, older)
3. Record a voicemail greeting that asks for name, phone number, email address and the reason for the call. [L1111.069] (2020, older)
4. Review the voicemails to learn what customers are asking. [L1111.069] (2020, older)

### Adding the phone number to the header

Locations: Online Store > Edit theme > Header, in the field phone number / email address. [L0122.010, L0338.010]

1. In the Shopify admin, click Online Store. Click Edit theme (in the theme customizer, click Customize). [L0338.009, L0700.009]
2. Click Header. In the left navigation, or click the header in the preview, then zoom out so the settings panel on the right is visible. [L0338.009, L0700.009]
3. In the field labelled phone number / email address, which initially holds an email address, delete the email address and type the business phone number. The preview updates in real time. [L0338.009, L0700.009]
4. Click Save. [L0338.009, L0700.009]
- Replace the email address in the header with your business phone number. [L0122.010]
- The header contact details (contact text, hours of operation, phone and email) are set under Header > Contact details. Set the hours to the times you can actually answer customer service, and leave the phone field blank until you have a number. [L0775.026]
- Promo header: set the small promo header element to a heading "Call us today", enter the business phone number, link it to the contact page if wanted, and set the icon to a black cell phone icon. [L1371.040] (2021-22, older)

### Adding the phone number to the footer

Locations: Online Store > Edit theme > Footer menu > Contact us section. The phone field sits directly above the email address. Save after changes. [L0338.010, L0122.011]

1. Scroll down in the theme editor to the footer menu. [L0338.009, L0700.010]
2. Open the Contact us section. It may show an email address and a filler phone number. [L0700.010, L0700.016]
3. Click the section so the WYSIWYG editor opens on the right. [L0700.010]
4. Type the business phone number directly above the email address, so the footer shows two contact methods. [L0338.009, L0700.010, L0122.011]
5. Click Save. [L0338.009, L0700.010]
- Replace the filler phone number with the real business number rather than leaving filler text visible to customers. [L0700.016]

### Adding the phone number to the contact page

1. In the Shopify backend, go to Online Store and then Pages. [L0700.011]
2. Open the Contact us page. [L0700.011]
3. In the WYSIWYG editor, add a section that reads "call us at", followed by the business phone number. [L0700.011]
4. Click Save. [L0700.011]

### Contact methods on the site

- A contact page should offer at least two ways to reach the business, such as a business email and a contact form. A phone number or a virtual address can be added as extra methods. [L0970.056] (2023)
- Include multiple ways to contact the business: a contact page with email, phone and contact form, plus email and phone in the footer. A single email address is not enough. [L1111.065] (2020, older)
- Show a phone number in the homepage contact area when one is available. An email address alone is acceptable, but a phone number adds more trust. [L0971.087] (2024)
- If no dedicated contact exists for a supplier form, use 'support' or a store email address in the contact fields. [L0964.090] (2023)

## Core admin settings: general, checkout, payments and orders

### Store identity and account settings

#### Opening Settings and the section map
- Open the admin's main settings page with the gear icon labeled Settings in the bottom-left corner. The sections are Store details, Plan, Billing, Users, Payments, Checkout, Customer accounts, Shipping and delivery, Locations, Apps, Sales channels, Domains, Notifications and Policies. [L0096.001]
- Settings open with the gear icon at the bottom left of the admin, labeled Settings. [L1306.001]
- Shopify's Settings sections are reached from the gear icon. Payments are opened with Settings > Payments. [L1285.027, L1305.043]

#### Store details
- Under Settings > Store details, the billing address is tied to the card on file. [L0096.002]
- The pencil icon edits three fields: store name (internal only, not shown to customers), store email and store phone number. [L0096.002]
- The default store name is "my store". Replace it with the real business name. [L1306.002]
- To change the store name, click Edit, change the name and then click Save. The store URL does not change. [L1305.001]
- Keep the store email and store phone number as entered at account creation until a business email address and business phone number exist. Update them after the business accounts are created. [L0096.003, L1305.001]
- The billing address shows the business address entered at account creation. Leave it as is at first and update it later with Edit once a business address is available. [L1305.002]
- The general store settings, including Store details, are preset. Other default options do not need changes at launch. [L1306.005]
- Older advice (Source B, 2021-22): fill in store name, store contact email, sender email, store address, legal name of business and phone number. The store address appears on invoices and is used for shipping rate calculation. Shopify shows a warning if tax information is missing. [L0677.004]
- Older advice (Source B, 2021-22): in Settings > General, also fill in Store name, Store contact email (where Shopify contacts you), Sender email, Legal name of business, Phone and Store address, and set the industry field. [L1145.019]
- The two approaches conflict on the store email, phone and sender email. Use the Source A approach (keep them until business contacts exist) for a new store, and the Source B approach (fill them in during setup) only where the business contacts already exist. [L0096.003, L1305.001, L0677.004, L1145.019]

#### Plan, billing and users
- Settings > Plan shows the current Shopify plan. The recommended plan at the start is Basic Shopify. Upgrades and downgrades are handled here, and paid apps and subscriptions also appear in this section. [L0096.004]
- Settings > Billing lists Shopify charges and billing history. No changes are needed there at the start. [L0096.005]
- Settings > Users controls who can access the store. The default user is the account owner. Add staff users later when help is hired. No action is needed at the start. [L0096.006]
- At the foundation stage, check the trial and billing dates. [L1305.043]

### Store defaults: time zone, units, currency and order numbers

#### What Settings > General contains
- Settings > General contains Backup Region, Unit system, Default weight unit, Time zone, Order ID format and the store currency. [L0351.054]
- Currency display is not set in General. Customer-facing currencies are managed under Markets. [L1167.005, L0351.054]
- Keep only the primary market active under Settings > Markets. [L1305.043]

#### Time zone
- The time zone controls when orders and analytics are recorded. Set it first. [L0351.054, L1327.007]
- Examples: GMT+08:00 Singapore for a Singapore-based business [L0351.054], and (GMT+08:00) Singapore for the same case [L1327.007].
- Older example (Source B, 2021-22): an Australian store uses (GMT+10:00) Brisbane. [L0677.002]
- The time zone defaults to the sign-up location. Using your own time zone is recommended because it makes analytics tracking easier. [L1305.004]

#### Unit system and default weight unit

| Target market | Unit system | Default weight unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| United States | Imperial | Pound (lb) | [L1327.006, L1305.005] |
| Australia (older example) | Metric | Kilogram (kg) | [L0677.002] |
| Metric-based store (Source D 2026) | Metric | Kilogram (kg) | [L1167.005] |

- Set the unit system to Imperial and the default weight unit to Pound (lb) when the store targets the US, since the US uses the imperial system. [L1327.006]
- Keep the unit system on Imperial and the default weight unit on pound for a US store. [L1305.005]
- Store defaults are set under Settings > General: unit system Metric and default weight unit Kilogram (kg). [L1167.005]
- Conflict: one Source D 2026 lesson lists the default weight unit as Pound (lb) [L0351.054], while another sets Metric and Kilogram (kg) [L1167.005]. Choose the pair that matches where the store sells: Imperial and lb for the US [L1327.006, L1305.005], Metric and kg for metric markets [L0677.002, L1167.005].

#### Order ID format
- Shopify starts order numbers at #1001 by default. The number itself cannot be changed. A prefix or suffix can be added under Settings > General > Edit order ID format, for example EN1001 or 1001-A. [L0677.001] (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.)
- Use the pound symbol (#) as the prefix for order numbers and no suffix. [L1305.006]
- Set the Order ID format so the first order number is high, starting from around 200 onwards (for example #200 or #2001 style numbers) instead of the default #1001. A low starting number makes a store look new to buyers. [L1327.008]
- Conflict: the 2021-22 source says the starting number cannot be changed [L0677.001], while the Source D 2026 lesson sets a higher starting number [L1327.008]. Verify in the admin whether the starting number is editable on the plan in use, and use the prefix option [L0677.001, L1305.006] if it is not.

### Checkout

#### Contact method and required fields
- Set the customer contact method to Email. [L0096.010, L1306.014, L1305.017, L1327.020]
- Show the link to download the Shop app, a customer app from Shopify for tracking orders and reordering. [L1305.017]
- Keep the order tracking link enabled. [L0096.010, L1306.014]
- Customer information fields:
  - Require last name only: left on. [L0096.010]
  - Require first and last name: left on, with last name required and first name also required. [L1305.019]
  - Company name: disabled. Include it only for B2B. [L0096.010, L1305.019]
  - Address line two: optional. [L1305.019]
  - Shipping address phone number: don't include. The phone number entered as the main order phone is used by carriers. [L1305.019]
- The two sources differ on name fields: L0096.010 turns on only "Require last name", while L1305.019 lists both options as left on. Use the option you choose consistently, and check the field in the admin. [L0096.010, L1305.019]
- Verify all required fields are configured under Shopify > Settings > Checkout > Customer contact. [L0274.040] (2023.)

#### Sign-in and customer accounts
- Keep Require customer to log into their account before checkout OFF, so customers can check out as guests. A login requirement adds friction. [L1305.018]
- No required sign-in before checkout (Source D 2026 default). [L1327.020]
- Customer accounts: keep customer accounts enabled and sign-in links enabled. No customization is required at this stage. [L0096.011]
- Keep customer account creation hidden, so there are no accounts before purchase. Leave the login experience and self-serve returns off. [L1305.025]
- Enable Shop Pay under Sign in with shop only after Shopify Payments is active. [L1305.025, L1305.026]
- Source D 2026: keep the default customer account configuration. Self-serve returns and cancellations can be configured later with conditions and fees. The sign-in links and store credit options stay at their defaults. [L1327.021]

#### Marketing, tipping, billing address and order notes
- Marketing emails: turn on the email marketing opt-in and keep it pre-selected. SMS opt-in can also be offered. [L1305.020]
- Default marketing settings are left enabled, and SMS marketing is not enabled by default. [L0096.010]
- Source D 2026 keeps no marketing opt-in checkbox at the foundation stage. [L1327.020]
- Conflict: Source A turns the email opt-in on [L1305.020], while the Source D 2026 foundation keeps no opt-in checkbox [L1327.020]. Choose the setting that matches your consent and email plan.
- Tipping: The sources give three positions.

| Position | Source | When it applies |
|---|---|---|
| Keep off | [L1305.021] | Standard Source A setup |
| No tipping options | [L1327.020] | Source D 2026 foundation |
| Enable the default tip setting (for example a 5, 2 or 3 pound tip) | [L0773.043] | Source C notes many store owners remove these settings |

- Address collection: keep Use the shipping address as the billing address by default on, and keep Use address completion on. [L1305.022]
- Keep the order notes field enabled in the Shopify cart before checkout. Few customers use it, but it is worth having. [L0470.024]
- Leave the order notes field on at first, then remove it if customers never use it. [L0855.107]
- Checkout language should default to the store's business language. Change it only if needed. No checkout rules are added. [L1305.024]
- Customized checkout (logo, favicon and checkout appearance) can be left for later. [L1305.016]
- Checkout trust and security:
  - Shopify's default checkout trust elements are enough for security display, so no extra security app is needed at checkout. [L1110.026] (Older advice, Source A, 2019.)

#### Order processing (dropshipping)
- Order processing options after payment (Source D 2026):
  - Automatically fulfill the order's line items.
  - Automatically fulfill only the gift cards of the order.
  - Don't fulfill any of the order's line items automatically.
  - Automatically archive order after fulfillment or payment can also be set. [L1327.009]
- The Source D 2026 lesson left these at the default for the foundation setup. [L1327.009]
- For dropshipping, choose option 3 (do not automatically fulfill any line items). The order is then marked as fulfilled manually once the supplier provides tracking. [L1305.023]
- Keep the automatic archiving of fulfilled orders on. Archived orders are not deleted; they are only removed from the open list. [L1305.023]
- Mistake: automatic fulfillment after payment marks orders as fulfilled before a supplier has shipped or provided tracking. Option 3 avoids this. [L1305.047]
- Conflict: Source A recommends option 3 for dropshipping [L1305.023, L1305.047], while the Source D 2026 foundation keeps the default [L1327.009]. For a dropshipping store, option 3 avoids marking orders fulfilled too early.

#### Checkout apps, custom code and third-party buttons
- Checkout customization apps (countdown timers, custom images) were removed from the Shopify app store around 2020. Checkout customization is reserved for Shopify Plus, which starts at about $2,000 per month (as of 2020). Use the native cart and theme settings for standard stores. [L0664.034] (Older advice, Source A, 2020.)
- Avoid adding custom code to the Shopify checkout page, because it can cause issues. Extra payment buttons such as Klarna, Affirm or Amazon Pay appear prominently on their own and are a better way to add choices. [L0962.038] (Older advice, Source A, 2021.)
- Blocks or links for Amazon checkout are not a trust signal. Keep your store's checkout on your own platform and payment methods. Avoid links that send visitors to the marketplace. [L0551.070] (Older advice, Source A, 2020.)
- Use the discount code and discount row settings at their defaults. The footer and the shipping protection row are optional extras during testing. [L0560.026]
- The Shopify checkout allows little customization. Most conversion changes must happen earlier in the journey: on the product page, in the offer and in the shipping and trust messaging. Plan the store around these pre-checkout elements. [L0640.053]

#### Checkout friction and language
- Minimize checkout friction by accepting common payment methods and reducing steps to purchase. Every additional step increases cart abandonment. [L0233.018]
- Use words that signal security and trust such as "free" and "tracked". Check the checkout and cart for hidden prices, mismatched currencies or a missing add-to-cart button. Shipping charged by mistake is a common cause of abandonment. [L0640.039]
- PayPal one-click checkout: Integrate PayPal's payment button so customers can complete a purchase immediately after adding a product to the cart with one click. This removes the multi-step checkout, reduces effort and increases the completion rate. [L0233.014]
- One-click checkout app: displays checkout immediately after the customer adds a product to the cart, bypassing the cart review page. [L0233.016]

#### Price display
- Settings > Pricing > Price rounding is off by default. Enable it and choose the ending 95 or 99 so bundle prices end in.95 or.99. [L0861.007]
- Other pricing toggles in the same section are Show prices per item, Use product compare-at price and Show prices without decimals. [L0861.007]

### Payments

#### Choosing a processor
- Shopify Payments is a built-in payment processor with the most straightforward integration. Implement it when your region is supported. [L0270.019] (Geographic list is older, Source A, 2021: Australia, Austria, Belgium, Canada, Denmark, Germany, Hong Kong, Ireland, Italy, Japan, Netherlands, New Zealand, Singapore, Spain, Sweden, UK and US.)
- Shopify integrates with most payment processors. A store can be built with a gateway other than Shopify Payments. Shopify Payments is recommended because it is the easiest option. [L0967.052] (Older advice, Source A, 2020.)
- Use a checkout-related Shopify payment processor (Shopify Payments or Stripe). Check the main payment processor of the target country before launch. [L1259.046]
- Stripe can be used instead where Shopify Payments is not available. [L0678.036]
- Other payment providers can be used if Shopify integrates with them. The Payments page links to the list of all providers via See all other providers. The recommended setup is Shopify Payments plus PayPal. [L1305.015]
- Accept at least one processor for credit cards and PayPal. PayPal performs particularly well in the USA and the top four markets. [L1259.045]
- The lesson reports that adding PayPal to a single-processor checkout roughly doubled conversion in one case. [L1259.045]
- Crypto acceptance (Older advice, Source A, 2021): it can be added through Coinbase Commerce, BitPay or CoinPayments.net in a few minutes, but it historically did not generate sales for the business. [L0834.045]

#### Activating Shopify Payments
- The only required action at the initial setup stage is activating Shopify Payments. The goal is to learn where settings are located rather than to configure heavily. [L0096.020]
- Settings > Payments: click Activate Shopify payments and enter bank account details for payouts. Customers can then pay by card, debit, Apple Pay and Google Pay. The setup takes a few minutes. [L1305.010, L1111.058]
- Accepted methods: credit cards, debit cards, Apple Pay and Google Pay. [L1062.006]
- Shopify Payments deposits funds within 2 business days of order completion (Older advice, Source A, 2022). No upfront money is needed to submit orders to suppliers in the first month. [L0249.021]
- Business or personal account (Older advice, Source A, 2019): either a personal checking account or a business checking account works to accept payments. Business checking accounts are easy to open (phone or online, typically 20 minutes) and are recommended for long-term operations so that account numbers do not change later. [L0243.039]
- Shopify Payments shows as Shop Pay, which is the first option listed under Settings > Payments. [L1285.027]
- Enabled payment methods must match those declared in Google Merchant Center. Enable or disable Apple Pay, Google Pay and other options under Settings > Checkout > Payment methods. [L0255.024]
- Apple Pay shows at checkout when Shopify Payments or Stripe is active. Some themes can hide it. After switching themes, place a real test payment to confirm the button appears. [L0773.057]
- Payment methods shown in the footer: tick the checkboxes under payment methods for every method you accept. Each ticked method then appears automatically in the store footer. [L1050.008, L0377.020]
- Test mode: the payment section of Shopify has an Enable test mode option that checks whether transactions go through. One seller skipped it in favour of a dummy order with an immediate refund. [L1381.048]
- Payments are set under Settings > Payments. [L1090.041]

#### PayPal
- Settings > Payments > PayPal: click Complete account setup to link PayPal to the store. Customers can then choose PayPal at checkout alongside Shopify Payments. [L1305.011]
- PayPal must be on a business account before it links to Shopify. If PayPal is not connected after opening the business account, retry the connection in Shopify payment settings. [L0834.046] (Older advice, Source A, 2021.)
- Shopify has support documentation on integrating PayPal as a payment method. It was shared in the September 2025 coaching call. [L1286.008]

#### Payment capture method
- Capture options (Older advice, Source A, 2020): automatic captures and charges immediately. Manual authorizes the card but holds the charge until you confirm stock. [L1111.059]
- Set the payment capture method from automatically at checkout to manually (Manage > manually). Orders then only authorize funds and are not charged until you capture them. This prevents charges on test or demo orders. [L1305.012]
- Mistake: leaving payment capture on automatic lets any test or demo purchase charge the buyer's card immediately. Set it to manual before the store is public. [L1305.013]
- With manual capture, each new order triggers a notification email to the account email. The payment must be captured manually in Shopify to charge the card. [L1305.014]
- Authorization windows (conflict between sources):
  - Shopify payment authorization defaults to seven days and cannot be extended on Shopify Payments. If a product will arrive after that window, plan a deposit or payment-capture workflow in advance. [L1090.039] (Older advice, Source A, 2021.)
  - Shopify payment capture windows vary by card, so a manual capture must be completed within the authorization window. Check the timeframe before relying on a long hold. [L1111.060] (Older advice, Source A, 2020.)
  - Use seven days as the planning figure for Shopify Payments, and check the card-specific window before relying on any longer hold. [L1090.039, L1111.060]

#### Authorization-only workflows
- Set up payment authorizations (not capture) initially using the payment authorization setting. This holds funds on the customer's card without charging them. You can confirm stock with the supplier and then decide whether to capture or release the payment. [L0249.022] (Older advice, Source A, 2022.)
- Procedure: Capturing payments only after stock is confirmed (Older advice, Source A, 2020):
  1. In the Shopify payment settings, turn on the setting that authorizes the card without capturing the payment.
  2. When a customer orders, the card is authorized, which confirms that the funds are available.
  3. Confirm with the supplier that the product is in stock.
  4. Click the capture payment button on the order to charge the customer.
  5. Process the order with the supplier once the payment is captured. [L0852.037]
- Deposit workflow for an out-of-stock item whose authorization will expire (Older advice, Source A, 2021):
  - Step 1: ask the customer if they will pay a 50% deposit to hold the order.
  - Step 2: charge that half to the card.
  - Step 3: submit the order to the supplier.
  - Step 4: when the product arrives, charge the remaining balance and send the invoice before shipping. [L1090.040]

#### Manual payment methods, draft orders and invoices
- Shopify draft orders can be used to create a customer invoice before the order is paid. This lets a store owner send a payable order to a customer manually. [L0851.004]
- Continue using Shopify draft orders to improve customer service and checkout completion, despite their conversion tracking limitation. They make operations easier and are an effective way for customers to finalize orders, especially in high-ticket businesses. [L0256.016] (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.)
- Procedure: To accept ACH or wire transfer payments (Source A, 2025):
  1. Create draft order in Shopify for customer.
  2. In draft order notes section, include routing number and checking account number for wire/ACH transfer.
  3. Send draft order to customer.
  4. Customer sends ACH or wire transfer to your bank.
  5. Once funds received, manually mark order as paid in Shopify.
  6. Note: ACH and wire transfers have no fraud protection like credit cards. [L0255.033]
- Procedure: For split payment orders (Source A, 2025):
  1. Create first draft order for half of total amount.
  2. Create second draft order for remaining balance.
  3. Send both draft orders to customer.
  4. Specify payment method for each (e.g., draft 1 on card, draft 2 via wire).
  5. Customer pays both.
  6. Mark orders as paid when payments received. [L0255.034]
- Phone order process (Older advice, Source A, 2020):
  1. Customer calls and tells you product they want.
  2. You go to website, pull up product page.
  3. Add to cart through your store.
  4. Walk through checkout with customer on phone.
  5. Collect name and payment details as customer reads them to you. [L0268.022]
- Partial refund for return shipping: when a return is accepted and the customer is charged a return shipping fee, issue a partial refund in Shopify to deduct the fee. [L1094.063]

#### Shop Pay Installments
- Shop Pay Installments is a Shopify Payments feature that lets customers split purchases into 2 or 4 interest-free installments, with the payment option selected at checkout. [L0153.001]
- On product pages, messaging such as "Pay in 4 interest-free payments" displays below the product price, along with the calculated installment amount. Both adjust automatically based on the product price. [L0153.002]
- The toggle applies to the whole store, not to individual products. Once switched on, installment messages can appear on every eligible product page. [L1296.012]
- All payment method changes save automatically in Shopify Payments. No additional configuration is required after enabling Shop Pay Installments. [L0153.006]
- The option turns on in roughly 30 seconds of clicking. It can take a few minutes to appear on storefront pages after the toggle is switched on. [L1295.017]
- Once active, the financing message appears across the store's pages, including product pages, the cart and checkout. No separate theme editing is needed. [L1295.016]
- Activation path: Settings > Payments, click Manage under Shopify Payments, then Manage payment methods, locate Shop Pay Installments and toggle it ON. [L0153.004]
- Procedure: Enable Shop Pay Installments in the Shopify admin (Source A):
  1. Log in to the Shopify admin.
  2. Click the settings gear icon in the bottom left of the admin navigation.
  3. Click Payments.
  4. Under Shopify Payments, click Manage.
  5. Click Manage payment methods.
  6. Find Shop Pay Installments in the list. If it is off, click the slider and drag it to the right to turn it on.
  7. If a save prompt appears, save the settings.
  8. Wait a few minutes, then open any product page on the store and refresh it.
  9. Confirm that the financing message appears on the product page, and check that it also shows in the cart and at checkout. [L1295.014]
- Troubleshooting: if the financing message does not show right after enabling, the most likely cause is that the page has not refreshed yet. Give the change a few minutes and refresh the product page before assuming the setup failed. [L1295.015]
- Testing Shop Pay Installments against Affirm (Source A, 2023):
  1. Keep the Affirm account open.
  2. Turn off Affirm on the site for two weeks.
  3. Turn on Shop Pay Installments for the same period.
  4. Compare conversion results at the end of the two weeks and keep the option that performs better. [L0970.073]
- Shop Pay as installment financing (Older advice, Source A, 2022): Shop Pay is sufficient for installment options, and no separate Klarna setup is required. [L0969.088]
- Klarna or Afterpay can be added when the product price is above 60 USD. [L1259.045]
- Conflict: L0969.088 (2022) says no separate Klarna setup is needed, while L1259.045 adds Klarna or Afterpay above 60 USD. Use Shop Pay Installments as the base, and add Klarna or Afterpay for products above 60 USD. [L0969.088, L1259.045]

#### Settings order for payments
- Payments must be configured before ads. Once payments are configured and saved, the checkout is live and visitors can place real orders. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0677.022]
- Complete the Shopify settings in this order before running any ads: General, Payments, Shipping and delivery, Taxes, then Legal. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0677.052]

### Shipping, taxes and duties (boundary of this section)
- Do not adjust taxes or duties in the shipping settings yet. Tax configuration is done in a later stage of the setup. [L0096.013]
- Shipping procedure (Source A, complete setup order, step 8): delete default rates, create Custom flat rate Free shipping at $0, delete the International zone. [L1305.043]
- Taxes and duties: no changes at this stage. [L1305.043]

### Orders, fulfilment and notifications

#### Order notification emails
- Settings > Orders: configure which email addresses receive order notifications and which address emails should be sent from. The customer can choose to receive notifications at any configured email. [L0270.039] (Older advice, Source A, 2021.)
- Set your notification email in Settings > Orders. The Shopify mobile app can play a sound with every sale. [L0834.037] (Older advice, Source A, 2021.)
- Customer order notes field at checkout: leave it on at first, then remove it if customers never use it. [L0855.107]
- Use the Shopify order number and the order's information for customer purchase receipts. If a customer does not receive the automatic Shopify order email, resend it from the order page or send a screenshot of the order. [L0964.097]

#### Order details
- Clicking into an order in Shopify Admin shows the products bought, any notes the customer added at checkout, the customer's name, email and address, and the customer's full purchase history with the store. [L1302.005]

#### Fulfilment and tracking
- Shopify Admin > Fulfillment screen > Tracking Information section: select the shipping service and enter the tracking number before marking the order as fulfilled. [L1291.006]
- Mistake: marking an order as fulfilled sends the shipping notice to the customer straight away. Do not mark it fulfilled before the shipping service and tracking number are entered. [L1291.007]
- Procedure: send tracking information to a customer when the supplier ships (Older advice, Source A, 2022):
  1. Get the tracking number from the supplier.
  2. Open the customer's order in the Shopify admin.
  3. Attach the tracking number to the order so the customer receives updates. [L0803.084]
- Procedure: orders split across multiple suppliers (Older advice, Source A, 2021):
  1. Receive order.
  2. Mark as partially fulfilled in Shopify.
  3. Add tracking number for first shipment, send first email.
  4. Mark remaining items as fulfilled when second shipment ships.
  5. Customer receives separate tracking email for each shipment. [L0270.023]
- Fulfilment request notification: turn on the option that notifies your fulfilment service provider when an order is marked as fulfilled. Use it when an outside 3PL or fulfilment partner needs the order update automatically instead of by hand. [L1327.062]
- Install a fulfilment app via Apps > Sales channel settings > Shopify App Store. Search for the app name, then click Install. Once installed, the app shows Open app. [L0685.006]

#### Draft orders and receipts (cross-reference)
- Use the draft order and receipt rules above for manual orders. [L0851.004, L0964.097]

### Locations

#### Default location and plan limits
- Settings > Locations: the default single location is the address entered at sign-up. It is sufficient at launch. Extra locations are only needed for inventory tracking across several warehouses or suppliers. [L1305.032]
- Shopify's Locations page shows how many active locations the plan allows, for example "Using 1 of 10 active locations available on your plan". A Point of Sale subscription is included with the plan, so in-person selling can start from any active location. [L1250.041]
- Up to 1000 locations are allowed on the Shopify Basic plan. [L1305.032]
- Conflict: the Locations page example shows a limit of 10 [L1250.041], while L1305.032 gives up to 1000 on Basic. Read the number shown on the Locations page for the plan in use. [L1250.041, L1305.032]
- Keep the default location at the foundation stage. A location only needs to be changed when stock is held or sold from a different address or when in-person selling is added. [L1327.029]

#### Dropshipping rules
- Warehouse locations in Shopify should reflect the primary fulfilment location, typically the home office for dropshipping. Setting one per supplier is unnecessary, because the supplier location determines the actual shipping, not the Shopify warehouse setting. [L0274.059] (Source A, 2023.)
- Set the Shopify default location to the country from which you actually ship. If all orders ship from the US, set the Shopify location to USA so it matches the shipping origin in Google Merchant Center. [L0835.069] (Source A, 2023.)
- Point of sale is not used for a dropshipping store, since it requires a physical card reader. [L1305.033]

### Customer notifications and sender email
- Settings > Notifications contains the sender email settings. Do not change them yet. They are updated once the business email setup is finished. [L0096.018]
- The sender email address can be changed later once business email addresses (for example @yourstore.com) exist. Add the logo and brand colours to email templates. The default templates (for example order confirmation) can be edited, but editing is not required. [L1305.039]
- The sender email in Settings > General is the address customers see when the store emails them. It may not appear in the same way in every customer's inbox. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0677.045]
- Revisit brand, notifications and business contact details once the logo, brand colours, business phone, business email and business address are in place. [L1305.044]
- Policies are completed during an earlier step of the setup, so no action is needed in Settings > Policies. [L0096.019]
- Source D 2026 places Policies in the foundation order before any sales channel is opened. [L1327.001]

### Gift cards
- Set an expiration date for gift cards rather than never expiring, so pending gift cards do not become old liabilities when the business is sold. Apple Wallet passes are optional and not a priority. [L1305.034]
- Shopify has a built-in gift card feature. Customers choose the amount they want to pay, and the code is sent by email to be used by the buyer or given to someone else. [L1439.036]

### Apps, sales channels and domains

#### Apps and sales channels: what they are
- The Apps item is at the bottom of the left navigation menu. It lists every app already installed and shows recommendations for further apps. [L1301.001]
- Shopify Settings has a combined page for apps and sales channels, reached from the Apps page via its app and sales channel settings. [L1301.002]
- Apps add features to the store. Sales channels are platforms through which the store sells. Both are managed from the same Shopify account. [L1301.003]
- The Online Store is the default sales channel, because it is where orders are accepted. Add other sales channels only when the store needs to sell somewhere else. [L1301.004]
- Most sales channels used in practice are native to Shopify and free to activate. Only some third-party channels or add-ons carry a cost. [L1301.006]
- At the time of the lesson, the sales channels available included TikTok, Etsy, the Shopify Marketplace and multi-feeds for Google Shopping. Check Shopify's current sales channel list before relying on them. [L1301.005]
- Shopify sales channels connect the store to traffic platforms such as Facebook, Instagram and Google from within the same account. [L1304.018]

#### Starting point
- Settings > Sales channels: keep only the Online Store active at the foundation stage. [L1327.064, L0096.016]
- Open the Online Store channel and confirm it shows the store name and the primary domain before adding any other sales channel. [L1327.064]
- If Point of Sale appears, click the three dots next to it and choose Uninstall, because this business model does not use it. [L0096.016, L1305.036]
- Keep Online store and Email. Add sales channels such as Facebook, Instagram or Google later as the store grows. [L1305.036]
- Shop is an optional sales channel that can stay enabled. Point of sale is not needed and can be removed under Settings > Apps and sales channels. [L0971.030]
- Do not connect sales channels such as Facebook, Shopify Chat or Wholesale before the store is launched and selling products. Add them later under Sales channels. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1292.023]
- Sales channels vary by country. Facebook is free to add (Facebook fees may apply), and the Wholesale channel is unavailable in some setups. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1292.024]
- Among Shopify's sales channels, Google is generally a poor choice for a new store, while Messenger is a better alternative. eBay can be connected in Australia. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1292.033]
- Conflict: Source B 2021-22 calls Google a poor first choice and Messenger better [L1292.033], while Source A's setup adds Google later as the store grows [L1305.036]. Treat the Source B advice as older.

#### Removing apps and channels
- To remove a sales channel or app: go to Settings (gear icon, bottom left) > Apps and sales channels, hover over the item and click Uninstall. Remove international markets under Settings > Markets. [L0971.074] (Source A, 2024.)

#### Domains
- Settings > Domains shows the Shopify domain and the custom domain. Both are already configured and need no further action. [L0096.017]
- Check that the purchased domain is primary. [L1305.043]

### Activity log
- Settings > Store activity log: a running list of all store changes. It is useful when sales rise or fall, to find what was changed. [L1305.042]

### Setup order for a supplier-ready store
- Procedure: Shopify Settings configuration order for a supplier-ready store (in the sequence of the course):
  1. Store details: set the store name; keep phone, email and billing address until a business phone, email and address exist.
  2. Billing, currency, time zone, units, order ID format.
  3. Plan and billing: check the trial and billing dates.
  4. Users and permissions: add staff if needed.
  5. Payments: activate Shopify Payments, complete PayPal setup, set payment capture to manual.
  6. Checkout: customer contact by email, Shop app link on, customer login off, last name only or first and last name, company name off, phone number not included for shipping, email marketing on, tipping off, shipping address as billing on, order processing option 3, archive fulfilled orders on.
  7. Customer accounts: keep hidden, enable Shop Pay after payments are set up.
  8. Shipping: delete default rates, create Custom flat rate Free shipping at $0, delete the International zone.
  9. Taxes and duties: no changes at this stage.
  10. Locations: keep default location; remove point of sale if installed.
  11. Gift cards: set an expiration date.
  12. Markets: keep only the primary market active.
  13. Apps and sales channels: uninstall point of sale.
  14. Domains: check the purchased domain is primary.
  15. Brand, notifications, languages, policies: revisit after logo, colors and business email are set. [L1305.043]
- Source D 2026 foundation order: first open the account with the business email, then work through Settings > General, Payments, Shipping and delivery, Apps, Domains, Customer privacy and Policies before any sales channel is opened. The lesson treats these as the foundation before advanced settings are touched. [L1327.001]
- At the initial setup stage, most Shopify settings should stay at their defaults. The goal is to learn where settings are located rather than to configure heavily. [L0096.020]
- The general store settings are preset. Other default options do not need changes at launch. [L1306.005]
- At launch, the recommended default options in Settings > Checkout can be left unchanged, apart from the customer contact method, the order tracking link, and the required-field and sign-in settings. Later checkout changes are made in the same section. [L1306.026]
- At the foundation stage, keep Shopify's defaults for advanced settings such as metafields, customer events, post-purchase and marketing pixels. Do not spend time on them until the store is scaling. The reasoning is to build a stable base first. [L1327.055]

#### Further details

- By default, Shopify does not send orders to suppliers. Orders must be forwarded to the supplier separately. [L0663.063]

- In Online Store > Preferences, turn off Google reCAPTCHA on contact forms and on login, create-account and password recovery pages. Enable it only if you get spam, because it can require customers to complete a reCAPTCHA task. [L1145.022]

## Shipping, taxes, markets, currency and international selling

### Shipping model for supplier-fulfilled stores

- Shipping profiles are rules that decide which products ship to which destinations and what they cost. Manage them under Settings > Shipping and delivery in the Shopify admin. [L0970.070]
- Most stores use free shipping on all products through a single profile that covers all products. Upgraded options such as expedited shipping or white-glove delivery are the exception. [L0970.071]
- Standard policy (2025 source): free shipping on all orders to the continental USA. The store pays the shipping cost; the customer does not. [L0255.018]
- Always offer free shipping to all customers, with the company covering the cost. Handle expedited or upgraded shipping and white-glove delivery as fixed-cost add-on options. [L0268.018]
- Free shipping is the norm among competing stores in this niche, so the store offers it and absorbs the shipping cost. [L1305.029]
- Shopify's built-in shipping rates are not used when suppliers ship on their own accounts, because suppliers usually have better prices. Shopify's discounts with UPS, FedEx, DHL and USPS exist but are rarely used by this business. [L0805.033]
- Do not rely on live carrier shipping rates. The store-owner setup is a free shipping option for almost all products, an expedited option as an upcharge, and a white-glove option as a fixed upcharge. [L0359.045]
- Counterpoint (older, 2020): when charging for shipping, Shopify's built-in calculator can display real-time carrier quotes. Use it before looking for third-party shipping apps. [L1088.029]
- A store on a basic Shopify plan does not need supplier locations entered. The store does not use Shopify shipping calculations and handles freight through suppliers and carriers. [L0854.071]
- Shipping abroad only makes sense for small, inexpensive items. Premium and bulky products, which fit the high-ticket model, rarely justify international shipping costs. [L1274.074] (older, 2018)
- Shipping must be reviewed before launch so checkout shows correct delivery costs for each country served. [L1008.004]

#### Multi-supplier procedure (older, 2020)

1. For multiple suppliers, use one free shipping rule: "Continental US = free shipping." [L0268.020]
2. Use fixed-cost add-ons for expedited delivery or white-glove delivery. [L0268.020]
3. For inventory: enter a random inventory number (100-300) if you are not tracking real-time stock. Customers don't see internal numbers. [L0268.020]
4. No need for a setup with 4 or more locations. [L0268.020]

### Free shipping setup

- Basic principle: set up a domestic shipping rate before launch. Without a rate for your country, checkout does not work properly for customers. For most new stores, free shipping is the simplest starting point. [L0677.023]
- Shipping is set up in basic form first, with free shipping as the most appealing option. Later, once real costs and the product mix are known, decide whether to charge shipping for certain locations or products. [L0677.026]
- Free shipping as a general rule (2019, older): in Shopify admin, open Shipping and create one method named "Free shipping" with a price of 0. Customers see it in checkout and pay nothing; the supplier's shipping cost is paid by you. [L0357.050]

#### Free shipping for the US (2023-2025 source)

1. Open General shipping rates in the general shipping profile. [L1305.028]
2. Check the shipping origin address. It defaults to the address entered at account creation; update it later with the business address. [L1305.028]
3. Delete all default rates listed under the shipping rates (the default free rate and others). [L1305.028]
4. Edit the first rate and change its type to Custom flat rate, with no transit time. [L1305.028]
5. Name the rate "Free shipping" and click Add. [L1305.028]
6. Set the price to $0 and the minimum order price to $0. [L1305.028]
7. Click Done. [L1305.028]
8. Under Shipping zones, keep Domestic (United States) with the Free shipping rate at $0. [L1305.028]
9. Delete the International zone. [L1305.028]
10. Confirm that the remaining 27 countries and regions are not covered, so customers can only ship to the United States with free shipping. [L1305.028]
11. Click Save. [L1305.028]

- Settings > Shipping and delivery > General shipping rates: delete all default rates, then add one Custom flat rate with no transit time, named "Free shipping", price $0, minimum order price $0. Click Done. [L1305.027]
- International shipping is not offered at launch because international rates are too expensive. With only the US in the shipping zones, other markets stay uncovered. [L1305.030]

#### Free shipping for Australia (2021-22 source)

1. Go to Settings > Shipping and delivery. [L0677.024]
2. Click Manage rates in the shipping box at the top. [L0677.024]
3. Choose the Domestic profile for Australia. [L0677.024]
4. Delete the default Express and Standard rates. [L0677.024]
5. Delete the Rest of World rate. [L0677.024]
6. Click Add rate, name it "Free shipping" and leave the price at 0 (zero means free). [L0677.024]
7. Click Done. [L0677.024]
8. Click Save. [L0677.024]

- Do not set a Rest of World shipping rate for a store that only sells domestically. Without it, checkout will not let customers continue with a foreign shipping address, which is the desired effect. [L0677.025]
- The Shipping from address in the domestic profile comes from the store address in Settings > General. You can edit the address used for rate calculations in the shipping settings. [L0677.047]
- The Local delivery and Local pickup options on the Settings > Shipping and delivery page are for stores with a physical location and their own warehouse. A dropshipping store does not need to configure them. [L0677.046]
- To charge a different rate for only certain products, create a new profile in Settings > Shipping and delivery instead of changing the default domestic profile. [L0677.048]

### Paid upgrades and expedited options

- Priority shipping is set up in Shopify settings as a shipping option. It is free and takes about 5 minutes to configure. Pair it with a visible delivery time so customers can choose it at checkout. [L1076.045]
- Expedited shipping is built as a shipping profile in Shopify so customers can select it at checkout. No third-party app is needed for expedited rates. [L1094.062]
- Fixed upcharge options: expedited delivery as an upcharge and white-glove delivery as a fixed upcharge. [L0359.045, L0268.018]
- Example of a free option and a paid faster option side by side:
  - Free option: name "Free Express Delivery", Rate type Flat, Price $0.00, Transit time 3 to 5 business days. It appears at checkout with the estimated delivery dates. [L0798.012, L0798.011]
  - Paid faster option: name "Express Priority Delivery", Price $4.95, placed next to the free option. Customers choose between free and paid speed at checkout. [L0798.013, L0798.011]
- Pricing limits (2019-2022 source, older): aim for a maximum shipping charge of about $10 on low-priced products, and ideally offer free shipping by building the cost into the product price. Expedited shipping should not exceed about 15 to 20 percent of the product price. [L1259.041]
- Shopify shipping option timeframes are static text, such as "five to seven days". For longer custom production times, put the lead time on the product pages and leave timeframes off the shipping option text. [L0962.031]

#### Setting up a shipping option (Source D 2026 procedure)

1. Click Settings at the bottom left of the Shopify admin. [L0798.011]
2. Click Shipping and delivery. [L0798.011]
3. Click General profile. [L0798.011]
4. Check the fulfillment location (the shop location) and the shipping zones. [L0798.011]
5. Add a shipping option to the zone with Add shipping option (or Add rate). [L0798.011]
6. Name the first option "Free Express Delivery", set Rate type to Flat and Price to $0.00, and set Transit time to 3 to 5 business days. [L0798.011]
7. Add a second option, open it with Edit shipping option, and name it "Express Priority Delivery" with a price of $4.95. [L0798.011]
8. Save the profile. [L0798.011]

#### Custom shipping rates and zones (2019-2022 sources)

- Delete the default shipping rates and create custom rates such as "Free Track Shipping" (0 USD) and "Expedited Shipping" (4.99 USD). For other regions, create zones, for example "ePacket" or "T4" for Canada, Australia and the United Kingdom. [L1259.040, L1260.049]
- Weight-based shipping rate formula: product weight × rate per pound. Example: at $2 per pound, a 50 lb product costs $100 to ship. Product weights must be accurate for this to work. [L0671.216] (older, 2021)

### Shipping profiles and product-level rules

- Create a new profile for a group of products from one supplier: Settings > Shipping and delivery > Create new profile. Choose the shipping zone first, then add the rate. [L0971.050]
- Some themes do not support different shipping rates for individual products by default. Ask the store developer or support for custom code before installing a third-party app. [L1279.100]
- The Charlotte Shopify theme has one default delivery estimate for all products. Different estimates per supplier, category or product need a custom code change from the developer. [L1281.057]
- Dispatch-based estimate: for products shipped from a supplier that dispatches the same or next day with a domestic carrier, 3 to 5 business days is a safe estimate. Suppliers that dispatch only on certain days, such as weekly on Fridays, need a longer window. [L1069.026]

### Shipping zones and locations

- The General profile applies to all products that are not in another shipping profile. New products are added to it by default. Its rules are set under Settings > Shipping and delivery > General profile. [L0798.008]
- In the General profile, check the Fulfillment location under the shop location setting, because that location sends the orders. The Shipping zones section lists which countries each zone covers and the rates attached to it. [L0798.009]
- A shipping zone can cover many countries at once. In the example store, one zone covered several countries and 234 more, with a paid rate of $3.95 for that zone. [L0798.010]
- Check that each target market is included in shipping in Shopify. If shipping is not set up for a country such as Australia, customers may not be able to check out. [L0950.028]
- Check shipping and sales regions in Shopify against the markets you advertise to. A store sending paid traffic that could not buy because it only shipped to Australia and New Zealand needs its location settings changed. [L1225.039]
- Third-party carrier rates at checkout are switched on under Settings > Shipping and delivery with "Enable third-party calculated rates at checkout". This needs your own carrier account connected. The option showed an "Upgrade your plan" prompt, so check the plan first. The default package under Saved packages feeds these calculations, so enter your real parcel size and weight there. [L1167.037]
- Source D 2026 packages and local delivery: leave the default settings at the foundation stage, including the packing slip template, the pickup in store option and the sender name on shipping labels. Delivery customizations can be used later to hide, reorder or rename delivery options at checkout. [L1327.027]

#### Ask Shopify for setup help before outsourcing (2020, older)

1. Call Shopify support by phone before hiring someone. [L1277.063]
2. Ask them to walk you through the shipping zone and location setup, which may take around 30 minutes. [L1277.063]
3. Only outsource the setup if Shopify cannot resolve it. [L1277.063]

- Use the setup problem above if supplier location ZIP codes are not accepted in Shopify. [L1277.063]

### Estimated delivery and checkout display

#### Estimated delivery on product pages (Source A procedure)

1. In the product template, expand Product information. [L0139.007]
2. Click the eye icon next to Estimated delivery to enable it. [L0139.007]
3. Set the earliest delivery day (example: 4 days). [L0139.007]
4. Set the latest delivery day (example: 7 days). [L0139.007]
5. Save changes. The system automatically updates these calendar dates each night at midnight based on the current day. [L0139.007]

- Estimated delivery text appears on product pages when the setting is not hidden in the theme editor and the country, earliest delivery days, latest delivery days, and text are all filled in. [L0971.054]
- Show when orders are expected to arrive: in Shipping and delivery > General profile > Estimated delivery dates, switch on "Show when orders are expected to arrive" so customers see the expected delivery window before buying. [L0798.015]
- Shopify shows the warning "Not showing transit time may impact conversion at checkout". Hiding the transit time on a shipping option can lower checkout conversion, so keep it visible. [L0798.014]

#### Shipping information and trust

- Customers want to know how a product will be delivered, what it costs, and how trustworthy the delivery company is. Shipping should be presented to look better than what competitors offer. [L0411.024]
- Missing shipping information on the product or cart page weakens trust. Show delivery time and shipping cost clearly before checkout. [L0524.021]
- Never add costs at the cart or checkout stage that the shopper did not see earlier, including shipping, taxes or other fees. Hidden costs are the number one reason for abandoned checkouts. Show any delivery costs on the product page. [L1522.022]
- Rephrase shipping information positively (2019-2022 source, older): instead of "free shipping", state "We pay for shipping". Instead of "delivery in 12 to 15 days", state "We ship within 1 to 2 days". If delivery is slow, add an explanation, such as "Due to high demand, delivery takes 7 to 10 business days; you will get a tracking number within 12 to 24 hours". [L1259.042]
- Add a shipping policy note with typical delivery times, and add a warning that delivery can take longer. [L0964.099]

#### Show "Free" at checkout instead of "calculated at next step"

1. In Shopify go to Settings > Languages. [L1345.062, L1259.035, L1260.043]
2. Open the edit default theme content option. [L1345.062, L1259.035, L1260.043]
3. Search for "shipping". [L1345.062, L1259.035, L1260.043]
4. Set the shipping default value to "Free". [L1345.062, L1259.035, L1260.043]
5. Save and check the checkout to confirm the text reads "Free". [L1345.062]

- If shipping is free, display "Free" at checkout rather than "calculated at next step", because the latter makes buyers expect extra charges. If shipping is not free, the calculated text can stay. [L1345.063]

### Taxes

- Tax settings are found under Settings > Taxes and duties, where tax collection by location and method is configured. [L0855.095]
- Shopify's built-in tax calculation is a starting point. A tax app can track sales thresholds across states as revenue grows. [L0855.106]
- Shopify can manage and collect sales tax and other charges paid by the customer. No changes are made at first; it becomes relevant when a sales tax threshold for states or markets is reached. [L1305.031]
- Shopify adds a default tax setting, such as VAT included at 20 or 21 percent, on new products. If you do not charge taxes, disable the tax setting on each product, because visible tax lines can cause checkout abandonment. [L1259.036]
- If the exact product category is not in Shopify's list, choose the closest top-level category, since taxes may be calculated from the selected category. [L0970.072]
- Change the store owner's tax country in the Shopify admin: open Settings in the bottom left and find the address settings for the account. Update the country to match the business location. [L1111.057]

### Store currency

#### Which currency to choose

- Store currency should match the country where the business is based and where it sells, not the country of the website's audience. Once the first sale is made, the store currency is locked and cannot be changed. [L0677.003]
- Set the store's currency to the currency of the primary selling market (for example USD for a US store). If the account defaulted to another currency such as CAD but the store sells in the US, change it to USD. Settings > Store currency. [L1305.003]
- Set the store's default currency to the currency used by customers in the country being targeted. This keeps prices and checkout consistent for those buyers. [L0853.038] (older, 2021)
- When targeting a new market, set the store currency to the local currency. Showing USD or another foreign currency at checkout causes buyers to lose trust and leave. [L1185.020]
- Currency display for a US market: in Settings > General > Store defaults > Currency display, set US Dollar (USD $) if you sell to the US market. If the store currency is left on a local currency (for example SGD), US customers see the local currency on the website and at checkout, which discourages purchases. The currency customers see is managed under Markets, where the backup region is set to United States. [L1327.005]
- Set the default display currency to the currency of the main traffic source. If most visitors come from the United States, show US dollars by default, because visitors who see unfamiliar prices often leave before buying. [L1325.001]
- Check the default currency: open your own store from the country where most of your traffic comes from, without a VPN, and see which prices appear. If visitors would see an unfamiliar currency, change the store's default to the local one. [L1325.029]
- Keep currency consistent across the whole buying path. Showing prices in one currency on the product page and switching at checkout confuses buyers and makes them leave, especially in markets like the UK. [L0694.048]
- Competitor stores show prices in the visitor's currency and place a country and currency selector in the header, for example CAD for Canada. Set this up before selling into a second market so the displayed price matches what the customer pays. [L0600.040]
- Orders from Canada can be taken in US dollars without conversion. Currency conversion apps exist but are not required to start. [L1092.048] (older, 2022)

#### Without Shopify Payments and with it

- Without Shopify Payments, checkout may show only the store currency (for example USD). With Shopify Payments, buyers see prices in their local currency. Use the same currency as the target market and check the main store currency under Settings. [L1259.039]
- Shopify Payments shows the visitor's currency automatically. For other countries, set up tax and shipping rules in Google Merchant Center first, and keep the other country's Google Shopping campaigns separate from the US ones. [L0834.047] (older, 2021)
- Shopify Multi-Currency Support lets a store display and charge prices in several currencies, which matters when selling to customers in more than one country. [L1089.002] (older, 2021)
- Base currency: each market can have its own base currency, which only Shopify Payments can manage. [L1167.008]

#### Changing the store currency

- To change the store currency for a new market, contact Shopify support on Twitter (X), which replied fastest in this case. Then open a support ticket asking to change the store currency for the new market. The currency can be switched back to the original one later. [L1185.045]
- Changing the store currency can be requested from Shopify support by opening a ticket. It can be switched back later. [L1185.020]

#### Currency conversion apps

- Bucks Currency Converter (free) setup procedure:
  1. Install the app from the Shopify App Store. [L0635.014]
  2. Copy the modified money format HTML with currency and the HTML without currency from the app. [L0635.014]
  3. Go to Shopify Settings > Store currency, click Change formatting, and paste both HTML snippets into the matching fields. [L0635.014]
  4. Save. [L0635.014]
  5. Go to Theme app extension settings, turn on the currency converter option, click Save, confirm with "I have done this", then click Enable app. [L0635.014]
- Once the currency converter is active, visitors from other countries (for example Canada or Australia) see product prices in their own currency on the product page. [L0635.015]
- Currency-conversion apps only convert the displayed price. This conflicts with MAP pricing in dropshipping, so they do not solve custom USD pricing for a Canadian store. Before creating a second store for another currency, ask Shopify support through live chat whether its native features or an app can set separate prices per currency. [L0854.113] (older, 2022)
- Shopify Markets can show prices in visitors' local currency automatically, if enabled. Enabling it does not solve shipping costs, which decide whether selling to a distant market is worth it. [L0360.020] (2024)

#### Currency conversion in a spreadsheet (2022, older)

1. Put USD prices in column A. [L0429.048]
2. In column B, enter a formula multiplying column A by the current exchange rate (CAD cell = USD cell × exchange rate). [L0429.048]
3. Drag the formula down. [L0429.048]
4. Update the rate, because exchange rates change. [L0429.048]

### Shopify Markets

#### Basics

- Shopify Markets is the built-in feature for selling to customers in other countries from one store. Each market groups one or more regions and can carry its own currency, language, catalog and discount settings. [L0798.001]
- Shopify Markets lists 237 regions that can be added to a market. A market can be set to cover specific selected regions instead of all of them. [L0798.002]
- Markets > International > Edit: the regions of the International market are changed here. Use Market conditions set to Specific, then pick the countries and press Done. [L0798.003]
- The market detail page has Parent market and Sub-market fields, so one market can sit inside another in a hierarchy. The market for the store's own country is labeled as the store default in the Markets list. [L0798.021]
- Each market has a Customized section that says "Create unique configurations for customers in this market". It contains Currency, Catalogs, Discounts, Domain and language, and an option for market-specific settings. If nothing is customized, the store configuration applies. [L0798.007]
- Example (Singapore store): the International market used Singapore Dollar (SGD) as its currency in its Customized section, while the Markets list showed each market's region count and status. Checking the currency per market before activating it prevents prices from displaying in the wrong currency. [L0798.022]
- Market settings have separate areas for Languages and domains, Products and pricing (for example All products included and the market currency), Duties and import taxes, and Store settings (Payments and Taxes). [L1167.008]
- Shopify's Markets recommendation says 22% of worldwide online sales are cross-border, and it suggests activating an International market to start selling abroad. Activating that market is what makes the other countries available at checkout. [L1167.040]
- Turning on International Active activates geolocation, which converts product prices into the visitor's local currency automatically. Most merchants stop there, but each target country should also get its own market with manually set prices. [L1316.001]
- Activate markets for each country you target so shoppers see prices in their local currency. Do this before running paid traffic to several countries, otherwise conversion suffers in those markets even when the ads perform. [L1171.053]
- In Shopify Markets, set a specific price for each market instead of relying on automatic conversion. Enter prices such as 24.90 or 26.90 for the UK instead of 27. [L1161.042]
- Shopify Markets sets the language and content for each country, but you must supply the translations yourself or install a translation app to fill them in. [L1158.050]
- Shopify Markets: go to Settings and use Markets to assign a language and translated content to each country. Markets can also serve separate URLs for each market, such as a subfolder or suffix for France. [L1158.033]

#### Setting up the International market

1. In the Shopify admin left menu, click Markets. [L0798.005]
2. Click International. The default market for the store's own country is shown; if it is not the market you want, work in the International market. [L0798.005]
3. Click Edit. [L0798.005]
4. Add the top five countries: United States, United Kingdom, Canada, Australia, New Zealand. Add more regions if needed. [L0798.005, L0798.004]
5. Click Done. [L0798.005]
6. Check the market status. A new market starts as Draft; change it to Active so it can sell. [L0798.005, L0798.006]

- For the first international expansion, add the five largest English-speaking export markets: United States, United Kingdom, Canada, Australia and New Zealand. These five are the recommended starting set. [L0798.004]
- A new market often starts in Draft status. A market in Draft does not sell, so it must be switched to Active after the regions are added. [L0798.006]

#### Market setup order (Source D 2026 procedure)

1. Go to Settings > Markets > International. [L1167.006]
2. Set the market to Active. Only active markets allow customers in those countries to check out. [L1167.006]
3. Confirm the countries you sell to are included, for example United States and United Kingdom. [L1167.006]
4. Once Shopify Payments is set up, change the base currency to match the currency of the place you sell (USD in the example for US sales). [L1167.006]
5. Click Save. [L1167.006]

#### Inactive markets block checkout

- A market that is inactive blocks checkout, so customers in its countries cannot buy. The Markets overview shows each market as Primary, Active or Inactive, and the preview displays "Customers can't check out" for inactive markets. [L1167.007]
- Shopify does not enable checkout for every market by default, so visitors from other countries may be unable to check out. Check the Markets settings for each country you want to sell to. [L0363.034]
- Targeting countries such as Canada, New Zealand or Australia while only the US is activated in Shopify Markets causes checkout failures. Customers cannot select their country and abandon the cart. [L0773.007]
- A new store with inactive markets lost sales: only the primary market (United States) was active, so buyers from other countries could not check out. This caused high add-to-cart numbers without conversion. [L1259.038]
- A new store with a disabled non-US market lost around 100-200 sales within an hour because visitors from other countries could not check out. Always test the checkout for every target country. [L1260.046]
- While selling in one country, keep the primary market (for a US store, United States) active and keep International inactive. Over time Markets show which market makes most sales and conversion rates per market. [L1305.035]

#### Checking markets before sending traffic

1. Go to Settings > Markets. [L1259.037, L1260.045]
2. Verify all target countries (for example USA, Canada, Australia, UK) are active. [L1259.037, L1260.045]
3. Test checkout with a random address in each country. [L1259.037, L1260.045]
4. Ensure the store can take orders in each target market before ads go live. [L1259.037]

- Disabled markets block checkout and can lose sales. [L1260.045]

#### Shopify Markets and language

- Shopify Markets lets you assign a language and translated content to each country. [L1158.033]
- Use a separate store or market for each target country when the market shows consistent results. Translate not only landing pages but also checkout language and default Shopify text, so the whole experience is in the local language. [L1158.034]
- Shopify suggested adding German to a market that already includes Austria, because German converts best there. Match market languages to the countries where a language is most likely to be used. [L1167.009]

### Languages and translation

#### Adding languages

- Settings > Languages: add a language to serve customers in their own language. Shopify states that translations improve cross-border conversion by an average of 13% and that adding them is free. The Published languages list shows which languages are active in the markets they were added to. [L1250.016]
- Add German for customers in Germany (or other markets where German is used). The lesson says translations improve cross-border conversion by an average of 13%. [L1327.049]
- For a multi-language store, begin with one English store and use Shopify's built-in translation feature rather than a second store. Shopify reports traffic sources, which makes it easier to see where customers come from. [L0352.037]
- For a multi-language store, use a translation app or Shopify's native translation feature with a language selector instead of building separate content for each language. The language switches automatically when a visitor selects it. [L1113.032]
