- A theme can be published while the store is still password protected, so the theme can be prepared before the store opens to visitors. The password protection stays active until it is removed. [L1330.014]
- In Shopify, the password protection of a store is managed on the theme card under Online Store > Themes. [L1330.015]
- The theme card shows the buttons Edit password and Remove password, and displays Password protected while the protection is active. [L1330.015]

### Moving to a new Shopify store while reusing content

Moving to a new Shopify store while reusing content:

1. Create the new store with another email. [L1382.061]
2. Export the theme from the online store section and import it into the new store. [L1382.061]
3. Export and import the customer and SMS lists, and set up the email settings and apps again (Shopify's export and import buttons handle the data). [L1382.061]
4. Rebuild any settings that do not transfer, such as logos and notification email settings. [L1382.061]

## Brand assets: logo, colors, fonts and image production

### Setup order and file preparation

1. Check the theme's image sizes before you design anything. Each Shopify theme needs its own graphic sizes, and files made for one theme may not fit another [L0098.002, L0342.009, L1045.001].
2. Designing without checking the theme's dimensions produces files that crop, stretch or do not fit [L1045.038].
3. Look up the theme's sizes in the theme backend and resize to them before uploading. Oversized images can push section titles down the page [L0665.029].
4. The theme is called "Manhattan (Source A)" in one unit and "Source A" in others. The size figures below come from both names, so confirm they match your theme before you use them [L0098.002, L0342.008, L1045.001].
5. Upload all brand images in bulk before you edit the theme: Shopify admin > Content > Files > Upload files. Uploaded files are not linked to any section automatically, but you can pick them later in the theme editor without uploading again [L0775.001, L0857.058].
6. For bulk uploads, use the Files section for many images at once. Large uploads can run in the background while you keep working. Image links can also go into the product CSV instead [L1111.063] (2020, older).
7. Note the download folder and file name as soon as you save a file. Some computers save to Downloads and others to the desktop [L0342.012, L1045.013].
8. Shopify and Canva both ask for files, so keep the Canva originals. Canva stores finished designs under All designs and you can download them again without rebuilding [L0342.013].
9. Store graphics change with sales, holidays and new collections. Refresh them regularly, and keep the Canva files so the set can be updated quickly [L0342.037, L1045.035].

Setup order for the theme (Source E, 2025-26):

1. Open Online Store > Themes, click Customize, and go to Theme settings > Logos.
2. Upload the main logo, then the mobile logo.
3. Check the logo in the mobile preview before you continue. About 90% of store traffic is typically on phones [L1331.001].
4. Go to Theme settings > Colors and paste the brand hex codes into the existing color fields [L1331.001].
5. Go to Typography, upload the font file, and paste its link into the font field [L1331.001].
6. In Logos, select the favicon and upload the favicon file made from the logo [L1331.001].
7. Save after each step and refresh the preview [L1331.001].

### Canva work

**Free plan limits**
- On the free plan, the Transparent background option in the download menu is greyed out. A PNG downloads with a white background. Transparent PNGs need the paid plan [L1045.011].
- The white PNG shows as a white box around the logo on any coloured section of the site. Either remove the background before uploading, or use a paid plan with transparent download [L0342.010, L1045.011, L1045.037].
- An existing template cannot be resized on the free plan. To get a different banner shape, create a new design [L0342.021, L1045.021].

**Working in Canva**
- Start from a template for each asset type, then change text, colours and images to fit the brand. The template links are faster than building from scratch [L1045.036].
- Text: select the text and use the text colour (A) icon to choose a brand colour. Use the highlight options for font, bold, letter spacing and line spacing [L0342.032].
- Centring: select several elements, open the three-dot menu, and choose Position > Center [L0342.032].
- To use a photo as a banner background: search the Elements panel under Photos, drag the photo onto the design, right-click it (or two-finger click on a trackpad), choose Set as background, then move the image to the right position [L1045.020].
- Once a photo is a background, you can move it inside the frame to position the subject. For example, lower it so a person's head is not cut off at the top [L1045.047].
- Supplier product photos can be uploaded to Canva the same way as stock photos. This makes all images in a set crop to the same size [L1045.024].
- Canva's built-in Photos library has many free images for popular niches such as surfing. For niches with few usable stock images, such as basic furniture or mechanical products, download photos from Pexels, upload them to Canva and use them like built-in photos [L1045.046].
- For a website banner, search the Canva templates for "website banner" and use that size as the starting format. Check the result on mobile first, then adjust the desktop version [L1219.020].
- Canva is enough to create a simple logo and to edit ebook covers and branding at the start, without a designer [L0871.020].

**Ebook assets (tools)**
- Use Canva's ebook template: search "ebook", choose a cover, type the title and author name, and export it for the store [L0513.025].
- Placeit creates 3D book mockups. Upload the Canva cover, set the book colour to match (for example black), and download the mockup. This needs a subscription [L0513.026].
- Pexels.com provides free stock photos for ebook covers. All images are free except those from iStock [L0513.027].

### Logo and favicon formats

**Logo formats**
- Shopify accepts SVG for logos. Upload the SVG to the logo settings rather than a pixelated image [L0952.036].
- PageFly also accepts SVG uploads for logos and icons [L1462.018].
- SVG can be enlarged up to ten times without losing sharpness, because it is built from mathematical shapes and angles [L0952.037].
- PNG is pixel-based and loses quality when enlarged. Use PNG as the fallback logo format where the platform does not accept SVG [L0952.038].
- Export logos with a transparent background, so they work on white, dark and dark-mode websites without a visible box [L0952.039].
- Upload PNGs with transparent backgrounds to PageFly, so the logo sits on any section colour without a white box [L1462.018].
- When a logo goes in a header, export only the logo selection with no extra page margin, so the header height is not affected [L0952.040].
- Logo for the Source A theme: 800 × 300 px, PNG, transparent background [L0075.001, L0342.008].
- Fill the artboard close to its edges, with the logo filling the template almost edge to edge at the right proportions. This prevents empty margins or wrong shapes when uploaded [L0342.008, L1045.010].
- Keep the logo simple. A text-plus-icon logo is easier to read at small sizes and looks more professional. A logo that is too detailed or showy is hard to read on the site [L1045.039].
- A designer logo can be very large, for example 1527 × 518 px, which loads slowly at full size. Crop the surrounding white space, then resize to about 500 px wide while keeping the rectangular shape. This suits the header and checkout (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1371.046].
- Pixel dimensions describe width by height, not file size. A 40 × 40 px image is 40 pixels across and 40 pixels down. Logos and favicons are sized by these pixel dimensions, usually with the Resize function in the editing tool (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1371.045].

**Favicon**
- Favicon size: 64 × 64 px, JPG or PNG, transparent background [L0075.001].
- The favicon is the small icon in the browser tab. Using the same icon as the logo is the simplest, most consistent choice [L1045.015].
- Create the favicon in Canva:
  1. Open the favicon template from the Source A templates and click Use Template [L0342.014, L1045.014].
  2. Copy the icon from the logo design, paste it into the favicon template, and centre it [L0342.014, L1045.014].
  3. Click Download and keep PNG. On the free plan the file keeps a white background, because transparency is not available. JPEG is an alternative and also keeps the white background [L0342.014, L1045.014].

### Logo and favicon in Shopify

**Uploading the logo**
1. Open Theme settings (gear icon in the left sidebar), or go to Online Store > Themes > Customize > Theme settings > Logo [L0775.005, L0954.014].
2. Under Logo, click Select image and choose the file from Files. Alternatively, in the header section, open the logo setting in the theme settings and upload the file [L0775.005, L0954.014, L1326.007].
3. Click Done and check the header preview. Adjust the size until it displays correctly [L0775.005, L0954.014, L1326.007].
- The default desktop logo width is 200 px. Change it only if the uploaded file is noticeably larger or smaller, and check the preview [L0775.006].
- The logo appears in the store header after upload [L0954.014].
- In one example store, the logo height was set back to 100 after being enlarged. The logo should be neither so large it crowds the header nor so small it is hard to read. Mobile uses the same logo image, but its size is set separately [L1371.047] (Source B, 2021-22, older).
- In Source D' theme, footer logos are chosen the same way as header logos [L1188.004].

**Uploading the favicon**
1. Open Theme settings > Favicon.
2. Click Select image and choose a small version of your logo, or another image that represents the store.
3. Click Done [L0775.007].
- If the favicon is not replaced, the browser tab shows Shopify's default logo instead of your brand. Setting it takes under a minute and matters even for a demo store [L0775.008].

**Brand and metadata**
- Shopify Settings > Brand: logo, brand colours, slogan and description are added after the logo and colours are created. Social media pages are linked here later [L1305.038].
- Add your logo and update the metadata with your store information. This helps the store look distinct from copycat stores [L0920.026].

**Source D theme settings**
- Theme settings > General Settings contain Logo, Colours, Typography, Button Styling, Navigation Buttons, Brand information, Search behaviour, Animations and Cart Drawer options. Logo images are chosen with the Select from library button [L1188.004].

### Colour palettes

**Shopify colour fields**
- Theme settings > Colors contains: Solid button label, Accent 1, Accent 2, Secondary text, Outline button, Background 1, Background 2 and Price for you save. Click a swatch and type a six-digit hex code [L0775.010].
- The Source E example set includes Text and Background 1 and 2 as well as the fields above [L1331.002].
- Example A (Source A): Solid button label white; Accent 1 brand pink; Accent 2 brand teal; Secondary text black; Outline button brand blue; Background 1 solid white; Background 2 light grey [L0775.011].
- Example B (Source E): Solid button label #FFFFFF; Accent 1 #6D388B, used for solid buttons; near-black #121212 as the dark contrast colour [L1331.002].
- Keep the theme's default dark and light colours in their roles. Replace only their values with brand colours, so the store stays consistent [L1331.003].
- Changes show in the homepage preview as soon as you click Save. Scroll through the preview after each change to check buttons, backgrounds and text [L0775.014].

**How many colours**
- Use a solid palette of 2-4 brand colours for a professional, trustworthy look [L0101.019].
- Avoid too many colours. Pick a few and reuse them across buttons, menu links, borders and banners, rather than a unique colour for each element [L0775.013].
- Keep one accent colour for buttons. If you use a gradient, reuse the same accent rather than pasting new colours each time [L1331.003].
- Gradient options exist for accent colours, but a simple store can leave them off and use solid colours only. Leaving Accent 2 at no colour is valid when no second accent is needed [L0775.012].

**Choosing colours**
1. Search Google Images for "colour palette" [L0355.010].
2. Pick a palette whose colours already fit together [L0355.010].
3. Copy the colour codes from that palette [L0355.010].
4. Use those codes as the website colours, so the whole design matches [L0355.010].
- Match colours to the audience. For an older audience, minimal design with white and grey backgrounds and black text usually works better than bright colours [L0656.020].
- A simple, clean store uses a header announcement bar and a limited set of recurring colours. The example store used green, white and grey consistently [L0657.036].
- Use the store's colour scheme in AI prompts for section graphics if the section should match the site [L1075.028].

**Problems**
- A near-black page background makes black product photos disappear into the page. Use a background colour that contrasts with the product images on any page showing dark products [L1225.090].
- Footer colours are set in Theme settings > Colors, scrolled to the bottom. The Footer group contains: Background, Headings, Text, Text link, Text link hover, Menu list link, Menu list link hover, Additional menu link, Additional menu link hover and Copyright text. Social icon style is set in the same section, with the options Light on dark or Dark on light. Adjust the copyright text colour so it matches the rest of the footer [L0681.001].

**Brand redesign sequence (Source E)**
1. Upload the new logo to replace the old one.
2. Open Theme Settings > Colors and update all colour codes with hex codes from the brand guidelines.
3. Open Theme Settings > Typography and change the heading and body fonts to your paired fonts.
4. Rewrite the website copy in brand voice: screenshot each section, paste it into ChatGPT, rewrite it in brand voice, and copy it back to Shopify.
5. Preview all changes and publish [L0317.021].

### Typography

**Where to set fonts**
- Theme settings > Typography sets the font for all text on the store by default. Click Change, search Shopify's font library, select a font and click Select [L0775.015].
- Shopify path: Theme Settings > Customize theme > Typography [L0317.015].
- For each page type (homepage, collection page, product page), open the theme settings and choose the page type. Change fonts and styles page by page from the dropdown (2020, older) [L1111.062].

**Font choice and speed**
- System fonts, such as Helvetica, New York, system_ui and SF Mono, are pre-installed and load fast. Downloaded fonts, such as Abel, Abril Fatface, Acme, Alegreya, Poppins and Playfair, load more slowly because visitors must download them. Prioritise system fonts for faster page load [L0317.015].
- The Shopify font list shows Sans-serif, Serif and Mono fonts, which load faster, and "Other" fonts, which are downloaded to the visitor's computer and can slow load times. Prefer the faster fonts (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1371.016].
- Adding too many fonts and colours quickly makes a store look messy [L0775.013].

**Pairing**
- Set the body font to Montserrat or a similar clean, readable sans-serif for accessibility. Choose a distinct display font for the heading font, to create visual hierarchy and brand distinction [L0101.015].
- Typography includes a Font size scale, which enlarges or reduces all text proportionally. It also includes Body font with weight options such as regular, italic, medium and medium italic. A 110% scale and medium weight are example values that gave bolder, more readable body text [L0775.016].
- Heading font size is a percentage. It defaults to 130% in the Manhattan theme. Setting it to 100% makes product titles smaller [L0966.056].
- A light body font weight can make product descriptions look faint. Take a screenshot before you change the font weight [L0971.052].

**Using a brand font**
1. Download the free Deja Vu Serif font (or your brand font) and unzip it [L1331.004].
2. Upload the TTF file in Shopify admin under Content > Files [L1331.004].
3. Copy the file link and paste it into the theme's font field, so the store font matches the logo font [L1331.004].
4. Upload a few options and compare them before choosing [L1331.004].
- Brand Fetch can report font data wrongly for Shopify sites. Custom fonts often appear as CSS variable names, such as --font-body-family, not real typeface names such as Helvetica or Georgia. Visit the live website or inspect the theme CSS to find the real font. Do not assume the variable name is the typeface [L0318.022].

### Layout and buttons

- Theme settings > Layout sets the page width. The default is 1200 px and it can be raised to 1600 px. A wider page only shows on larger monitors, so 1200 px is a safe default [L0775.017].
- Theme settings > Buttons controls border thickness, opacity, corner radius and transparency for all buttons. The same area includes Variant pills (product variant display), Inputs (for example search box size), and Product and collection cards. Default values are fine for a beginner store [L0775.018].

### Theme image size specifications

| Asset Type | Dimensions | Format | Requirements |
|---|---|---|---|
| Logo | 800px × 300px | PNG | Transparent background |
| Favicon | 64px × 64px | JPG or PNG | Transparent background |
| Mega Menu Icons | 250px × 250px | JPG or PNG | Transparent background |
| Slideshow/Image Banner | 1800px × 400-900px height | JPG |, |
| Product Page Graphic | 1600px × 200-600px height | JPG or PNG | PNG requires transparent background |
| Collection Images (collection page) | 1600px × 400px | JPG |, |
| Collection Images (homepage) | 800px × 800px | JPG |, |
| Blog Images | 1600px × 900px or any 16:9 ratio | JPG |, |
| Pre-Footer | Less than 285px × 285px | JPG or PNG | PNG requires transparent background |

[L0075.001]

- These specifications are for the Manhattan (Source A) theme. Confirm the graphic sizes for your specific theme before creating assets [L0098.002].
- Shopify displays one image per collection on the homepage, in a square format. Wide, banner-style images are poorly cropped in this display [L0098.024].

### Banners and slideshow images

**Sizes**
- The table gives 1800 × 400-900 px height for slideshow and image banners [L0075.001].
- For the Source A theme, the standard homepage banner is 1800 × 600 px. Banners are full-width graphics used as slideshow images [L0342.018, L1045.018].
- A custom banner shape can be made in Canva, for example 1800 × 900 px [L0342.021, L1045.021].
- Shopify's slide image field shows a recommended size of 1800 × 1000 px. This differs from the 1600 px hero width in the older PicMonkey procedure below. Compare the theme's hint with the size you prepared, and choose the one that fits your slideshow height before uploading [L1310.041] (Source B, 2021-22, older).
- For a wide hero banner, a 1000 × 500 px result is workable. A 624 × 628 px square cannot fill a full-width banner and should be rejected for that slot, even though it suits a square image area [L1310.040] (Source B, 2021-22, older).

**Text on banners**
- The theme can place text over banner images itself. Banners can be plain photos with no text added in Canva [L0098.014, L0342.019, L1045.019].
- Add text to banners only when a design-level message is wanted [L0342.019].

**Hero banner procedure (PicMonkey, 2021-22, older)**
1. Search a stock source for an image that reflects the outcome your customer wants [L1310.016].
2. Check the image dimensions and reject images too small for a full-width banner [L1310.016].
3. Download the image and open it in PicMonkey [L1310.016].
4. Use Smart resize to set the width to 1600 px, and reduce the height to between 400 and 600 px [L1310.016].
5. If one dimension is still too large, crop the side you do not need, keeping the main subject [L1310.016].
6. Download the edited image [L1310.016].
7. Compress it in ImageOptim [L1310.016].
8. Upload it to the Shopify slideshow [L1310.016].

### Collection images

**Sizes**
- Collection page banner: 1600 × 400 px, JPG [L0075.001].
- Homepage collection image: 800 × 800 px, JPG [L0075.001].

**Collection page banners**
- Collection page banners are plain banner-format images with no text. Each collection gets one, uploaded to that collection. They act as atmospheric backgrounds at the top of each collection page [L1045.028, L0342.023].
- They can show lifestyle scenes, zoomed-in details, or colour and fabric swatches that match the products [L1045.028].
- Each collection can have its own image. A more specific one (for example living room couches versus living room chairs) helps buyers recognise the category [L0342.023].
- Before suppliers are connected, generic lifestyle photos are enough for collection banners. Product-specific images can replace them later, and collection images can be refreshed when new products or suppliers are added [L0342.024].

**One image for two places**
- A collection's image is shared between the collection page and the homepage. The same collection cannot show different images in each place [L0342.025, L1045.029].
- Use either the long collection banners or the square homepage collection images, not both for the same collections [L0342.025, L1045.029].
- Expecting one image on the homepage and another on the collection page does not work. Plan one image per collection [L1045.040].
- Homepage collection images are square. A long banner reused on the homepage is cropped from its centre, so place the main subject in the middle of any image used in both places [L0342.026, L1045.030].

### Product page graphics, blog images and pre-footer

**Product page graphic**
- Size: 1600 × 200-600 px height, JPG or PNG. PNG needs a transparent background [L0075.001].
- The product page graphic appears directly above or below the Add to Cart button. Add, change or remove it depending on the season or whether a sale is running [L1045.025].

**Blog images**
- Size: 1600 × 900 px, or any 16:9 ratio, JPG [L0075.001].
- Blog images are optional. Create them in Canva only if your store uses blog posts or blog previews [L0098.031].
- For featured blog tiles, you can use templates with text overlays instead of plain image backgrounds [L0098.032].
- Keep the same size across all posts so the blog layout stays consistent. A blog template can carry a title over the image, and each post can use a different background image [L1045.032].

**Pre-footer**
- Size: less than 285 × 285 px, JPG or PNG. PNG needs a transparent background [L0075.001].

### Mega menu icons

- The Source A theme supports up to five mega menu icons. Each icon is a separate page in one Canva design. Downloading the design produces a separate image file for each page [L0342.015, L1045.016].
- Size: 250 × 250 px, JPG or PNG, transparent background [L0075.001].
- Create them in Canva:
  1. Click the mega menu icons template in Canva [L0342.016].
  2. Open Elements, add an icon (for example the favicon icon or a wave), make it full size, centre it and set its colour [L0342.016].
  3. Click Add page and repeat for each additional icon, up to five in total [L0342.016, L1045.016].
  4. Click Download. Each page downloads as a separate image file [L0342.016].

### Product photo sourcing and editing

**Why photos matter**
- Ecommerce sites depend on images for two core functions: brand identity (logos and graphics) and product visibility, which lets buyers make informed purchase decisions [L0300.008].
- Images are often the largest elements on a page and the biggest contributor to load time. Compress them offline before upload. Compression apps are less controllable than a dedicated offline tool [L1266.015].
- Poor product images are a common reason stores do not convert. Find better images before concluding the product is weak [L1345.028].
- Lifestyle images help buyers see the product in use and increase trust. A landing page needs good-quality product and lifestyle images, which make the brand look better and more trustworthy [L1382.028].

**Supplier and stock images**
- Supplier and stock images come in varying dimensions and file sizes. They usually need editing or resizing to fit specific layout sections [L0300.009].
- Image sources to check, in order: Pexels, Pixabay, Google Images, Alibaba, AliExpress. If no good image exists, look for a very similar product. Instagram hashtag research can also reveal raw user photos for the niche [L1345.029].
- Check whether the store's product photos are the seller's own. Matching backgrounds, tables and hands across several images show the owner shot them, which is stronger than generic supplier photos. Use this as a quality check when the store looks different from the supplier listing [L0530.035].
- Put your own brand name on copied product photos before publishing. This makes them your store's assets, not identical copies of a competitor's [L0600.010].
- Supplier product photos can be downloaded from the supplier's website. If the file type is not JPEG, convert it with a free online converter, found by searching for the file type conversion (2020, older) [L1111.075].
- Generic lifestyle photos are enough for collection banners before suppliers connect [L0342.024].

**Editing yourself or outsourcing**
- During setup, editing single images yourself with PicMonkey is often faster than coordinating with a freelance designer. It is practical to learn basic image editing skills early (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L0300.010].
- Standard editing sequence: edit images in PicMonkey by resizing, cropping and adding text as needed. Then selectively compress the larger files with platform-specific tools: ImageOptim for Mac, or Windows' built-in compression [L0300.012] (Source B, 2021-22, older).
- Cropping or resizing in PicMonkey does not change the original file on your computer. Download the edited version as a new file. Download as PNG to keep a transparent background [L1371.044].
- For bulk processing, outsource it. Give a freelancer a Photoshop automation or batch script that resizes each image, places it on a white background if needed, and compresses it. The speaker paid about $10 per 100 images for this, which he called one of the cheapest things to outsource (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1456.023].
- Fiverr retouchers can be hired for about $5 to $10 per image to change backgrounds, add pop, and clean up pixelation [L1345.031].
- AI image tools such as DALL-E (transcribed as "Dahl") can create or edit product images that look realistic [L1345.031].
- DALL-E can create new images for a print-on-demand store, and can edit an existing product photo and produce variations from the same source image [L1416.006].

**Supplier file preparation (2021-22, older)**
1. Rename the file to include the supplier name, product description and model number. Default file names are often gibberish [L1456.020].
2. Give multiple images of one product distinct file names by adding a word such as front, back, side or in use [L1456.020].
3. Set the alt text. It can match the file name, but it is a separate field. Apps that copy the title to every image produce duplicate alt text. That is weaker for image search than unique alt text, but better than none [L1456.020].
4. Resize the image from 2000 × 2000 px to about 800 × 800 or 1000 × 1000 px. This keeps good resolution while loading fast on mobile [L1456.020, L1456.021].
5. Save as JPEG, which is smaller than PNG [L1456.020].
6. Compress the image again with a compressor such as ImageOptim on Mac [L1456.020].

**Background and layout**
- Keep the background white if the image is not a full-size lifestyle shot [L1163.016].
- Put no text on the image, except branding that is on the product itself [L1163.016].
- Apply a unique alt tag to every image, and give every image file a descriptive file name [L1163.016].
- Product images in a grid should share the same proportions, such as square. Mixed sizes in a grid usually come from the dimensions of the uploaded files, not from a theme setting, so resize them to match [L0971.088].

### Product image sizing rules

**Target sizes (sources disagree)**
- Ideal size: 800 × 800 px, square, not rectangular (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1163.008].
- Minimum size: never smaller than 600 × 600 px. Smaller images may stop a product from being advertised on Google Shopping (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1163.008].
- Minimum size: at least 1000 × 1000 px. Smaller images look pixelated when customers enlarge them (2023) [L0970.058].
- Both values apply to different needs. The 600 px minimum is about Google Shopping eligibility. The 1000 px minimum is about on-site enlargement quality, and is the more recent advice [L1163.008, L0970.058].
- Maximum: do not exceed 1000 × 1000 px. Larger files slow the site, and mobile users cannot see the extra detail, so oversized images hurt site speed, conversions and SEO (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1163.009].
- A 1024 × 1024 px size was suggested for product images in 2020. It was not confirmed, so check the exact size with the platform or your support contact before uploading the full set [L0961.049].
- A 1:1 template works for uniform images. Place a rectangular image on an 800 × 800 canvas and scale it as large as possible without distortion. Square images are not required, but uniform sizes look better side by side (2023) [L0470.089].
- A 500 × 500 px canvas was the example Canva size for a product image design [L0036.032].
- A square product image at 1080 × 1080 px fits square product tiles and social-style grids. Prepare images at this ratio before upload [L1462.045].

**Very large and very small files**
- Very large supplier images, such as 8000 × 8000 px, slow page loading. Resize them before upload, and keep them at a size that still meets the 1000 × 1000 px minimum [L0970.059].
- Product images at 2000 × 2000 px are too large and slow the page on mobile. Resize them before upload [L1456.021] (Source B, 2021-22, older).
- Product photos above about 1 MB are too large and should be compressed. Slow opening of image files or slow uploads is a sign that compression is needed (2019, older) [L1002.022].
- Product images above roughly 3000 × 3000 px are usually more than a website needs. A 10,000 × 10,000 px image can be reduced to 1000 × 1000 px with no visible loss on desktop or phone, and about ten times less data (2019, older) [L1002.023].

**Consistency**
- All product images on the site should share one consistent size and shape. Mixed sizes make collection pages look messy. Some advertising channels, especially Google Shopping, also require standard image dimensions (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1163.007].
- Do not resize all product images to one identical size. Suppliers send different aspect ratios, and forced sizing causes cropping. Compress the files to reduce file size instead [L0972.021].
- Do not convert product images to different aspect ratios (square or landscape) in bulk. Resizing without keeping proportions crops important parts or distorts the image [L0278.035].
- Shopify's product grids and collection layouts depend on consistent proportions, so resize product images to the recommended sizes before upload [L0665.029].

### AI image prompts

**Product photo prompt (studio)**

Run this prompt with the product name filled in:

```
Act as a professional product photographer shooting for a high-end Shopify storefront. Create a photorealistic image of this item: [PRODUCT NAME GOES HERE]. These requirements are mandatory and must be followed exactly. The backdrop is flat pure white at #FFFFFF, with no gradient, no texture, no scenery, no floor line, no horizon, and no shadow reaching the frame edges. The item sits centered and fully in view with nothing cut off, shows true-to-life proportions and scale, and displays realistic materials and fine detail in a brand-new state. Logos may appear only if they are generic or unbranded. Light it with soft studio lighting from several directions, with shadow only directly beneath the object. Keep contrast high and edges crisp, in the style of catalog photography comparable to Amazon, Shopify, or premium brand listings. Shoot from a front three-quarter angle, slightly rotated for depth. Deliver ultra-high resolution, sharp focus, no motion blur, no artistic filters, no stylization, and a fully photographic look. Exclude people, hands, props, any text, watermarks, branding overlays, and reflections of the camera or the room. The final image should read as if it came from a professional product studio and be ready to upload directly to a Shopify product page.
```

[L0536.001]

- Before running the prompt, replace [PRODUCT NAME GOES HERE] with the exact product name plus its key visible traits (colour, material, size category), so the image matches the real item [L0536.001].
- For sets or multi-part products, place every part together in one frame and keep each part fully visible [L0536.001].

**Hero shot prompt (uploaded item)**

```
Create a square picture of the uploaded item on a plain, pure white backdrop. Position the item in the middle of the frame with soft shadows that give it a sense of depth. Since this is the product's primary hero shot, keep it sharp, evenly lit, and free of any text, props, or background scenery.

Product fidelity: reproduce the item's exact form, dimensions, colour, texture, and surface finish. Do not invent branding or packaging that is not present in the uploaded photo. If the uploaded photo already displays a logo or label, leave it exactly as it appears.
```

[L0887.033]

- The two prompts disagree on shadows. The studio prompt allows shadow only directly beneath the object, with no shadow reaching the frame edges [L0536.001]. The hero shot prompt asks for soft shadows that give depth [L0887.033]. Use the studio prompt for catalogue-style listings with strict rules. Use the hero shot prompt when you start from the supplier's own photo and need to keep its branding and details exactly [L0536.001, L0887.033].
- The studio prompt allows generic or unbranded logos only [L0536.001]. The hero shot prompt keeps any logo or label already in the uploaded photo unchanged [L0887.033].

**AI graphics for store sections**
- AI image generation can make visuals for sections that have no product photo, such as a free-shipping banner. It can also cover product pages, blog posts, and About Us or Contact Us pages. Describe the section's message and the scene or graphic you want. Review the result for accuracy before publishing [L1075.028].
- If the section should match the site, include the store's colour scheme in the prompt [L1075.028].

### Compression and file formats

**Targets**
- Compress images to the smallest possible file size while keeping visual quality. Slow-loading images hurt the site [L0300.002].
- Uploading uncompressed images of several megabytes significantly slows the site and reduces conversion rates [L0300.003].
- Product images: under 300 KB, ideally under 50 KB, with quality as close to the original as possible. Compress with TinyPNG or Photoshop before upload [L1345.032].
- Product photos above about 1 MB should be compressed (2019, older) [L1002.022].
- GIFs: compress below 800 KB. They can reach 1 MB, but the target is 800 KB. For product pages, create GIFs from one to three second clips of existing video ads [L1345.033].
- Animated GIFs load slowly. A 1.5-5 MB GIF converted to WebP becomes roughly 200-500 KB [L1382.033].
- JPG, PNG and GIF converted to WebP can reduce a 1-2 MB image to around 200 KB or less. Start with product images, because they load first [L1412.043].

**Tools**
- Compress before upload with tinypng.com for one-off images, or with an image compression app that does the same job [L0963.043].
- On Mac, ImageOptim compresses images offline [L0300.012, L1310.016, L1456.020].
- Smart SEO app or SEO app: reduces file sizes to improve page load speed without cropping or distorting images (Source A, 2025) [L0278.041].
- Conflict on apps: Source B (2021-22, older) advises against Shopify apps that resize and compress images on-site. They slow the store, some run scripts that hurt speed, and some lose quality. Process images outside the store instead [L1456.022]. Source C advises against installing an app only to compress images, because it adds code for little benefit. Use an online converter instead [L1412.053]. Source A's 2025 recommendation of Smart SEO is the more recent advice, and it applies when you want on-site compression [L0278.041].

**Conversion procedure (without an app)**
1. Search for a bulk PNG or JPG to WebP converter website [L1412.044].
2. Upload all product images and GIFs at once [L1412.044].
3. Download the converted files, which usually come as one zip [L1412.044].
4. Upload the WebP images to the store one by one [L1412.044].

**Formats**
- Save as JPEG for product photos. JPEG is smaller than PNG [L1456.020].
- Use PNG only when the image needs a transparent background [L0709.011].
- Prefer progressive JPEG unless the image needs a transparent background, in which case use PNG [L0709.011].
- Resize and compress images for each device. Use Liquid in the theme to serve smaller sizes where needed. Choose file type and quality as needed [L0709.011].

### Tools

| Tool | Use | Notes | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Canva | Logo, favicon, mega menu icons, banners, blog images, ebook covers | Free plan: white-background PNG, no resizing of existing designs | [L0871.020, L0513.025, L1045.011, L1045.021] |
| Placeit | 3D book mockups from a Canva cover | Subscription needed | [L0513.026] |
| Pexels.com | Free stock photos | All free except iStock | [L0513.027, L1045.046] |
| PicMonkey | Resize, crop, add text, Smart resize to 1600 px | Download as PNG to keep transparency | [L0300.010, L1310.016, L1371.044] (older) |
| ImageOptim | Offline compression, Mac (Windows: built-in compression) | Used after editing | [L0300.012, L1310.016, L1456.020] (older) |
| TinyPNG | Compression before upload | One-off images | [L0963.043, L1345.032] |
| Smart SEO / SEO app | On-site compression that does not crop or distort | Source A, 2025 | [L0278.041] |
| DALL-E | Create or edit realistic product images, variations | Can be used for POD stores | [L1345.031, L1416.006] |
| Fiverr retouchers | Background change, clean-up | About $5-10 per image | [L1345.031] |
| Photoshop batch script | Bulk resize, white background, compression | Outsourced at about $10 per 100 images | [L1456.023] (older) |
| Shopify Content > Files | Upload brand files and font files | Files can be picked later in the theme editor | [L0775.001, L0857.058, L1331.004] |

#### Further details

- Using GIFs heavily on a product store can make it look cluttered; keep the store design clean. [L0656.019]

## Collections, tags and filters

### Collection types

- A collection is a product category. It groups related products so visitors can find a product type, size, color, price range or featured group without searching. [L0371.001, L1312.001, L0083.001]
- Shopify has two collection types. A manual collection contains only the products added to it one by one. An automated collection includes every product that matches the conditions you set. [L1312.002, L0371.003, L0083.004]
- Automated collections suit a store that keeps uploading products, because new items sort themselves into the right collections. [L1312.002, L0083.002, L1303.006]
- Automated collections scale better and need no ongoing maintenance. [L0083.003]
- In a dropshipping store, automated collections should be used exclusively. [L0083.004]
- Manual collections only make sense when you want full control over a fixed list of products. [L0371.004]
- Stricter rule: do not assign products to collections manually at all. Use only tag-based automated collections. [L0088.007]
- Automated collections update in real time when products are tagged or untagged. No manual syncing is needed. [L0083.012]
- A product can belong to several collections at once, because each collection decides membership with its own rule. [L0083.001, L0971.089]
- Automated collections can also be built by supplier, so every product from one supplier lands in that supplier's collection. Price-based automated collections work the same way. [L0371.005]
- Typical product-type collections for a stand-up desk store: straight, curved and L-shaped stand-up desks. [L0083.016]
- Typical attribute collections: size, color, price range, featured items, bestsellers, staff picks, new arrivals and on-sale items. [L0083.016, L0371.002]
- Typical collections for a niche store: product types within the niche, size, color, material or feature, price ranges, and featured groups such as Bestsellers, Staff Picks, New Arrivals and Sale. [L0371.002]
- Example: a bamboo L-shaped desk tagged with both "L-shaped" and "bamboo" is listed automatically in the L-shaped collection and in the bamboo collection. [L0371.007]
- Collections can also group by skill level (beginner, intermediate, advanced) or by brand, because customers search for those groupings. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1463.004]
- Product types and collections have separate jobs. Assign each product one product type based on what it is, for example hybrid, shortboard, mid-length or longboard. Use collections for shopping paths such as price band, skill level, kids or color. [L1287.022]

### Planning which collections to create

- Set up collections before importing products. New products are then placed in the right collections as they are added, which keeps the organization consistent from the start. [L0083.002]
- Before creating anything, list every collection you plan to use. Avoid over-building the structure and focus on collections that match your actual product range. [L0083.014]
- Do not create every possible collection at the start. Collections can always be added later, so begin with the ones you are most likely to use. [L0371.013, L1312.004]
- Build the main collections first as separate entries. Sub-collections are created afterwards as additional separate entries. [L0083.008]
- Sub-collections are created the same way as any other collection. Shopify has no special subcollection setting. The parent-child link is set later in the navigation menu. [L0371.018, L1312.008, L0083.008, L0083.015]
- Do not configure menu placement while creating a collection. Link collections to the navigation once all collections exist. [L0083.015, L1303.001]
- Brand collections should not be created until suppliers have approved your store, even if competitors show them. Other collections can be created first. [L0371.012, L1312.007]
- Product categories can be broader than one product type, as long as they are related. Unrelated categories look like a copied template and can trigger review. [L0855.105]
- Create one collection per product group when the store has several categories, for example couch covers, or beds and blankets. A store with only a few products can keep them all in one collection. [L1326.019]
- Decide how products are grouped before building collections. For a non-technical beginner this was the hardest part of Shopify setup. [L1437.013]
- Avoid massive collections. Keep them specific and narrow, because long lists of near-identical products increase confusion. Very large collections only suit a store with very different products, such as a department store. [L1522.009]
- The same product can appear in several collections under different names, when customers may use different terms for it. Each name a shopper uses can lead them to the product. [L1522.007]
- Create collections around sub-styles within a niche, such as a gothic style or a colorful style, and group matching products in each. [L1325.012]
- Before naming style collections, check whether the store already has blog posts on those styles. Use the same terms, because existing topics show which style words the audience already recognizes. [L1325.031]
- Starting set sizes differ by source:
  - Five starting collections built around the product types you will sell, or five variations of one main product. This is a placeholder set, so pick them quickly and refine later (Source B, 2021-22, older). [L1303.003, L1303.005]
  - A starter set of six for a new demo store: Bestsellers, Staff picks, Top rated, New arrivals, On sale and one price-based collection such as Under 100. With three collections per row on desktop, six fill two rows. [L1309.064]
- Initial collections are placeholders and are not final. Rebuild them once suppliers are confirmed and the real product types are known. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.005]
- Research collection ideas from competitors:
  1. Pick the two to three future competitor stores in your niche and open their navigation menus. [L0083.007]
  2. Hover over each main category in the navigation to see its subcategories. [L1312.006]
  3. Note how they group products: by type, brand, size or price range. [L1312.006]
  4. Write the ideas in a document such as Google Docs or Notes. List each main collection and indent its subcategories beneath it. [L1312.006]
  5. Keep the list focused. You need several collections, but not every possible one. [L1312.006]
- Empty category pages (soft 404) can be deleted if they are not needed, and re-added when products for them are found. (Source A, 2022, older.) [L0429.041]

### Creating a tag-based automated collection

Procedure, merged from the three source procedures and the Source C variant:

1. Log in to the Shopify admin. Go to Products > Collections. The collections area is the first link under Products. [L0083.009, L0371.009]
2. Click Create collection in the top right corner. [L0371.014, L1312.009, L0371.009]
3. If you do not sell in person, switch off the Point of sale option. [L0371.014, L1312.009]
4. Enter the collection name in the Title field. The title is the name customers see. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009, L0371.015]
5. Leave the Description blank for now. It is the text shown above the products and can be added later. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009]
6. Skip images for now. They can be added later. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009]
7. Keep the default theme template unless your theme offers a different one that fits. [L0371.014, L1312.009, L1303.030]
8. Set Collection type to Automated. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009]
9. Set the condition to Product tag, is equal to, and enter the tag exactly as it is used on the products, for example surfboards. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009, L1326.018]
10. Check the Search engine listing and the URL handle. Verify the URL handle matches the collection name. Leave the meta description for the later SEO phase. [L0083.009, L0371.014, L1312.009, L0083.011, L0083.013]
11. Click Save and confirm the collection appears in the Collections list. [L0083.009, L1312.009]
12. Repeat for each collection on your list. [L0371.014]

Source C variant (short form, same logic): create the collection, for example titled Headphones, set Product tag is equal to headphones, save, add the tag to each product that belongs in it, then open the collection and confirm the products appear. [L1326.018]

### Collection conditions

- Shopify conditions are fields such as price, supplier, tag, product type, product title, compare-at price, weight or inventory. Tag-based conditions are the most commonly used approach. [L1312.003, L0083.005]
- Tag and Price are the two conditions used most in the course. [L0371.008]
- Set "Products must match" to all conditions if every condition has to be true. Set it to any condition if one match is enough. Conditions are the rules Shopify uses to decide membership automatically. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.007]
- Available operators: is equal to, is not equal to, is greater than, is less than, starts with, ends with, contains and does not contain. Use is equal to when the value must match exactly and contains for partial matches. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.009]
- Condition fields and typical use (Source B, 2021-22, older):

| Condition field | Typical use in collections |
|---|---|
| Product title | Match words in the product name |
| Product type | Match the product category set on each product |
| Product vendor | Match the brand or supplier name |
| Product tag | Match a keyword tag assigned to products (most common) |
| Product price | Filter by selling price |
| Compare at price | Filter by the compare-at price |
| Weight | Filter by product weight |
| Inventory stock | Filter by stock quantity |
| Variant's title | Match the variant name |

  [L1303.008]
- Tag the products of the same category with one shared keyword, then use the condition Product tag is equal to that keyword. Shopify pulls every product with the tag in automatically. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.010]
- Matching by product title contains a keyword is an alternative to tags and works the same way if the keyword appears in product names. There is no single right method. Choose one and use it consistently across the initial collections. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.011]
- Example of mixed conditions for an off-grid power products store (Source B, 2021-22, older):
  - Solar Power: product tag is equal to solar
  - Generators: product title contains generator
  - Water Filtration: product title contains water
  - Food Supplies: product tag is equal to food
  - Waste Disposal: product tag is equal to waste
  
  Mixing tag and title conditions is acceptable at this starting stage because the collections are placeholders. [L1303.032]
- Creating a collection with generic conditions (Source B, 2021-22, older):
  1. In the Shopify admin, click Products in the left-hand menu. [L1303.002]
  2. Under Products, open Collections. [L1303.002]
  3. In a new store, tick the box of the default Home page collection, then click Delete. [L1303.002]
  4. Click Create collection. [L1303.002]
  5. Enter the collection title, which is the name customers see. Leave the description empty if you do not need one. [L1303.002]
  6. Set Collection type to Automated. [L1303.002]
  7. Under Conditions, choose all conditions or any condition, then select a field, an operator and a value. [L1303.002, L1303.007]
  8. Click Save. [L1303.002]

### Price-band collections

- Price-band automated collections group products into bands, for example products priced 1 to 200, 2 to 300 and 3 to 500 dollars. Link them in the navigation as "Shop by price". This gives shoppers price sorting without extra apps. (Source A, 2021, older.) [L0968.035]
- Each band is a separate collection, so each product lands in the one matching its price. [L0371.022]
- Example band set for a stand-up desk store: $300 to $500, $500 to $800, $800 to $1,000, and $1,000 and up. [L0371.022]
- Example of a $300 to $500 price collection. [L0371.005]
- Price-range procedure:
  1. Navigate to Products > Collections and click Create collection. [L0083.010]
  2. Select Collection type: Automated. [L0083.010]
  3. For items priced $2,000 and above, set the condition Price is greater than $2,000. [L0083.010]
  4. For items priced between $1,000 and $2,000, set two conditions: Price is greater than $999.99 AND Price is less than $2,000.01. [L0083.010]
  5. Click Save. [L0083.010]
- "$2,000 and up" setting: click Create collection, keep Collection type on Automated, change the condition field to Price, select "is greater than" and enter 2000. Keep Products must match on all conditions. Every product priced above that value that you upload later enters the collection automatically. [L1312.013]
- Price-range formula for $1,000 to $2,000 with Products must match set to all conditions:

  ```
  Condition 1: Price is greater than 999.99
  Condition 2: Price is less than 2000.01
  Result: products priced above 999.99 and below 2000.01 are included,
          so a product at exactly 1000.00 or 2000.00 belongs to this range.
  ```

  [L1312.014]
- Boundary rule: Shopify price conditions use "is greater than" and "is less than", which do not match a product priced exactly at the threshold. For a range, set the lower bound a cent below and the upper bound a cent above, such as 999.99 and 2000.01. [L0371.021, L1312.014]
- When price collections meet at a boundary, use offset limits instead of exact values. The $2,000-and-up collection only takes prices above 2000, so a product at exactly 2000.00 sits in the range collection and does not fall between two collections or appear in both. [L1312.015]
- A higher tier is built the same way: a "$200 and up" tier uses the single condition Price is greater than 200. Tiers can be stacked by changing only the threshold values. A range tier uses two conditions as in the $1,000 to $2,000 example. [L1312.016]
- Threshold values differ by store. The course examples use $2,000 and up [L0083.010, L1312.013], $1,000 and up [L0371.022] and $200 and up [L1312.016]. Use the top band that matches your own price range. The same boundary rules apply in each case. [L0083.010, L1312.013, L0371.022, L1312.016, L1312.015]

### Featured and sorting collections

- Featured collections group products such as bestsellers, staff picks, new arrivals or items currently on sale, instead of grouping by product type. They help visitors find popular or timely products quickly. [L1312.005]
- Featured collection examples: Bestsellers, Staff Picks, New Arrivals, Sale. [L0371.002]
- Featured groups for a demo store: Bestsellers, Staff picks, Top rated, New arrivals, On sale. [L1309.064]
- Featured groups as a filter: bestsellers, staff picks, new arrivals and top rated. The tags must already exist on products in the store, because a tag list only lists tags applied to products. [L0369.014]
- Organize products into collections and sort each collection with best sellers first, on the homepage and collection pages. Shoppers look for what others want most, which reduces the chance they leave. [L1098.009]
- To see a store's best-selling products, open the collection URL /collections/all?sort_by=best_selling. [L0871.019]

### Product types and tags

#### Tag basics

- Apply multiple tags to a product. Tags place products automatically into the matching collections, so the store is organized by category without manual collection assignment. [L0036.015]
- Tags are created automatically the first time you enter them in the tags field. There is no separate place to create them first. [L0036.028]
- A product can carry several tags and then appears in every collection whose tag rule it matches. [L0371.006]
- Tag-based automated collections are the primary method. [L0371.006]
- Each tag is one collection rule. Tags assigned to a product on its page decide where it appears. [L0971.089, L0371.023]
- Once a product is saved with a tag, it is added automatically to every automated collection whose Product tag condition matches that tag. [L0371.023]
- A tag-based condition of "product tag is equal to" only picks up products that carry that exact tag name. Use the same spelling on every product that should appear. [L1312.010]

#### Adding tags to a product

Procedure, merged from the course variants:

1. Go to Shopify Admin > Products and click into the product, or select the product. [L0103.005, L0369.010, L0890.005]
2. Scroll to the Tags section, or open the Tags field. [L0103.005, L1326.018]
3. Add relevant tags, for example longboards, blue, hybrid. Each product that should appear under a filter needs the matching tag. [L0103.005, L0369.010, L1326.018]
4. Click Save. The product immediately becomes available in all matching filter tags and collections. [L0103.005, L0369.010, L0890.005]
5. Refresh the storefront collection page to confirm the product appears. [L0890.011]

#### Tag hygiene

- Tag spelling must be identical in the product tags and in the collection or filter entries. Example: "Shortboards" written as one word in both places. A spelling difference means the filter does not match the tagged products, so the grid shows fewer or no results. [L1010.022, L0369.008, L0890.007]
- A tag list only matches exactly, including spelling, spacing and capitalization. A mismatch hides that option even for tagged products. [L0369.008]
- Mistake, two-word tag: a tag written as one word did not match the two-word version, so only two of three options appeared until the tag was corrected. [L0369.011]
- Fixing case problems (Source A, 2020, older):
  1. Open the product in the Shopify admin. [L1111.061]
  2. Click edit. [L1111.061]
  3. Delete the existing tags. [L1111.061]
  4. Re-enter the tags in the correct case, such as lowercase. [L1111.061]
- Shopify has no bulk tag management interface. Tags cannot be deleted in one place. To remove a tag, it must be removed from every product individually. (Source A, 2025.) [L0255.029]
- Once a tag has been autocompleted in Shopify, the system remembers it. Removing it from all products does not delete it from autocomplete. (Source A, 2025.) [L0255.030]
- Tag lists can be filled in advance, but only tags carried by at least one product show as filter options. A tag such as "purple" with no matching product produces no option. [L0890.003]

#### Changing product membership

- Membership is controlled from the product page, not from the collection. Removing a tag from a product makes it leave the collection. [L0971.027, L0971.089]
- Procedure to find why a product is in an unwanted collection (Source A, 2024):
  1. Open Shopify admin > Products > Collections. [L0971.028]
  2. Check the number of products and the condition of the collection. [L0971.028]
  3. Open the collection to confirm the rule, for example Product tag is equal to bestsellers. [L0971.028]
  4. Open the product page and review the tags applied. [L0971.028]
  5. Remove the tag to remove the product from the collection. [L0971.028]
- Duplicate collections: when collections created by hand and by the supplier share the same tag, products appear twice. Check the collection conditions and remove the shared tag from one of the collections. (Source A, 2024.) [L0856.067]
- Automated collections need no manual syncing. Refresh after saving changes, and if a new filter value is missing, wait a moment and refresh again before troubleshooting. [L0890.011, L0083.012]

### SEO and handle fields

- The key fields on the Create collection form are Title, Description, Images, Theme template, Collection type, Conditions, Search engine listing and URL handle. [L0371.015]
- The Title is the name customers see. The Collection type decides whether products are added by hand or by conditions. [L0371.015]
- The URL handle is created from the Title by default. It usually looks like your-domain.com/collections/keyword. Always check it before saving, because it should end with the exact collection name. [L0371.016, L1312.011, L0083.011]
- The URL of a collection follows the pattern yourdomain.com/collections/keyword. The handle copies the title by default, so check it before saving. [L1312.011]
- Do not edit the SEO fields (collection description, page title, meta description) during the initial setup. They are configured in a later stage. [L0083.013]
- Check the search listing during setup, but leave the meta description for the later SEO phase. [L0371.014, L1312.009]
- Collection SEO is set under Search engine listing preview, then Edit website SEO. Add a title and description for search engines. The listing preview shows how the collection will appear in results. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.012]
- The collection description is useful for SEO and appears above the product list. Long descriptions go below the product grid. [L1312.009, L0368.006]
- A short description of one or two sentences can stay at the top without a noticeable effect. [L0368.006]
- Renaming a collection: on an established store, rename it and add a redirect from the old URL. This keeps traffic and links to the old page. On a new store with no live products, redirects are not necessary. (Source A, 2023.) [L1114.027]
- Text over collection images is a theme option, not an image edit, so no Photoshop or Canva work is needed. Enable the text overlay in the theme settings and enter the text. Whether to use it is a preference, so judge by how it looks. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L0664.038]

### Collection templates

#### Template basics

- The Theme templates section of the collection editor has a Collection template field set to "collection" by default. This is the template customers see. Leave it at the default unless you have a reason to change it. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.030]
- The Collection availability section lists the sales channels the collection is published to. On a new store it reads that the collection will be available to one sales channel, Online Store, with a Schedule availability option beneath it. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1303.031]
- One template can be assigned to many collections. One default template covered 30 collections in the example. Separate templates are only needed if some collection pages must have a different sidebar or other layout. [L0369.002]
- Each custom collection template affects only the collection it is linked to. Separate templates can therefore be built for each collection, with different text, images and layout. Layout changes such as alignment, image width, hiding the button or adding sections are made in the template editor. [L0368.010]

#### Creating a custom template

Procedure, merged from the course variants:

1. Go to Shopify admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize. [L0255.021, L0368.007]
2. Open the template dropdown and select Collections. Click Create template. [L0255.021, L0368.007]
3. Name the template after the collection or its keyword, for example "hybrid surfboards". [L0255.021, L0368.007]
4. Base the template on the default collection. Set the preview to that collection, so the layout shows real products. [L0368.007]
5. Add sections. Click the plus icon "Add section" and choose the section type, such as Rich text, collapsible content or images. [L0255.021, L0368.008, L0368.022]
6. Drag sections to the desired order. [L0255.021]
7. Save the template. [L0255.021]
8. Go to Products > Collections in the admin and open the collection. [L0255.021, L0368.009]
9. Under Theme template, change "Default collection" to the new template. [L0255.021, L0368.009]
10. Click Save and check the page with View. The content appears on the collection page. Without step 9 the template stays assigned to zero collections and the custom layout never appears. [L0255.021, L0368.009]

#### Rich text and sections

- Rich text content procedure: in the collection template editor, click the plus icon "Add section" and choose Rich text. Replace the filler text "talk about your brand" with the target keyword, prefixed by a qualifier such as "shop". Paste the edited description into the text box and save. [L0368.008]
- Sections can be stacked on the same template. Individual buttons inside a section can be hidden by hovering over them, or deleted if they are not needed. [L0368.022]
- An "image with text" section places an image next to text. Image height and width, text alignment and animation can be changed. It can split a long collection description into blocks, for example one paragraph per column with an image beside it. [L0368.023]

#### Manhattan theme: template editing

Procedure to edit a collection page in the Manhattan theme (Source A):

1. Open the Shopify admin. [L0369.001]
2. Go to Online Store > Themes. [L0369.001]
3. Click Customize and confirm that the Manhattan theme is the active theme. [L0369.001]
4. Open the page dropdown at the top and select Collections. [L0369.001]
5. Open the default collection template, which is the template used by the collection pages. [L0369.001]

- Breadcrumbs option: shows Home > current collection above the products. It should stay set to show. The option can be set to hide, but hiding it is not recommended. [L0369.003]
- Keep breadcrumb navigation enabled, since it helps both SEO and customer navigation. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L0370.001]
- The Collection banner section can show a collection image above the products. Uploading a collection image is not recommended in this layout, because it pushes the product grid and filters further down the page. [L0369.004]
- The Collection banner section has settings for image height, a color scheme and padding. The stock scheme is background 1, with a white background and black text. Gray and accent color schemes are available. Padding moves content up or down. Leave these on the stock values unless a change is needed. [L0369.005]
- The main menu can appear in the sidebar of a collection page, but it is not needed there because the main menu already sits at the top of the store. It can be removed from the sidebar. A different menu can be selected in the same settings if one is needed at the top. [L0369.006]

#### Description placement

- Place long collection descriptions below the product grid, not above it, so the products stay above the fold. [L0368.006]
- The description shown above the products is the one left blank at creation and added later. Use it for SEO content. [L0371.014, L1312.009]

### Collection images

- Upload collection cover images directly in Shopify Admin > Products > Collections. This stops Shopify from randomly selecting product images as thumbnails, which would look unprofessional and undermine perceived store legitimacy. [L0101.006]
- A collection without its own image uses the picture of one of its products as the cover. This looks unfinished when the store has few products. Upload a dedicated image to each collection. [L0775.044, L1309.032]
- Upload procedure:
  1. Go to Products > Collections and search for the collection. [L0775.045]
  2. Open it and click Add image under the image area. [L0775.045, L1309.032]
  3. Select the file and click Open. [L0775.045]
  4. Click Save and check the homepage preview. [L0775.045, L1309.032]
- Conflict on the collection banner: outside the Manhattan banner, upload a dedicated image to each collection [L0101.006, L0775.044]. Inside the Manhattan Collection banner, uploading an image is not recommended, because it pushes the grid and filters down [L0369.004].

### Collection page layout

#### Opening the collection page editor

- To reach the collection layout editor: Shopify admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize. In the editor's top dropdown, switch the page from Homepage to Collections, then pick the Default collection to preview how any collection page looks. [L0890.001, L0103.001, L1010.014]
- Edit theme > change the page view dropdown from Home page to Collections > select Default collection to manage collection navigation. [L0146.012]
- Expand the Product Grid section using its expand or collapse arrow. The Product Grid section controls how products display and where filters appear. [L0103.001, L0890.002]
- By default, a collection page shows every product in that collection, followed by the footer menu and any other sections added to the page. Tag list filters are added on top of this default layout through the Product Grid section. [L0890.012]

#### Sidebar and filter layout

- A plain grid is the default. It is functional but basic. Adding filters improves usability and shows potential suppliers a scalable store structure. [L0103.002]
- Sidebar on or off (sources differ, both apply):
  - Keep the sidebar and filters from the start on a new store with demo or few products. A few simple filter lists are enough and should be set up early, then extended as more supplier products with more variations arrive. [L0369.017, L1098.011, L1522.008]
  - Turn the sidebar off when no filters are needed yet. Go to the collection page section in the theme editor and uncheck "Show sidebar" under the SIDEBAR settings. This removes an empty white column. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L0370.001]
- Collection sidebar filtering helps visitors narrow products by style or print. [L1098.011]
- Effective filters let shoppers narrow similar products by the attributes they actually search for. Without filters, shoppers struggle to tell similar products apart. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1522.008]
- The Manhattan theme includes filtering by default, so no third-party filter app is needed. Visitors can filter by availability (in stock or out of stock), by minimum and maximum price, and by Types and Vendors. Vendors shows the brand entered when the product was uploaded. [L0369.015]
- Product filters based on product tags need no extra cost. Filtering by metafields usually requires custom code and is more expensive. (Source A, 2024.) [L0666.058]
- Default Types and Vendors blocks can be deleted from the Manhattan sidebar and replaced with custom tag list blocks. The defaults are quick to recreate later if needed. [L0369.018]
- Settings for the Collection Grid Page in the theme editor (Source D 2026) include: Enable side filter menu; Collection settings with options to filter products on the page and set text, pricing and stock colors; and Pagination settings with Enable pagination, Products per page and an option to load all products on a single page. [L1013.013]
- Pagination source conflict: Source D shows the pagination settings in the theme editor [L1013.013]. Source A says pagination on Shopify is usually set in the theme code, so a developer may be needed to show all products on a category page [L0575.022]. Check the theme editor first. If no load-all setting is there, a developer is required.

#### Utility bar and other layout settings

- Theme editor > Collection pages > Utility bar. Options are Show 'Sort by' dropdown, Show products per page links, Show grid/list view toggle and Show product counts. Collection pages also offer a Products per page value, a Pagination method (such as Jump-to-page) and a collection image banner at the top. These can stay at their defaults during the build phase. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L0370.027]
- A collection page lets shoppers buy several different products in one visit, which raises average order value and makes cross-selling easier. It suits products that customers buy in multiples or alongside each other. (Source C.) [L0388.012]

### Filter setup with tag lists

#### Adding a tag-list filter

Procedure, merged from the course variants:

1. Open Shopify admin > Online Store > Themes and open the theme editor. [L0890.002]
2. Switch the dropdown at the top from Homepage to Collections and select the Default collection preview. [L0890.002, L0103.004]
3. Expand the Product Grid section. [L0103.004, L0890.002]
4. Click Add block and choose Tag List. [L0103.004, L0890.002]
5. Give the tag list a title, for example "Surfboards by Type", and enter the product tags to filter by, for example shortboards, mid length, longboards, hybrid. [L0103.004, L0890.002, L0103.006]
6. Click Save. [L0103.004, L0890.002]
7. Go to the live store, open the collection page and refresh it. The filter appears in the left navigation. [L0890.002, L0103.004]
8. Repeat steps 4 to 6 for further filter dimensions, for example a "Color" tag list with yellow, red and blue. Drag the new tag list below the existing ones. [L0890.002, L0103.007]
9. Click Save and refresh the live collection page to test. [L0890.002, L0103.004]

#### Manhattan theme: adding a tag-list filter

1. Open the collection template in the theme editor. [L0369.007]
2. In the sidebar section, click Add block and choose Tag list. [L0369.007]
3. Give the block a name that describes the filter, such as a product type or a color. [L0369.007]
4. Enter the tag names separated by a comma followed by a space, using exactly the same spelling as the tags on the products. [L0369.007]
5. Click Save. [L0369.007]
6. Drag the block to the top of the sidebar if it should appear first. [L0369.007]
7. Click Save again. [L0369.007]

#### Updating filters after new products arrive

1. Review newly uploaded products. [L0146.014]
2. Add relevant tags to each product, for example surfboard type, color or skill level. [L0146.014]
3. Return to the collection page in the theme editor. [L0146.014]
4. Expand tag lists in the collection sidebar based on the tags you added. [L0146.014]
5. Save changes. [L0146.014]

#### How filters behave

- A tag list block is not shown in the sidebar if none of the products in the store carries any of its tags. It appears once at least one product carries one of those tags. [L0369.009, L0103.003]
- Shopify only displays tags as filter options if products with those tags exist in the collection. Tags without matching products do not appear, even if they are defined. [L0103.003, L0890.003]
- Multiple tag lists combine. Selecting one value from each list narrows the results to products that carry both tags. For example, Long Boards AND Blue shows only products with both. [L0103.008, L0890.004]
- Example of stacked filters: selecting "Longboards" then "Blue" displays only products tagged with both. [L0103.008]
- When a visitor selects a filter, only products carrying that tag are shown. The other filter blocks then show only options that exist among the remaining products, so the number of options shrinks as more filters are applied. [L0369.012, L0369.021]
- Visitors can clear a selected filter by unchecking it. This restores the other filter options that were hidden. [L0369.021]
- One product can carry several tags from the same tag list and then appears under every matching option. For example, a yellow surfboard tagged with both mid length and longboard shows under both options in a product type filter. [L0369.019]
- Test case: a product tagged with both a product-type tag and a color tag appeared only when both matching filters were selected. The page then returned only the products carrying both tags, here two products in the same collection. [L0890.013]

#### Filter examples

- A filter block titled "Surfboards by Type" with tags shortboards, mid length, longboards and hybrid appears as clickable options on the left side of the collection page. [L0103.006]
- A filter block titled "Color" with tags yellow, red and blue can sit below the Surfboards by Type filter. Visitors can refine by color after or instead of board type. [L0103.007]
- A color filter can be built as a tag list named Color with yellow, red and blue, each added to the matching products. Choosing yellow limits the page to yellow products and can remove other options, such as a product type the yellow product does not have. [L0369.013]
- A featured filter can be built as a tag list with bestsellers, staff picks, new arrivals and top rated. The tags must already exist on products in the store. [L0369.014]

#### What to show on a demo store

- For a demo store, add only one filter group: filter by product type to match the main menu structure. This shows a scalable structure while keeping the store simple and clean for supplier review. [L0103.009]
- Demo-store scope differs by source. Start with one group by product type [L0103.009] or with a few simple lists [L0369.017]. Extend the lists as more supplier products with more variations are added [L0369.017].
- Working collection filters, even on a small demo store, signal to suppliers reviewing it that the store has a complete, usable setup. Set up basic filters before contacting suppliers. [L0369.022]

#### Checking the storefront

- A newly added tag list may not appear in the theme editor preview after saving. Save, then open the live collection page and refresh it, rather than relying on the preview. [L0890.010, L0369.020]
- A tag list only shows on the storefront once the saved changes are live. After saving tag list changes, reload the live collection page to confirm the filter appears and works. [L0369.020]
- After a product's tags change, the storefront filter may lag briefly. Refresh the live collection page after saving. If the new value is still missing, wait a moment and refresh again before troubleshooting. [L0890.011]

#### Further details

- Tag-based collections work by assigning each product a relevant tag and setting the collection to automatically include all products with that matching tag. [L0083.006]

- Collection filters are based on product tags. Only products with matching tags appear in filter options. [L0146.013]

- A collection works as a category that lets visitors browse and filter products. Collections are usually organized by product type, brand or another attribute that customers would want to filter by. [L1303.004]

## Navigation and menus

### Menu basics in Shopify

- Menus are managed in the Shopify admin under Content > Menus [L0092.001, L1319.002].
- Older course material (2021-2022) gives the path as Online Store > Navigation [L1303.013, L1320.001].
- Menu items can link to collections, products, pages, blog posts, the homepage, the search page, policy pages, or external URLs. Choose the link type in the item's Link field before saving [L1319.016, L0092.002, L1320.007, L1320.009].
- Menu item names are free text and do not have to match the linked page or collection. A refund policy can be listed as Returns/Exchanges [L1319.017, L1320.009].
- Policies appear under the Policies heading in the link search once they exist [L1320.007].
- Shopify does not allow a menu item without a URL [L1009.014].
- Create all collections, pages, and policies before building menus, because menu items can only link to existing items. This includes shortcut collections such as Bestsellers and On Sale Now [L1319.019].
- When searching for a collection in the link field, copy its exact name from the collections list and paste it. The search matches text, so a near-miss can return the wrong result [L1320.014].
- Give each menu a unique name. Two menus with the same name cause confusion [L1285.028].
- Each menu gets a handle generated from its title in lowercase with hyphens, for example main-menu. The theme uses the handle to find the menu, so the default handle is what the theme looks up [L1303.015].
- The header menu title is not displayed on the storefront and can keep a plain internal name. Footer menu titles appear as visible headings and must read as meaningful labels such as Company or Products [L1303.033].
- Save after every change, because lost connections can discard unsaved work [L1303.018].
- Save, then refresh the storefront. Menu changes can take a minute or more to appear [L1319.006, L1320.002, L1320.003].
- Menus show on the live site only after the theme loads them, so check the live page to catch missing links early [L1303.035].
- Before replacing a menu, take a screenshot of its items so the original structure can be rebuilt [L1010.012].
- Deleted menu items can be re-added at any time, so removing a wrong item is not a lasting error [L1320.003, L1320.012].
- The arrow beside a parent item in the menu editor collapses or expands its sub-items. This only helps while editing and does not change the storefront [L1319.018, L1320.013].

### Default menus in a new store

- Sources differ on how many menus a new store starts with. One source says three default menus, and most themes include at least three menu spaces (a main menu and two footer menus) [L1319.003]. Another says two default menus: Footer menu and Main menu [L1320.001]. Check the menu list in your own admin.
- The default main menu contains Home, Catalog, and Contact [L1326.008].
- The default footer menu is initially labelled Search [L1320.005].
