- Each footer menu assignment needs a menu that already exists. Create the footer menu and the quick links menu before assigning them to blocks 2 and 3. [L1018.013]
- Preparation before editing the footer:
  1. Create the main shopping menu (Search, main product category, New Arrivals, On Sale). [L0680.017]
  2. Create the Quick Links menu (About Us, Privacy Policy, Terms of Service, Contact Us). [L0680.017]
  3. Create a business email address. [L0680.017]
  4. Upload the store logo. [L0680.017]
  
  Only then open the footer editor. [L0680.017]
- Save every footer change before refreshing the preview, because an unsaved change does not appear on the store. [L1018.014]
- In the theme editor, a footer block can be edited in two ways: select it in the left navigation so its settings open in the right sidebar, or click the block directly in the store preview. Both routes change the same settings, so use whichever is faster. [L1018.016]
- Do not judge footer size and layout only in the theme editor. The preview can show overlapping elements that do not appear on the full-screen live store, so always refresh and check the live store before changing settings. [L0680.013]
- The preview can show the footer email text as oversized and overlapping. This is a preview artifact, so check the live store before changing the settings. [L1018.010]

### Footer block 1: Contact us

- Contact/Contact Us footer block, procedure:
  1. Click the Contact / Contact Us footer block in Theme Customizer. [L0102.002]
  2. Set Title to Contact Us. [L0102.002]
  3. Set Subheading text to something simple (example: Reach out). [L0102.002]
  4. Set Email to support@yourdomain.com. [L0102.002]
  5. Remove phone number placeholder if you do not have one yet; add the phone number later when available. [L0102.002]
  6. Set Footer image to the same logo used in the header. [L0102.002]
  7. Set Image width to 50%. [L0102.002]
  8. Save and refresh the storefront preview. [L0102.002]
- Footer first column (Contact us section): set the heading to Contact us and enter a short text such as Reach out, followed by a business email address and optionally a phone number. Upload the store logo as the footer image. [L0680.007]
- Footer contact block: add a heading "Contact us" with a short prompt line such as "have a question", then list the business email address and the phone number below it. These two contact methods satisfy the requirement for two contact methods in the footer. [L0377.028, L0377.019]
- Footer should show a contact us block with both a business email address and a phone number. [L0377.019, L0377.028]
- Create the business email addresses (for example info@ or support@ on the store domain) before contacting suppliers, and place them in the footer contact section. [L0680.014]
- A phone number in the footer is optional at the start of a store. It can be left out or deleted until the business has a phone line ready for supplier contact. [L0680.015]
- A phone number in the footer is expected on a customer-facing store, but it can be left out of a store whose only purpose is contacting suppliers. Delete the placeholder number before saving in that case. [L1018.018]
- Leaving a placeholder phone number in the footer contact block is unnecessary for a store used only to contact suppliers. Remove it before saving. [L1018.008]
- Block 1 phone: delete the placeholder phone number, since a phone number is not needed when contacting suppliers. [L1018.005]
- Conflict on the footer phone number, by use:
  - Customer-facing store: include email and phone [L0377.019, L0377.028]
  - Supplier-contact-only store, or no phone line yet: optional, delete the placeholder [L0680.015, L1018.018, L1018.008]
- Contact block sample for the Source B theme (older, 2021-22): open the Rich text block in the footer, set the Heading (e.g. Get in touch), write a call to action such as "Get in touch with us today on any of the contact details below" in the Text field, then add the phone number, email in capitals and postal address on separate lines, save and check the live home page. [L0681.013]

### Footer block 2: store name menu

- Second footer menu block, procedure:
  1. Click the second footer menu block. [L0102.004]
  2. Set Title to your Store Name (example: Shaka Surfboards). [L0102.004]
  3. Set Menu to Footer menu. [L0102.004]
- Footer second column: replace the default heading with the store name and link it to a menu that holds Search, the main product category, New Arrivals and On Sale. Create this menu beforehand alongside the collections. [L0680.009]
- Conflict on this block: the procedure in [L0102.004] sets the menu to Footer menu, while [L0680.009] specifies the menu contents (Search, main product category, New Arrivals, On Sale). Use both: name the menu Footer menu and fill it with those items. [L0102.004, L0680.009]

### Footer block 3: Quick Links

- Third footer menu block, procedure:
  1. Click the third footer menu block. [L0102.005]
  2. Set Title to Quick Links. [L0102.005]
  3. Set Menu to Quick Links. [L0102.005]
  4. Include these links: About Us, Returns / Exchanges, Shipping, Privacy Policy, Terms of Service, Contact Us. [L0102.005]
- Footer third column: rename the default Main menu to Quick Links and link a menu containing About Us, Privacy Policy, Terms of Service and Contact Us. Create that menu before editing the footer. [L0680.010]
- Conflict on the quick links menu: [L0102.005] lists six links, including Returns / Exchanges and Shipping. [L0680.010] lists four, without them. Use the six-link list when those pages exist, and the four-link list as the minimum. [L0102.005, L0680.010]

### Footer block 4: newsletter signup

- Newsletter signup block in the footer, procedure:
  1. Click the Newsletter signup block in the footer. [L0102.006]
  2. Change the headline to something more engaging (examples: Join [Brand Name], Get Updates, Join Shaka Nation). [L0102.006]
  3. Keep it simple and brand-appropriate. [L0102.006]
  4. Save and refresh the storefront preview. [L0102.006]
- Footer fourth column (newsletter signup): keep the default layout and change only the headline to a short, catchy line. Write the headline yourself, in the same style as the example pattern described in the unit below. [L0680.011]
- Newsletter headline pattern: a short, playful, lowercase line that gives a reason to join and sounds like a community, not a sales pitch. [L0680.012]
  - Formula: [action verb] + [group or identity] + [small benefit or hook]. [L0680.012]
  - New examples in this pattern: join the surf crew and get the first drops; be first in line for new arrivals. [L0680.012]
  - Keep it under about six words. [L0680.012]
- Block 4 (newsletter signup): replace the default newsletter text with your own wording and click save. [L1018.005]

### Footer settings and styling

- Footer global settings, procedure (Click Footer in the Theme Customizer, not a block):
  1. Click Footer in Theme Customizer (not a block) to access global footer settings. [L0102.003]
  2. Set Heading size to Small. [L0102.003]
  3. Set Padding top to 40. [L0102.003]
  4. Set Padding bottom to 40. [L0102.003]
  5. Turn Payment icons ON. [L0102.003]
  6. Turn Social media icons OFF. [L0102.003]
- Footer color scheme: click through the available schemes and pick the one that matches the brand colors chosen earlier. The lesson used background two. [L0680.002]
- Footer padding: set the padding above and below the footer to 40 each. Changing it only moves the footer content further down, so only change it if you want finer control. [L0680.006]
- Padding conflict: the reference values in [L0102.003, L1018.006] set 40 top and 40 bottom as standard. [L0680.006] treats padding as a finer-control option. Apply 40 as the standard value and adjust only when the live store needs it. [L0102.003, L1018.006, L0680.006]
- Footer localization (country/region selector): leave it off unless the store sells in several countries or regions. Turn it on only in that case. [L0680.003]
- Language selector: in the Manhattan theme footer settings, the Language selector toggle sits next to the Localization (country/region) toggle and should stay off for a store that sells in one language and one market. Turn it on only when the store actually serves several languages. [L0680.018]
- Footer > Show payment icons: keep it enabled. Turning it off removes the payment icons from the footer, and turning it on brings them back. [L0680.004]
- Payment icons only appear after payment gateways are connected. Visa, MasterCard and American Express show up with connected gateways, and Apple Pay or Google Pay appear only if those are accepted. Older advice (2021-22). [L0681.003]
- Footer settings also include Mobile alignment (Left or Center), Show payment icons, Show language selector and Show currency selector. Older advice (2021-22). [L0681.003, L0370.026]
- Footer logo size: the image width is set as a percentage. The lesson went from 80% to 65% and finally to 50%, which looked best on the live store. Test a few sizes on the live store and pick the one that looks right, since the editor preview can overlap and looks different at full screen. [L0680.008]
- The footer logo should be the same file as the main header logo so the brand looks consistent. Use it with the image width set to 50%. [L1018.007]
- Copyright and small text: the copyright year in the footer updates automatically each year, so the year does not need manual changes. The text under the copyright line is set in the field Small footer text, where the default theme credit (theme developer name and Powered by Shopify) can be deleted. Older advice (2021-22). [L0681.005]
- Removing the Powered by Shopify and theme developer credit from the footer is a branding choice to keep only the store's own brand visible. Leaving Powered by Shopify is considered acceptable now because Shopify is widely known, but it is optional. Older advice (2021-22). [L0681.006]
- Keep the footer settings in Theme settings consistent with the color decisions above. [L0775.063]

### Footer text rule summary (checks before launch)

- Check every footer block on the live store, not only in the editor, against the real business details. [L0681.019, L0680.013]
- Keep social media off until accounts exist, and turn it on only with full URLs. [L0102.008, L0836.050, L1018.009]
- Keep the footer phone number only if the business has a phone line for suppliers or customers. [L0680.015, L1018.018]

### Hiding vs deleting sections

- Use hide instead of delete when customizing sections in the theme editor while building. Hide removes the section from view without deleting it, allowing you to undo or reconfigure later, while delete removes it permanently. [L0101.002]
- Each section can be hidden with the eye icon or removed with delete. [L0775.003]
- Hide the rich text section (not needed for supplier approval). [L0101.001]
- Hide the image banner until content exists, or add links before it goes live. [L0101.020, L0775.051]
- Hide blog posts until content exists. [L0775.055, L0101.001]
- Unused default Shopify sections, such as an empty video section or a promotions box with no content, should be removed from the homepage. [L0355.011]

### Links and dead ends

- Only add hyperlinks or button links to sections if the destination pages already exist, because broken links damage the appearance of a legitimate, professionally managed store. [L0101.004]
- Include a search bar and continue shopping button on your store. Customers who cannot easily search or navigate to other products abandon the site immediately. Older advice (2020). [L0245.074]

### Search bar

- On stores with many products, place a large search bar in the header of every page. It should be easy to find and large enough to use comfortably on desktop and mobile. Older advice (2021-22). [L0409.048]
- Moving the search bar in the theme requires editing theme code. Copy and paste the original code before editing so the change can be reversed, and preferably have a developer do it. Older advice (2022). [L0963.046]

### Cart page

- Customizing the cart page natively in Shopify (older advice, 2020):
  1. Open the Shopify admin. [L0664.033]
  2. Go to Online Store. [L0664.033]
  3. Select Cart from the drop-down menu. [L0664.033]
  4. Edit elements such as the logo, reviews or contact info. [L0664.033]

### Checkout branding

- Checkout customization options available without Shopify Plus include changing the checkout background, the logo and adding a banner at the top of the checkout. A banner with a phone number or support text can be effective in some industries, such as weight-loss products. [L0390.046]
- Shopify admin: Settings > Checkout > Editor > Header > Logo. Click Logo and upload the logo formatted with the Trustpilot badge, and it appears in the checkout header. [L0560.027]
- Shopify admin: Online Store > Themes > Edit default theme content. Search for checkout, then find the field Checkout alternative payment method banner and enter the low-stock message there. Save the change so it shows at checkout. [L0560.028]
- Settings > Checkout > Editor > Color palette: set the Main, Accent and Error colors. The Accent and buttons field controls the color of links, selections and buttons on the checkout page. [L0560.038]

### Mobile and preview checks

- Theme editor > mobile view: adjust colors and other mobile theme settings and check that the layout still looks clean. Deeper mobile changes require custom code or a developer. Older advice (2019). [L0663.023]
- Mobile logo position is now set natively in the header settings, separately from the desktop position. Always save, then open the store on a phone to check the result. [L0775.032]
- Preview the slideshow on a phone after making changes, and save only after the mobile view looks right. [L0775.039]
- Refresh the homepage, scroll through, confirm the store looks legitimate, has no dead links, has consistent branding, and has clean supplier-ready sections. [L0101.001]

### Custom code, developers and page builders

- Avoid editing theme code unless a change truly cannot be made in the Customizer. [L0595.003]
- Shopify sanitizes raw HTML, so when the theme editor cannot produce a custom page, a Shopify specialist on Upwork can build the fix for a one-time fee. Third-party landing page builders are usually monthly subscriptions, and some cost more than Shopify itself. Older advice (2024 point of view, applies to the developer choice). [L0666.060]
- Native editor versus code: the older mobile and header advice required code [L0663.023, L0852.089], while the current editor moves elements natively [L0709.007, L0775.032]. Start with the editor and use code or a developer only for changes it cannot make. [L0595.002]

### Floating widget (Source E course)

- Add the floating widget to the Shopify homepage:
  1. In the Shopify admin, go to Online Store > Themes and click Customize. [L0959.006]
  2. Open the homepage and click Add section. [L0959.006]
  3. Search for Custom Liquid or HTML (the name depends on the theme) and add the section. [L0959.006]
  4. Set Section padding for Top padding and Bottom padding to 0px. [L0959.006]
  5. Make the section background invisible (use the section settings in the theme editor). [L0959.006]
  6. Paste the widget code into the Custom Liquid field. [L0959.006]
  7. Click Save, then refresh the storefront to see the floating widget. [L0959.006]
- The section name differs between themes: some call it Custom Liquid, others HTML. If you cannot find the Custom Liquid section, search for HTML in the section list instead. [L0959.010]

### Theme editor workflow

#### Opening and saving the editor

- Open the theme editor through Online Store > Themes > Customize on the active theme. [L1371.001, L1265.002]
- Homepage content is built from sections that can be added, removed and reordered in the editor. [L1371.001, L1371.002]
- Store changes are kept only after you click Save. Unsaved edits are lost when you leave the editor. [L1371.001, L1309.010, L1371.033]
- Save after every change or set of changes, and before moving to another settings area. The preview updates in real time, but only Save keeps the edit. [L1309.010, L1371.033, L1371.043]
- Before you edit, check that the dropdown at the top reads Homepage. Theme settings and section edits for the homepage must be made on the Homepage template. [L1309.004, L1309.001]
- The customizer shows the homepage preview on the left and the editing sidebar on the right. Hovering over a section highlights it on the page, and clicking it opens its settings. [L1309.006]

#### Preview and device checks

- Always switch the editor to mobile preview before saving. A page can look finished on desktop while the mobile layout is broken. [L1188.047, L1219.021]
- Check the header and footer separately in each view, because their layouts differ between desktop and mobile. [L1188.047]
- When testing a store, prioritise the mobile layout over the desktop layout. If a banner or section does not fit, adjust it in the page builder instead of forcing the same image. [L1219.021]
- Device preview steps:
  1. Click the device preview button.
  2. Choose Mobile, Desktop or Full screen from the dropdown.
  3. Select the same logo image for mobile if needed, then adjust the mobile logo size separately. [L1371.037]
- Mobile traffic is most of the total, so check the header on mobile. [L1371.037]
- The Ask for changes button accepts a plain-language request to edit the current section and suits small layout changes. Review the result in the preview before saving, because the change can affect more than the block you meant. [L1188.051]

#### Adding, removing and reordering sections

- Shopify themes build the homepage from sections, and each section adds a specific block such as a slideshow, featured collection or grid. The default demo sections are placeholders, and most should be removed for a new store. [L1371.002]
- To add a homepage section:
  1. Click Add section.
  2. Choose the section type, for example Featured product.
  3. Select the new section, add the content, and choose the product or details it should show.
  New sections are added at the bottom of the page first. [L1371.003]
- To remove a homepage section:
  1. Click into the section.
  2. Click the three dots at the top of the section panel.
  3. Choose Remove section. [L1371.004]
- To change section order, click and hold the six-dot handle of a section and drag it to a new position. [L1309.007]
- In the Manhattan theme, the Add section option is available on every template and offers the same section types as the homepage. Each one can be previewed before it is added. [L1450.017]
- In the Manhattan theme, any section available on the homepage can also be added to a page template through Add section. This lets a page carry more than rich text. [L1265.010]

#### Global theme settings

- Logo: go to Theme settings > Logo > Select image, choose the uploaded logo from the Files list, and click Done. The Desktop logo width defaults to 200 and should only be changed if the file is much larger or smaller. [L1309.011]
- Favicon: go to Theme settings > Favicon > Select image. The favicon replaces the default Shopify icon in the browser tab and should be a small version of the logo or a symbol for the store. [L1309.012]
- Layout width: the default is 1200 px and can be raised to a maximum of 1600 px so the site looks wider on large monitors. [L1309.013]
- Buttons: border thickness, opacity, corner radius and transparency of all buttons are set in Theme settings > Buttons. The defaults are fine for most stores. [L1309.015]
- Variant pills, Inputs, Product cards and Collection cards sit next to Buttons. For a supplier-stage demo they can stay at their defaults and be styled later. [L1309.069]
- Theme settings order for an early-stage high-ticket dropshipping store (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice):
  1. Layout: change the header layout from Minimal to Traditional.
  2. Colors: enter the three brand colors and keep the page background white.
  3. Typography: set the base font size to 18 px, Body font to Helvetica, and the heading font to one simple, readable font.
  4. Icons: change the cart icon from Bag to Shopping cart if preferred.
  5. Products: set Sale badge style to Save $10, Option style to Selectable boxes, Display stock levels to Hidden, Quick shop button to Disabled, Add to cart button to Enable on desktop only, Show dynamic checkout button on, and enable color swatches.
  6. Social media: leave until launch, then add Facebook, Pinterest, email address and Instagram.
  7. Favicon: upload the favicon PNG.
  8. Cart: no change needed.
  9. Custom styles and scripts and Advanced: leave untouched.
  10. Performance: keep the preset defaults.
  11. Checkout: upload the logo as a custom image, set logo size to Medium, change the button color to match the site buttons and the accent color to the main brand color, and leave error messages red.
  12. Click Save before leaving the editor or moving to another settings area. [L1371.043]

### Header

#### Header layout and settings

- Header settings include Logo position, Menu type, Header borders and a Top Border Style option that can use the theme color or a global section gradient. [L1188.003]
- Desktop and mobile font sizes are set separately in the theme settings, so check both views. [L1188.003]
- Sticky header set to always on keeps the logo, menu and contact information visible while the visitor scrolls. An option also shrinks the logo on scroll. [L1309.016]
- Desktop logo position: top left is the default. Other options are top center, middle left and middle center, which rearranges the header elements. [L1309.017]
- Separator line: a thin line under the header, on by default, can be turned off. [L1309.019]
- Menu font size 14 and font weight 500 are the typical values. Change the size only if the chosen font looks too small or too large. [L1309.020]
- Contact details: set opening hours for customer service, and add a phone number and email address once they exist. An email alone is acceptable at the supplier stage. [L1309.021]
- Mobile layout: logo position can be set separately for phones, for example center or left. Preview on a phone after saving. [L1309.023]
- Menu field: the default is Main menu. To use another navigation menu, click Change, then Change menu, and select a menu created earlier in Shopify. [L1309.062]
- The header should contain the brand logo, the main menu links, the search function and the cart. Shopify adds search and cart to the header by default, so for a new store you only need to select the menu to show. Customer login or account links are optional. [L1326.006]
- Make the logo smaller and place it on the left side of the navigation bar. Visitors care more about the products than the logo, so a large logo wastes space. [L1325.009]

#### Header layout and mobile search (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice)

- Set the header layout in theme settings under Layout to Traditional instead of Minimal, because only the Traditional header shows a search bar. Changing the layout means header section settings are no longer applied, so check the header after switching. [L1371.007]
- In the Traditional header layout, the search bar stays visible on mobile in this theme, which many themes do not do. Check on the mobile preview that the search bar and logo still fit the header. [L1371.048]
- Enable the sticky header in the static header settings, select the logo image in the header so it displays, and adjust the logo height so it is readable without being too large or too small. [L1371.036]
- Enable the utility bar in theme settings and add a top bar menu to it. Use a background matching the footer, white link color and yellow link hover color. A secondary menu can be placed on the right side. [L1371.034]

### Announcement bar and promo bar

- Edit the announcement bar under Online Store > Themes > Customize by clicking the announcement bar section. Change the message text there and add an icon such as a shipping symbol if needed. [L1326.004]
- The header can carry a scrolling promo bar for savings or shipping lines, plus a top row with Track Your Order and Support links and a country and currency selector. [L1188.053]
- Set the currency selector so buyers see prices in the currency they will pay in. [L1188.053]
- Hide the announcement bar until it is needed. [L1309.001]
- Older advice (2021-2022) states that in a Shopify theme the announcement bar is fixed and cannot be removed. The Manhattan setup above hides it instead. Use hiding rather than deleting if you are unsure. [L1371.005, L1309.001]

### Slideshow and hero banner

#### Hero banner principles

- A homepage banner should show the customer's outcome or passion, so a visitor immediately sees they are in the right place. Its main job is to state what the business does for the customer, and a temporary promotion is a secondary use. [L1310.001]
- A hero banner needs four elements: a title, a subheader, social proof such as review counts or numbers of items saved, and a call to action. A missing call to action is one of the most serious gaps. [L1225.025]
- Homepage banner pattern:
  > [Store name] introducing [top-rated or number one rated] [product] for [customer type] in [country], paired with a benefit-driven image, followed by a short section on why the product helps and what the store stands for. It works because the visitor instantly sees the brand, the product and a clear benefit.
  Example one: Lumora introducing the top rated travel pillow for frequent flyers in Germany.
  Example two: Brinelo introducing the best rated desk mat for home offices in the UK. [L1217.040]
- Homepage structure for a niche store begins with a banner that matches the feeling of the exact customer. Most traffic arrives via ads directly to product pages, so keep the page clean and branded. [L1158.028]

#### Slideshow settings (Manhattan and Superstore themes)

- Slideshow layout: full width (used in the example) or grid. Slide height set to Adapt to first image so the image is sized correctly. Options small, medium and large exist. [L1309.025]
- Pagination can be dots or numbers. Auto rotate can change slides every 3, 5 or 9 seconds. Set mobile content to show below images, and give the slideshow a short accessibility description. [L1309.026]
- Slide content: heading and subheading go on top of the image, the button label is Shop, and the button link points to a collection. Desktop content position bottom right and alignment right were used in the example. [L1309.027]
- Slideshow content settings let you set the button colour and the button text colour separately. A container background can be shown behind the text on desktop. Choose a button text colour that contrasts with the button colour so the label stays readable. [L1309.071]
- Slideshow section margins add space above or below the whole section. Use them to adjust the gap between the slideshow and the sections around it. [L1309.067]
- Keep one slide for the demo store. Delete the blank default slides and add more with Add slide later for new suppliers or sales events. [L1309.028]
- Buttons in image banners and slideshows look faded or nearly transparent while they have no link. Their full colour shows only after a real destination, such as a collection, is set in the button link field. [L1309.063]
- Slideshow settings in the Shopify theme editor include Enable slideshow, Autoplay slideshow, Autoplay slide duration and Slideshow height. The image field recommends 1800 x 1000 px. (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.017]
- A slide's BUTTON 1 has the fields Label, Button, Link and Style, and Style is set to Primary. Clearing the Label field hides the button. The same panel holds the Heading, Subheading, Text color and Text alignment (Left) fields. (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.037]
- Older advice (2021-2022) states that homepage sliders should not be used. JavaScript slows the page, and the cited figure says about 96% of customers never see the second slide. A single hero image at the top of the homepage is enough. [L1266.023]
- Older advice (2021-2022) for the early-stage store: the slideshow is used as a single static hero image, which is fixed in the section list. [L1371.006]
- Conflict: the older no-slider advice [L1266.023] and the single-static-hero advice [L1371.006] both point toward one image, while the Manhattan setup keeps a slideshow with one slide [L1309.028]. A one-slide slideshow matches both; a multi-slide rotation is what the older advice warns against. [L1266.023, L1371.006, L1309.028]
- Four small images side by side on desktop should stack vertically on mobile. On a phone they become too small to read and can trigger Search Console accessibility errors. Keep the stacking instead of forcing a side-by-side layout on mobile. [L1287.024]

### Collection lists and featured collections

#### Collection list

- Collection list settings (Manhattan theme): heading "Shop our featured collections" in a small heading size, image ratio set to Adapt to image so mixed image sizes fit, and desktop columns set to 3. [L1309.029]
- Text overlay on, placed bottom left, overlay colour teal from the brand palette, text white. Enable view all lets mobile visitors swipe through extra collections. [L1309.030]
- Adding collections:
  1. Click Collection and choose a collection with Select.
  2. Click Add collection to create a new row for more items.
  3. Repeat until all collections are set, then Save.
  The example used six collections in rows of three. [L1309.031]
- Superstore theme setup (2021-2022, older advice):
  1. Add the Collection list section and set the heading to Our Featured Collections.
  2. Set Desktop section spacing to 100 px (the maximum) and Mobile section spacing to 60 px.
  3. Keep four collections per row and delete any extra blocks.
  4. For each block, click Change, select a collection that has products, and click Select so its product image loads automatically.
  5. If a collection has no products, upload a custom image of a similar product type. Otherwise it shows a blank image.
  6. The collection title fills in automatically. Overwrite it with a custom title if needed.
  7. Click Save. [L1310.028]
- A collection without products shows a blank image in the featured collections section. Use a custom image as a temporary placeholder and replace it with real supplier products when the store goes live. (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.029]
- A simple homepage can feature the newest collection and a section with trending products. These two sections give a first-time visitor a clear starting point. [L1325.016]

#### Featured collection

- Featured collection (Manhattan theme): change the heading to a short phrase such as What's trending, select a collection such as Bestsellers, and show 4 products in 4 columns. Product card options include secondary image, vendor and rating. [L1309.033]
- Quick view shows a button that opens product details in place with an add-to-cart option. If turned off, the button leads straight to the product page. [L1309.034]
- Sale badge: the product card settings include a sale badge position, a sale badge colour and an editable badge text. The badge defaults to a dark gray background and can be switched to a theme accent colour such as the pink accent. [L1309.065]
- Keep featured collections linked only to collections that exist and contain products. [L1310.029, L1309.009]
- The Featured collection section in the Superstore theme started with a beige background set by the theme. A white background is acceptable, and a light contrasting background can break up a long white homepage. Either can be changed in section settings. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.042]

### Image banners and image-with-text sections

- Image banner (Manhattan theme): set the image as background, banner height Adapt to first image, desktop position middle center, container turned off so text sits directly on the image, and buttons linked to a collection once it exists. [L1309.035]
- Image banner > Image behavior: None keeps the image still. Ambient movement makes the image move very slowly, which draws the eye to the banner. [L1309.036]
- Highlights banner (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice): desktop section spacing starts at 36 px and mobile at 20 px, with banner text centered. The tested build raised desktop spacing to 60 px. [L1310.039]
- Create the destination pages first, such as Shipping, Contact us, Returns and Price match guarantee, before building Highlights banner blocks. Each block link must point to an existing store page. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.038]
- Shrine theme Image with text settings: Heading, Text, Link button, Image or Video, Media height (Adapt to media), Color schemes (Content color scheme and Section color), Desktop layout (Full page width) and Remove section. Use autoplay mp4 video that is muted and looping instead of GIFs or animated WEBPs. [L1331.025]
- Icon Bar: each icon uses the Check available icons search, so search for a soft word like pillow first. If no icon matches, scroll to the custom icon option and paste the icon code to replace the icon. [L1331.026]
- Shrine sections such as Image with text have a Display section ID field. Paste that ID into any button link so the button scrolls to the section, or use it to merge two sections. [L1331.042]
- Logo list for shipping carriers as proof: go to Online store, open Customize for the theme, click Add section, search for Logo list and add it, then upload the logos. You can delete the text to make it smaller, change the size, background and padding, and drag it to any position. [L1287.025]
- Logo lists can show well-known customers. This is useful for business-to-business products. [L1265.008]

### Pre-footer trust columns

- Each pre-footer column has an image, a header, an optional description and a link. The example used a gray background, changed to background 1 (white) for a cleaner look. [L1309.037]
- Pre-footer trust column pattern: each column pairs an icon image with a short benefit header that tells the visitor why the store is reliable, such as shipping threshold, origin or checkout security.
  Example 1: Free shipping over 75 dollars.
  Example 2: Secure payment at checkout. [L1309.059]
- Security cues such as trust badges belong on the cart and checkout pages as well. They reassure shoppers at the point where they decide to pay. [L1522.021]
- Homepage section for a single-niche store includes trust signals and a risk-free message. [L1363.055]

### Footer

#### Footer structure and content

- Footer structure in the theme editor: Brand description, Text column (contact information), Useful links column, and Legal links column with Privacy Policy, Shipping policy, Refund policy, Terms of service and Contact information. Upload a footer logo from the library and link each policy page. [L1188.042]
- Add footer text by opening the footer section, clicking Add block, and choosing Text. Write the About Us text with the brand mission, email address and business address, then click Save. [L1326.013]
- Add a footer logo by clicking Add block in the footer section and choosing the Image block. Upload the logo, increase its size so it is clearly visible, and click Save. [L1326.016]
- The footer should contain a customer care section with the links visitors look for, plus an about section. Visitors who land on a product page see the navigation first and scroll to the footer next, so it must support them. [L1325.022]
- The footer should contain a get-in-touch section, support hours, support email addresses, a live chat link and links to social media pages. Footer links to unrelated pages make the store look untrustworthy. [L1346.011]
- Footer editing is done in the same customizer sidebar and is a separate step. Cookie consent is a pop-up set in the same sidebar and can be switched on or off. [L1309.039]
- Do not link any section or footer item to a page that does not exist yet. Suppliers clicking dead links see a broken store, so leave link fields empty until the target page is created. [L1309.009]
- Repeat the Highlights banner near the bottom of the homepage with a different background color than the footer, so two identical colors do not sit next to each other. Use a second color from the brand palette. (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice.) [L1310.035]

### Pages and blog (Manhattan theme)

#### Editing a page template

- Open the theme editor, click the template dropdown at the top, switch from home page to Pages, and select the default page (the About Us page) to edit it. A separate Contact page template also exists. [L1265.002]
- In each page's settings in the Shopify admin, adjust padding, margin and width (full width or narrower, centered). [L1265.002]
- Each page type has its own template in the theme editor. To give a page different sections than the default page or the contact page, create an additional page template instead of changing the existing ones. [L1265.009]
- Add a logo list with a heading such as "Brands we've worked with" by clicking Add section and adding logos one by one. [L1265.002]

#### Blog listing and posts

- Make sure blog posts are published and visible, because hidden posts do not show in the listing. [L1265.002]
- Blog listing settings: choose Grid or Collage layout. Collage shows the newest post at the top, while Grid shows posts evenly. Featured images are recommended to be shown, and image height can be set. Show or hide date and author, and set card or standard layout in theme settings. [L1265.002, L1265.004]
- Card or standard layout and the background colour under post titles are set in the theme settings and are separate from the grid or collage choice. [L1265.011]
- Blog posts template: adjust padding, margin, page width, featured image size (Adapt to image keeps the uploaded size), title, date and author visibility, and the share link. [L1265.002]
- With Adapt to image, the featured image becomes full size based on the uploaded image size. Keep the page width unchanged so it matches the rest of the site. [L1265.003]
- Keep the blog post page width at the theme default. A narrower width makes the post text noticeably less wide on large monitors and stops it from matching the rest of the site. [L1265.013]
- Blog post pages show a share link by default that lets readers copy the post URL. Keeping it visible is recommended. Hiding it is possible but not advised. [L1265.012]
- Add an Email signup section to the blog posts template: enter a heading, click Save, optionally change the accent color, then Save again. [L1265.002]
- Hide Rich text and Blog posts sections in the first version of a store with no content yet. Enable them later when content exists. [L1309.038, L1309.001]

### Cart page

#### Manhattan theme cart setup

- To edit the cart, open the Theme Customizer, click the page dropdown at the top and scroll to the cart entry. The checkout entry is in the same dropdown but cannot be edited; only the cart can be changed. [L1450.001]
- Customers reach the cart either by clicking the cart icon after adding a product or by being sent straight to the cart, depending on the add-to-cart behaviour in the product page settings. The cart page must therefore work as a standalone step before checkout. [L1450.002]
- The default cart page keeps the header, the mega menu built earlier and the footer. These global elements can stay without changes. [L1450.003]
- Items section: shows the products the customer has added. Its padding and margin controls move the content up or down. No changes are needed in a default setup. [L1450.004]
- Subtotal section: shows the subtotal, the checkout button and the order special instructions field. Its padding and margin can be adjusted the same way as the Items section. [L1450.005]
- Cart note: in the cart's theme settings it can be enabled or disabled. When disabled, the order special instructions text disappears from the cart page after saving and reloading the store. [L1450.006]
- Keep the cart note enabled. Customers sometimes write notes there, and these are saved to the Shopify order information. Disabling it removes a channel for useful customer requests. [L1450.007]
- Do not modify or hide the checkout button, because customers with items in the cart must always be able to proceed to checkout. [L1450.008]
- Additional blocks such as a countdown timer or a coupon are added inside the cart template through the add block option. Each block is placed by moving it in the customizer, and its colours and text are set in its own settings. [L1450.010]
- Adding extra content such as rich text sections to the cart page is usually unnecessary and can distract buyers from checkout. Add sections only when there is a clear reason. [L1450.018]
- Cart page setup order:
  1. Open the Theme Customizer and select cart in the page dropdown.
  2. Check the Items and Subtotal sections and adjust padding or margin only if spacing needs fixing.
  3. Open Theme settings and keep the cart note enabled.
  4. Leave the checkout button unchanged.
  5. Add any optional blocks such as the urgency timer or the coupon only when they are needed.
  6. Click save and reload the storefront to check the result. [L1450.009]

#### Cart page content and checks

- Check the cart page by adding a product from the storefront and opening View cart, because the cart page is empty until a product is added. Text on the cart page can be changed through the theme's default content settings. [L1326.021]
- Optimise the cart page, which many stores neglect, because it is where shoppers start to doubt the purchase. Add USP messaging, shipping details and upsells there, presented the same way as on the product page. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1522.019]
- Reconfirm unusual shipping requirements on the cart page as well as on the product page. Upsells that some shoppers missed on the product page can also be shown on the cart page. [L1522.020]
- Cart in the Superstore theme needed no change in the early-stage settings order. [L1371.043]

#### Smart Cart (Source E, 2025-2026)

- Layout choice at creation is Single Column or Double Column. A Double Column cart gives cart line items a dedicated area, which suits carts with long item lists such as parts and accessories. The layout can be switched later without starting over. [L1231.011]
- Advanced settings pull typography from the Shopify theme's typography settings. The title heading level can be changed, for example from H1 to H2, to get the wanted font without custom CSS. The cart title can also be hidden entirely, for example to place a store logo with custom CSS. [L1231.014]
- The country field in Smart Cart v2 suggests matching countries as you type, so merchants do not need to know the two-letter ISO code. In v1 this code had to be typed in directly. [L1231.029]
- The order notes component collects a gift message or special instructions. These notes appear in the order attribution section on the right side at the top of the Shopify order, not under the line items. [L1231.052]
- The cart subtotal has singular and plural label fields, and the item count can be inserted with a variable. Keep the subtotal label simple, since heavy changes are rarely needed. [L1231.054]
- The Express checkout block in the Smart Cart editor is only a placeholder and does not appear as text on the live store. The buttons that show depend on the accelerated checkout methods connected in the Shopify admin and the express checkout preferences set there. They can display as separate buttons in a row instead of one combined block. [L1231.061]

### Homepage structure and order of work

#### Principles

- The homepage is the first page visitors see on the root domain and the hub of the site. It should introduce the business, state what is good about it, give reasons to buy, and introduce the products sold. It connects to all other pages through the main menu, the bottom navigation and the search bar. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1463.003]
- In high-ticket dropshipping the reseller sells the same products as other sites, so the homepage must sell the business as well as the products. Customers compare where to buy as well as what to buy. At launch the competition is on identical products; a brand's own products come later. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1463.002]
- A weak store design holds back sales even when ads perform. Improve the store before running ads, with better images and a clear layout. [L1439.039]
- Homepage imagery must match the store's product type. Show people using the products and link to the category collections of that product type. [L1346.008]
- A homepage that mixes lifestyle photos unrelated to the products confuses visitors and makes the store look incoherent. [L1346.009]
- Shopify core components are the announcement bar, header, footer and the main page types (home, product, collection, cart, blog and checkout). Set up these components before adding more content. [L1326.001]
- The home page can stay simple with two or three sections: a banner related to the current product, the product itself, and a benefits section with badges such as priority shipping and money-back guarantee. [L1412.049]

#### Homepage structures by store type

- Niche store (procedure, as the lesson describes it):
  1. A banner that matches the feeling of the exact customer.
  2. An About Us or mission section telling the brand story.
  3. One or two sections for the main product.
  4. A collection of products.
  Keep it clean and branded, since most traffic arrives via ads directly to product pages. [L1158.028]
- Single-niche store, in this order:
  1. Headline with the offer and a big benefit, such as a percentage discount.
  2. Striking product visuals.
  3. Review badge that links to the reviews section.
  4. One customer testimonial.
  5. More product images.
  6. A best-sellers section with a link to each product the visitor can choose.
  7. Trust signals and a risk-free message.
  8. Benefit copy describing what the product does for the customer. [L1363.055]
- Tested Superstore theme homepage section order (2021-2022, older advice):
  1. Utility bar
  2. Header
  3. Slideshow
  4. Highlights banner
  5. Featured collections (Collection list)
  6. Image with text overlay
  7. Featured collection (product grid)
  8. Search
  9. Highlights banner (repeated near the bottom)
  10. Footer [L1310.034]
- Early-stage high-ticket dropshipping store section list (2021-2022, older advice):
  1. Announcement bar (fixed).
  2. Utility bar (fixed).
  3. Header (fixed).
  4. Slideshow used as a single static hero image (fixed).
  5. Highlights banner.
  6. Collection list.
  7. Image with text overlay.
  8. Featured collection.
  9. Footer (fixed).
  All other default sections such as Grid, Rich Text and Testimonials can be removed. [L1371.006]
- Conflict between structures: the tested Superstore order has 10 positions and the niche procedure has 4 (plus banner). The early-stage order has 9. Use the niche structure for a single-product ad landing store [L1158.028, L1363.055] and the Superstore order as an example of a full multi-section homepage [L1310.034]; the older 2021-2022 orders are examples, not requirements. [L1310.034, L1371.006, L1158.028]
- Start a new Shopify store's public home page from the basic template, which has an Image with text overlay section and no images or design. It is changed from the storefront preview using the Edit home page option, as part of the design work after account setup. [L1314.022]

#### Supplier-ready demo store setup (Manhattan theme)

- Time: a full homepage customization of a new Shopify theme takes about one hour when the brand images are already finished. [L1309.002]
- At the supplier-approval stage the homepage only needs to look credible and work. Advanced mega menu, product card and blog settings can wait until the store is live. [L1309.003]
- Procedure:
  1. Upload all prepared images in one batch under Content > Files > Upload files, select them all and click Open.
  2. Go to Online Store > Themes and confirm the Manhattan theme is active.
  3. Click Customize and check that the dropdown at the top reads Homepage.
  4. Open Theme settings (gear icon) and set logo, favicon, colors, typography, layout, buttons, social media and contact details.
  5. Open Header and set sticky header, logo position, menu, menu type, separator line, menu fonts and navigation colors.
  6. Edit the Slideshow: keep one slide, add image and text, link the button to a collection.
  7. Edit the Collection list: heading, columns, overlay, then add collections and their images.
  8. Edit the Featured collection, Image banner (only if needed), Email signup and Pre-footer.
  9. Hide Rich text, Blog posts and the Announcement bar until they are needed.
  10. Edit the footer and cookie consent, click Save after each section and check the homepage on desktop and on a phone. [L1309.001]
- Uploading images to Content > Files does not link them to any section. Each image still has to be selected inside its section; the upload only makes them available in the picker. [L1309.005]
- Imagery and sections should be finished in a supplier-approval demo as credible placeholders; the full store can be refined after launch. [L1309.003]

#### Build order for the initial site (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice)

1. Set up the homepage with the business message and links to the main sections.
2. Set up the collection page template once so every collection uses the same settings.
3. Set up the product page template once so every product uses the same settings.
4. Create all single pages (About Us, Contact Us, policies, terms) and link them into the navigation.
5. Leave the blog untouched until after the business has launched.
When steps 1 to 4 are done, the site is ready for the next stage. [L1463.014]

### Social links

- Add social media links in theme settings, next to the logo, colors and contact details. Social media is part of the Supplier-ready demo procedure at step 4. [L1309.001]
- Leave social media until launch, then add Facebook, Pinterest, email address and Instagram. (2021-2022, older advice.) [L1371.043]
- The footer should link to social media pages, and links to unrelated pages make the store look untrustworthy. [L1346.011]

### Hiding vs deleting sections

- Deleting or hiding a section is reversible in the customizer, but deleting removes its settings. For a first version it is safer to hide sections with the eye icon than to delete them. [L1309.008]
- While building a theme, hide a section you are unsure about with its visibility toggle instead of deleting it, so you can restore it without rebuilding. Delete a section only when you are sure you will not need it. (Shrine theme.) [L1331.044]
- Hide Rich text and Blog posts in the first version of a store with no content yet, and enable them later when content exists. [L1309.038]
- Removing a section: click into it, click the three dots at the top of the panel, and choose Remove section. [L1371.004]
- In a Shopify theme the header, announcement bar, utility bar and slideshow sections are fixed and cannot be removed, and the footer is always part of the site. Only the optional sections in between can be removed. (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice.) [L1371.005]
- The Shrine theme editor's Add section panel has a Search sections box that filters the whole list of section groups. A section can be found by typing its name instead of scrolling through the Header, Footer, Sections, Others and Apps groups. [L1330.028]

Sections available in the Shrine Pro theme editor, grouped as shown in Customize > Add section:

| Group | Section names |
|---|---|
| Header | Horizontal Ticker, Announcement bar, Header |
| Cart drawer | Cart drawer |
| Home page template | Slideshow, Comparison table, Icon Bar, Results, Contact Form, Instagram stories, Promo Pop-Up, Scroll to Top Button, Global Music Player |
| Footer | Section divider, Footer |
| Sections (main list) | Featured product, Collection list, Rich text, Image with text, Image banner, Slideshow, Collage, Multicolumn, Multirow, Collapsible content |
| Others | Email signup, Contact form, Video, Blog posts |
| Apps | 3D Parallax hero, Announcement bar, Before & After slider, Bundle deals, Comparison table, Content tabs, Custom Columns V2, Facebook testimonials, Featured on, Horizontal Ticker, Icons with content, Image/Video Slider, Instagram stories, Music Player, OLD Custom columns, Page scroll progress, Custom Liquid, Page |
[L1330.016]

### Checkout branding

#### What can be changed

- The Shopify checkout cannot be fully redesigned because Shopify controls it for security, using 256-bit encryption. Store owners can only add a custom logo, a background image of about 1000 x 400 px, and some color and button changes. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1371.031]
- Shopify Plus (about 2,500 USD per month) is required to customize the checkout. Otherwise the checkout is the same for all Shopify stores. Within that limit, upload the brand font in Shopify so it appears at checkout, set the logo in the checkout settings, and use a logo with trust signals such as free shipping or a money-back guarantee. [L1363.030]
- Conflict on access: one source says full checkout customization requires Shopify Plus [L1363.030], while another describes adding a checkout banner under Settings > Checkout without naming a plan [L1259.034, L1260.041]. Check your plan before you promise a custom checkout.
- Conflict on size: the checkout banner is 1500 × 300 px [L1259.034, L1260.041], while the older source gives a background image of about 1000 × 400 px [L1371.031]. Use 1500 × 300 px for the checkout banner under Settings > Checkout; use the 1000 × 400 px background only where the older Superstore-era guidance applies. [L1259.034, L1260.041, L1371.031]

#### Checkout banner and trust elements

- Shopify checkout image: go to Settings > Checkout and add an image (checkout banner). Recommended size is 1500 × 300 px. Create it in Canva with custom size 1500 × 300, add the logo, and add trust badges such as Privacy Protected, Secure Checkout and Satisfaction Guaranteed from Canva's Elements section. Add payment icons (Visa, Mastercard, PayPal) and optionally a 30-day money-back guarantee. [L1259.034, L1260.041]
- Case: a store that changed nothing except adding checkout trust elements (secure checkout, guarantee, payment logos) moved from a conversion rate of 0.29% to 2.15% in one day, with an Add to Cart rate around 8%. [L1260.042]

#### Checkout settings procedure (Superstore theme, 2021-2022, older advice)

1. Open Checkout in the theme settings.
2. Upload the logo as a custom image, set it to Medium size, and place it in the center.
3. Leave error messages red.
4. Change the button color to match the site buttons and the accent color to the main brand color.
5. Click Save before leaving the editor, because unsaved changes are lost. [L1371.032]

## Demo store for supplier approval

### What a supplier-ready demo store is

- A demo store is a fully functional Shopify storefront with placeholder products. It shows that the business is supplier-ready without being connected to approved suppliers or accepting real customer orders. It becomes a live store later, when the demo products are replaced with approved supplier products. [L0069.001]
- It has a complete layout and design, populated with a small number of representative demo products. Suppliers review it to picture how their own products would look before they approve real uploads. [L0245.027]
- It is built to be supplier-ready and looks and behaves like a live store. The finished design is used to show brands when applying to become an authorized retailer. [L1062.001]
- Older (2019): It should be built exactly like the permanent store, with the same design and pages. The only difference is that the real products stay unpublished until the supplier approves. [L0356.022]
- The store built during setup is the same store that will later take real sales, not a separate test site. Only the content differs, because no suppliers or real products exist yet. [L1061.005]
- Before suppliers are contacted, fill the store with demo products instead of real supplier products. The store should look as it will when it sells, so suppliers can picture their own products on it. [L1061.006]
- Examples: ShakaSurfboards.com (surf niche) and Happy Paws (pet supply niche) are demo stores built to a supplier-ready standard. Both are design showcases that resemble a real store. [L0069.007, L1062.004]
- A supplier-facing store should look exactly like a live store, the only difference being that the products are demo items. It must not look half-built. [L0767.003]
- Suppliers review the store before approval, so it should look complete even with only a few products. It should show what the store will look like once the supplier's products are uploaded. [L0467.041]
- Suppliers apply the same skepticism a future customer would. A store that looks untrustworthy fails both audiences. Trust signals are therefore the core criteria. [L0767.009]
- Older (2019): Suppliers judge a retailer partly on how the store looks, so store quality matters before the first sale. A professional look raises the chance of approval. [L1086.061]
- Older (2019): The store should be lean but credible: sample collections and sample products that match the target niche, clean, not a large store with many pages or products. [L0960.004]
- Older (2019): Build it lean but good-looking, without many extra content pages or a large product upload. Add the extras after vendor approval. The goal before approval is a credible, functional store, not a finished one. [L0960.005]
- Minimum set for credibility: a working logo, favicon, colors, fonts, a single slide, a few collections, a pre-footer and an email signup. Defer announcement bars, mega menu details, blog content, buyer guides and advanced product card settings. [L0775.061]
- The store must look relevant to its niche before outreach. Add products from the chosen niche, even placeholders, so the brand sees a store that matches its category. [L0468.061]
- Older (2020): Use the niche-specific demo products and stock sources described below. [L0468.061]
- Build a new demo store for each niche, even if you already own other stores. Mention your existing experience when contacting suppliers, and say the new store goes live on a specific date. [L0934.090]
- A demo store can stay small while suppliers are contacted, for example three collections with three products each. It does not need rebuilding when the niche changes. [L0247.083]
- Build the store to its final look before contacting suppliers, so they can picture how their products will appear. Finish the build first, then start outreach. [L0941.036]
- Timing: the store is built before suppliers are contacted. Demo products are uploaded so suppliers can review it during approval. [L0969.086, L0506.004]
- Before applying to silver suppliers, build the demo store so the supplier can see a working, professional storefront when it reviews the application. [L0486.008]
- Have a live, high-quality demo store before contacting suppliers, so they can see how their products would look on your site. [L1422.017]
- Supplier outreach comes only after the supplier-ready demo store is complete. [L1062.002]
- At the demo stage, no real products are uploaded and no supplier approval is needed yet. Demo products fill the catalog so the store looks realistic, and they are replaced by live products when the store goes live. [L1062.002]
- Loading real products before a supplier has approved you is outside the demo stage. Use demo products in the catalog instead. [L1062.003]
- Store build sequence from the Source D course, with product validation first:
  1. Build the store with Elixir by following the step-by-step store build guide.
  2. Optimize the product page: images, copy, urgency elements and trust badges.
  3. Keep the design clean and trustworthy.
  4. Submit the store for review via ticket before it goes live.
  [L0491.015]
- Conflict on sequencing. Source D says not to build a store until a product has been approved, because building early wastes time on products that may not pass review. [L0491.014] The Source A and Source B units assume the product choice is made and say to build the demo store before supplier outreach. [L0506.004, L0969.086, L0771.031]
  - Applies: validate the product first (Source D). Once the product line is chosen, build the demo store before contacting suppliers.
- Store domain: the store must have its own domain name. Suppliers see this as a sign of professionalism and an established business. [L0123.002]
- The shop name and the website domain: a Shopify subdomain such as store-name.myshopify.com in outreach or on the store makes the business look unprofessional and untrustworthy. [L0767.005]

### How many demo products

| Figure | Source | Date | Context |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 to 20 | L0088.002, L0219.001, L0219.004 | not dated | Build before contacting suppliers. L0219.001: this lets suppliers visualize the brand and significantly increases the chance of becoming an authorized retailer. |
| About 30 minutes for 10 to 20 | L0088.003, L0219.005 | not dated | Time to create and upload 10 to 20 demo products. |
| 3 to 5 | L0123.003 | not dated | Shows the product types and that the store is operational without looking thin or cluttered. |
| 5 | L0243.030 | 2019, older | Five is enough before launch. Put the effort into About Us, shipping policy and return policy pages instead of many products. |
| About 5 | L0934.089, L0934.159 | 2023 | Credible for suppliers. Descriptions can be short. |
| 4 to 5 | L0962.036 | 2021, older | A demo store needs about four or five stock product pages to look complete. Collections need not be filled before launch. |
| 4 to 5 | L1085.038 | 2018, older | Pick 4 to 5 products, each with a royalty-free image. |
| 4 to 5 | L1275.031 | 2019, older | Upload about 4 to 5 demo products using stock images before approval. |
| 4 to 5 per collection | L0452.022 | not dated (Source B) | Four products took about 10 minutes. |
| 5 | L0468.062 | 2020, older | About five placeholder products from one niche. |
| 5 to 10 | L1068.040 | 2019, older | Enough to start contacting suppliers. |
| 5 to 10 | L0853.009 | 2021, older | Enough to show suppliers how the store will look. |
| 5 to 10 | L0804.038 | 2023 | Enough; checkout does not need to be enabled. |
| 3 collections × 3 products | L0247.083 | 2021, older | Keep the store small while suppliers are contacted. |
| A handful | L0968.038 | 2021, older | A handful of demo products is enough to build the store. |

- Across these sources, the figures agree that the store should stay small. Five appears most often; 10 to 20 is the upper range. [L0243.030, L0934.089, L0934.159, L0468.062, L0123.003]
- Do not overbuild at this stage. [L0088.002]
- A demo store needs about five products to be credible to suppliers. Product descriptions can be short, because suppliers only need to see what the product pages look like. [L0934.159]
- Keep the product pages short during review, since the purpose is to show suppliers how the site is organized, not to impress with copy. [L0934.166]
- Collections do not need to be filled with products before launch. [L0962.036]

### Stock images: number, sources and legal sourcing

#### How many images per product
- One image per demo product is sufficient. Multiple images are not required. [L0036.008]
- One product photograph is sufficient for each demo product. [L0219.007]
- One image per product at acceptable quality is enough. It does not need to be perfect; the best available image is sufficient. [L0964.092]
- Demo products that have only one product photo are fine. Stock images with only one angle are acceptable. [L0855.097]
- Recommended: at least one product photo without people. Lifestyle photos with people are optional. If none is available on Canva, search Google royalty-free images for one product shot per product. [L0834.034]
- Five to 10 images in total are enough for five demo products plus a homepage banner. [L0268.066]
- For a mock store, use three to five royalty-free stock images found through a Google image search. Do not use the supplier images you plan to show in outreach. [L0671.046, L0671.185]
- Five distinct product photos are enough to start. Search the niche plus the words white background. [L1111.074]
- A handful of product photos can be enough at launch. [L0247.086]
- Only a handful of images per product type is needed, because dummy products are removed as soon as real suppliers are live. [L0359.040]
- Upload no more than two source images per product when copying from a source page. If the source page has only one image, upload only that one. [L0452.009, L0452.028]
- Never launch with only demo or stock images. Use stock photos during the build and demo phase, and replace them with supplier product photos as soon as a supplier is approved. [L0270.040]

#### Image sources

| Source | Notes | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Burst (Shopify), Pexels, Pixabay, Negative Space, FreePik, Vex, Adobe (free section), FreeImages.com, Unsplash | Free stock photo sites | L0036.003 |
| Burst, Pexels, Negative Space, Freepik, Vecteezy, Adobe Stock, Canva with Magic Media (AI-generated) | Use when supplier images are unavailable or you need more visual variations | L0091.001 |
| 123RF.com | Millions of images, $2 to $3 per photo. Use when supplier images are unavailable. Older (2021). | L0270.031 |
| Paid stock photos, about $1 per image | Paid sites can fill gaps when free royalty-free images are scarce. Older (2021). | L0247.086 |
| Paid stock photos, a dollar or two each | Buy a few only if free images cannot be found. Older (2021). | L0968.038 |
| Canva Magic Media (AI-generated) and licensed stock | Shows the layout of a product page without implying an existing supplier relationship | L0623.015 |
| Pexels | Free stock site with a product-type search, useful for placeholders. Older (2019). | L1275.030 |
| Pixabay | Free stock alternative | L0962.037, L0853.047 |
| Pexels, Pixabay, Google Images, Alibaba, AliExpress | Search for usable photos. If nothing usable turns up, search for a very similar product and use its photos. | L1259.060 |
| Instagram hashtags (customer-style UGC photos) | One weighted hula hoop campaign using such images reached an 8 to 9 percent conversion rate. | L1259.060 |
| Fiverr freelancer | About 5 to 10 USD to fix backgrounds and make the product stand out, or retouch with an AI image tool when photos are pixelated or cluttered | L1259.060 |
| Brand public sites | Older (2020): if no suitable stock photos exist, use photos from brands you have not contacted yet, for example from their public sites. Replace them once the supplier is approved. | L1277.056 |

- Paid stock photos are generally not worth buying for a demo or mock store. If images have already been bought, reuse them on other content pages and in social media marketing. Older (2019). [L0467.075]
- Google Images, Pinterest and similar sites are not a stock source for this purpose. Use the sources in the table above. [L1259.060] *(Conflict noted below.)*

#### Sourcing procedures

Procedure: Commercial rights on Google Images [L0036.004]
1. Search your niche on google.com/images.
2. Click Tools.
3. Click Usage Rights.
4. Select 'Commercial and other licenses'.
5. Review results and verify each image by visiting its source site before using.

Procedure: Labeled for reuse on Google Images [L0359.041]
1. Open Google and search for the product type, for example leather sofa.
2. Click Images.
3. Click Tools.
4. Open the usage rights menu and select Labeled for reuse.
5. Save only a handful of images for the demo products.

Procedure: Labeled for reuse, from the Source D-style store guide [L0767.006]
1. Search the product type on Google.
2. Switch to the Images tab.
3. Open Tools.
4. Set Usage rights to 'Labeled for reuse'. Downloaded images are then royalty-free and safe to use on a website.

Setting: Google Images path for royalty-free photos [L0767.007]
- Google search > Images tab > Tools > Usage rights > 'Labeled for reuse'.
- 'Labeled for reuse with modification' is the alternative when edits are planned.

Procedure: Royalty-free images for demo products, older (2020) [L0852.087]
1. Search Google for the product name, for example leather sofa.
2. Click Images.
3. Click Tools.
4. Click Usage rights.
5. Choose labeled for reuse or labeled for reuse with modification.
6. Download a set of images and name the products as you want.
7. Write a one or two sentence basic description for each product.

Free stock photo sites can also be used. [L0852.087]

Procedure: Free images for demo products, older (2021) [L0853.047]
1. Search Google Images for the product name.
2. Click Tools, then choose Usage rights and select a free-to-use option.
3. Pick suitable stock images to use as demo products.
4. Alternatively, browse Pixabay for free stock images.
5. Upload 5 to 10 demo products and replace them with real products once suppliers are approved.

Procedure: Free images with usage rights, older (2021) [L0962.037]
1. Run a Google image search for your product.
2. Open Tools.
3. Under Usage rights, select Creative Commons licenses.
4. Use the results that show the product clearly.

Pixabay is a free stock photo alternative. [L0962.037]

Procedure: Demo product pages, older (2018) [L0466.055]
1. Open Google Images and search for the product, for example stand-up paddle boards.
2. Click the Images tab, then Tools, then Usage rights, and choose Labeled for reuse.
3. Select a royalty-free image of the product.
4. Create a product page named after the product, for example orange paddle board.
5. Add a few lines describing the product, such as size, color, brand, and included parts.

Setting, older (2018): Google Images > Settings > Advanced search > Usage rights dropdown > Free to use or share > Advanced search. Choose images labeled for reuse and use them only as placeholders until supplier images arrive. Do not copy images from other sites. [L1274.068]

Setting, older (2019): Google Images > Tools > Usage rights > Labeled for reuse. Use this filter to find product photos you can legally use as stock images for a demo store. [L1275.029]

Setting, older (2019): To find demo product images, search the product term in Google, open the Images tab, click Tools, then set Usage rights to 'Labeled for reuse'. Use the generic photos you find for your demo store. [L1002.024]

Procedure, older (2020): Image sourcing for a new store before brands send photos [L1277.056]
1. Start with stock photo sites, since only a handful of images per product is needed.
2. If no suitable stock photos exist, use photos from brands you have not contacted yet, for example from their public sites.
3. Replace these placeholders with supplier images once the supplier is approved.

#### Usage rights: which label applies

| Label | Where it appears | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Commercial and other licenses | Google Images > Tools > Usage Rights | L0036.004 |
| Labeled for reuse | Google Images > Tools > Usage rights | L0359.041, L0767.006, L0767.011, L1275.029, L0466.055, L1002.024 |
| Labeled for reuse with modification | Google Images, used when edits are planned | L0767.007, L0852.087 |
| Free to use or share | Google Images advanced search (older, 2018) | L1274.068 |
| Creative Commons licenses | Google Images > Tools > Usage rights (older, 2021) | L0962.037 |
| Free-to-use option | Google Images > Tools > Usage rights | L0853.047 |

- Images labeled for reuse can be published on your own website without the risk of a copyright claim. [L0767.011]
- Google Images labeled 'licensable' means the copyright holder expects payment. Always verify the source website to confirm the actual licensing terms before using any image found through the commercial license filter. [L0036.027]

#### Legal rules for images
- Do not copy product photos from competitor websites. Use free stock sites to avoid legal risks and to demonstrate professionalism. [L0036.013]
- Do not copy product photos from Google Shopping or competitor sites. Use your own photos or licensed stock images to avoid rights problems. [L1462.020]
- Do not copy images from other sites. [L1274.068]
- Do not use actual supplier photos or PLR (Private Label Rights) content. [L0268.066]
- Never upload real supplier products, photos or descriptions before you have approval. [L0551.023]
- If you use royalty-free stock photos from image search, you can keep them on a demo store. If the rights are unclear, remove them and use images from suppliers that have approved you. [L1112.039]
- Demo products that use another supplier's photos should be deleted once that supplier approves. [L1112.038]
- Demo product pages should show only a general idea of a future product page. They must not be used to claim that a brand has already approved the store. [L0623.016]
- Older (2019): Using a supplier's photos for demo products before approval usually does no harm. Stock photos avoid the question entirely and are easy to swap out. [L1275.032] *(Conflict noted below.)*
- Conflict on supplier photos: L1275.032 (2019) says supplier photos usually do no harm, while L0268.066 (2020), L1462.020, and L0551.023 say not to use them before approval. Applies: use royalty-free or licensed images before approval. L1275.032 is older advice.
- Conflict on competitor copying: the competitor-copy procedure in L0452.009 includes copying images from the source page, while L0036.013 and L1462.020 forbid copying competitor photos. Applies: copy only the title and description where allowed, and replace photos with licensed or own images.

#### Image quality and sizing
- Demo store images do not need top quality. A demo store shows the setup, not conversion. Focus on consistent sizing. [L0806.038]
- Canva procedure, older (2024) [L0806.037]
  1. Click Create a design in the top right.
  2. Choose Custom size.
  3. Enter 600 x 600 pixels and click Create new design.
  4. Add images with Magic Media (AI generated) or drag in your own.
  5. Save; all images have the same size.
- Demo-site images can stay in place during setup. After launch and once suppliers are onboard, swap them for supplier and brand lifestyle images that show the actual products. [L1310.002]

### Demo product content

#### Descriptions
- Demo product descriptions can be identical across products, using a generic template with placeholders swapped out for each product. Do not overthink the creation process. [L0036.009]
- Demo product example: name Aloha Blue Longboard Surfboard, weight 13 pounds, with description, key features, and specifications populated from a provided template. [L0036.012]
- Do not use Lorem Ipsum for product descriptions on demo stores. Write basic but genuine sentences describing actual product attributes and benefits. Older (2020). [L0245.025]
- Older (2020) example sentence, given in italics: *This blue eight-foot surfboard accommodates up to 150 pounds and is great for enjoying water activities during favorable weather.* [L0245.026]
- Demo product descriptions can be basic placeholders. They do not need persuasive copy at the supplier-approval stage. [L0855.098]
- A one- to two-line description lets the supplier picture its product on the site. Do not optimize for sales or conversions. Older (2018). [L1085.038]
- Demo products do not need materials, dimensions, technical specifications, or detailed descriptions, since customers cannot buy them. Generic product descriptions are sufficient. Older (2020). [L0268.082]
- Mock product descriptions and images can be taken from images labeled for reuse in Google image search, combined with basic descriptions. Older (2020). [L0967.048]
- Supplier product descriptions may be used for demo or test products only if they come directly from the brand. Search a sentence from the description in Google; if it appears on many sites, it is the brand's stock text. Older (2019). [L0467.039]
- Copying a description that someone else rewrote or paid for, to make it look unique, creates a copyright problem. Write your own description, or use the brand's original text. Older (2019). [L0467.040]
- Do not present a demo product as stock you are selling, and do not write a long invented story about it. The demo page only shows suppliers how their product could look on the site. Older (2018). [L0466.056]
- Rich text is not needed for supplier approval and can be hidden. Turn it on later for brand story or promotion content once the store is live and the unique value proposition is clear. [L0775.049]
- Product reviews, vendor names and secondary images need product data that a demo store does not have yet. Leave those options off until the store has the content. [L0775.048]

#### Names, prices and vendor
- Name pattern: size, product type and color. Example: a clear, descriptive title. Older (2018). [L1085.038]
- Example: seven foot blue standup paddle board. Demo products do not need invented brand names or elaborate descriptions. The brand name can be the store name. Older (2021). [L0834.033]
- Do not invent a company or brand name that does not match the picture. Older (2019). [L0356.024]
- Demo products may have made-up names and invented prices, since they are placeholders and not meant to be sold. [L0767.008]
- Demo products should be generic items from the same category as the niche, for example a lamp or candlestick for a chandelier store. This lets a supplier see what the catalog page for that category would look like. [L0767.012]
- Price: use $799 for demo products. [L0219.006]
- Price: the price can be anything; image size or editing does not matter, because demo products will be deleted later. [L0452.008]
- Price: the exact price is not critical for demos. [L0088.006]
- Show average prices and generic inventory numbers on the demo store before launch. Cart is disabled, so no purchases occur. Older (2022). [L0272.013]
- Hide discount codes and inventory levels, so suppliers do not see broken or unfinished elements. [L1189.024] *(Conflict noted below.)*
- Vendor for a demo: your store name. [L0036.021, L0069.001]
- Vendor for a demo: your own company name, since it does not matter for demonstration. [L0452.013]
- Supplier name field: enter your own store name instead of a real supplier's name. Older (2019). [L1069.024]
- Conflict on displayed prices and inventory: L0272.013 (2022) says show average prices and generic inventory numbers; L1189.024 says hide inventory levels and discount codes. Applies: show plausible generic figures if you want the catalog to look realistic (L0272.013, older); hide them if the goal is to keep the page free of unfinished elements (L1189.024).

#### Featuring brands
- Do not feature products from brands you are not yet authorized to sell. Add products to the live store only after the supplier has approved you. [L0276.005]
- Demo products are temporary placeholders, removed and replaced with real supplier products once you have brand partnerships. [L0088.008]

#### AI-assisted content workflow

*Full prompt texts are not part of the source units. Only the opening lines are given, in italics.* [L0088.006, L0219.010]

Procedure: Demo product with AI prompts [L0088.006]
