- *Older (2021-22).* In the search engine listing preview of a product, click the Edit SEO link to change the URL and the page title and description by hand. If you leave them alone, Shopify fills the title and URL from the product and copies the main description. [L1166.038]
- *Older (2021-22).* Leaving the search description on its automatic setting copies the main description, which gets cut off in search results. Replace it with a short text ending in a call to action. [L1166.036]
- *Older (2021-22).* Check that the search engine listing title matches the product, then shorten the description and end it with a call to action. [L1166.004]

### Variants and options

- *Older (2021-22).* Close variants of the same product, such as different colours, sizes or materials, should be set up as variants with a selector on one product page, not as separate product pages. Using your own judgement to decide what counts as a close variant is required, and the question can be asked when unsure. [L1163.022]
- *Older (2021-22).* Creating a separate product page for each range, colour or size with identical images creates duplicate pages that complicate marketing later. Use variants on a single product page instead. [L1166.034]
- *Older (2021-22).* Use variants on one product page when a product comes in different ranges, colours or sizes with the same images. Shopify allows up to three options per product, and more options need a separate app in some markets. [L1166.018]
- Shopify limits a product to 100 variants. For products with more variants, use an app like Infinite Options or list only the 100 most popular variations. [L1279.039]
- *Older (2021-22).* Each variant can have its own price, stock quantity and SKU, and different variants often have different supplier model numbers. Enter them in the variant table that appears after the options are set. [L1166.019]
- Variant procedure, Source A 2025: [L1287.029]
  1. Open the product and go to the Variants section.
  2. Click the option to add options, such as Color or Size.
  3. Enter the values, for example blue and green, and click Done.
  4. Set the price for each variant.
  5. Set the quantity for each variant, which requires inventory tracking.
  6. Upload a separate image for each variant.
  7. Click Save and then Preview, and swipe through the variants to check the price, image and stock.
- *Older (2021-22).* Use a variant app for products with more options than Shopify's native variant system can handle, and set the app up separately from the standard product variants. Do not mix the app's variants with the native variants on the same product. Test one product before uploading a whole group. [L1396.062]

### CSV import and export

- *Older (2021-22).* CSV bulk method, in this order: [L1166.023]
  1. Discard the unsaved product form, then go to Products > Export > All products > Export products. Shopify emails a CSV of existing products, which serves as your template; the template file from Shopify's help pages also works.
  2. Open the file in a spreadsheet. Each line is one product.
  3. Fill the columns from Title through the column before SEO Description. Leave Handle blank.
  4. Paste titles, descriptions, prices and SKUs from supplier spreadsheets into the matching columns.
  5. Enter option names and their values; list option values on the lines below the product in the layout of the template.
  6. Set the taxable and requires-shipping columns to TRUE for taxable physical products.
  7. Upload each image in Settings > Files, copy its URL and paste it into the Image Src column.
  8. Save the file as a CSV file; the exact menu depends on Mac or Windows, and the file must be a CSV.
  9. Go to Products > Import, choose the file, tick the option to overwrite products with the same handle, then click Upload.
  10. Check that the products appear with their data.

- *Older (2021-22).* Columns to fill in the Shopify CSV, one product per line: [L1166.025]

| Column | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Handle | leave blank; Shopify builds the URL handle from the title |
| Title | product title |
| Body (HTML) | product description |
| Vendor | brand or supplier name |
| Type | product type, named consistently |
| Tags | tags for grouping products |
| Option1 Name / Option1 Value, up to Option3 | option name and values; maximum three options |
| Variant SKU | supplier model or part number |
| Variant Inventory Tracker | shopify |
| Variant Inventory Qty | stock quantity; 10 as placeholder |
| Variant Price | selling price per variant |
| Image Src | hosted file URL from Settings > Files |
| Variant Taxable | TRUE for taxable products |
| Variant Requires Shipping | TRUE for physical products |
| SEO Description and columns to the right | fill up to SEO Description; ignore the columns to the right |

- *Older (2021-22).* Leave the Handle column blank in the CSV, because Shopify creates the handle automatically from the product title. The handle becomes the product URL. [L1166.026]
- *Older (2021-22).* When importing with the overwrite option ticked, products with the same handle are updated instead of duplicated. Use this when re-importing products that already exist in the store. [L1166.027]
- *Older (2021-22).* Settings > Files stores images uploaded to the store and gives each file a URL. Paste that URL into the Image Src column of the CSV; a local file path will not import the image. [L1166.028]
- *Older (2021-22).* A CSV image link must be a hosted URL from Settings > Files; putting a local file name in the Image Src column will leave the product without an image. [L1166.037]
- *Older (2021-22).* Shopify's help pages include a CSV template file with a description of each column and what it must contain. Use it when a column is unclear before importing. [L1166.029]
- *Older (2019).* Shopify publishes the required column format for product CSV imports. Search for the Shopify CSV format, then reformat the supplier's file to match it. [L1275.028]
- *Older (2021-22).* Manual entry suits a small number of products or a team that finds spreadsheets daunting. CSV import suits hundreds of products at once, and supplier spreadsheets can be pasted straight into it. [L1166.024]

### Supplier file cleaning

- *Older (2021).* Supplier file cleaning, in this order: [L1279.038]
  1. Open the supplier CSV and the Shopify product CSV template side by side.
  2. Reorder and rename the supplier columns to match the Shopify columns; copying and pasting covers most of the work.
  3. Fill in any remaining fields and upload the file.
  4. If manual entry is needed, first list the competitors' best-selling products that come from your approved supplier, then upload everything else in the background.

### Bulk edits

- *Older (2022).* Bulk updating product fields via Shopify CSV import: [L1281.060]
  1. Add a column for the product guarantee or description to a product CSV.
  2. Fill it in for every product from the same supplier.
  3. Import the file to update existing products in one step. The same import can create new products.
- *Older (2021-22).* Keep all catalog changes organized in a single supplier list, and note whenever a manufacturer updates its price list so the uploads and quotes stay current. Update the price list in every place where it is stored. Assign this update to a staff member as part of their regular tasks. [L1396.060]

### Outsourcing uploads

- *Older (2021-22).* Set up each new product using the product upload board in this order: [L1396.037]
  1. Enter the supplier name.
  2. Enter the product description or a link where the description can be found.
  3. Enter the source for product images, such as a shared Dropbox link.
  4. Enter the pricing rule: use the MAP price list if one exists, or otherwise add a fixed markup, such as 20%, and match prices from named competitor sites.
  5. Move the supplier card to the next stage once the upload is finished.
- *Older (2021-22).* Split large catalog imports into two roles: one staff member assembles the CSV for simple products, and a second person checks product features before the import. CSV errors are hard to trace after import, so products with many options should be uploaded manually or through a variant app instead. [L1396.075]
- *Older (2021-22).* For bulk catalog uploads of thousands of SKUs with many options, use CSV imports for simple products and manual uploads or a variant app for products that exceed Shopify's native options. A store with about 3,000 SKUs and products with up to 18 options needed this split. Put only the simpler products into a CSV, since errors in CSV files are hard to find. [L1396.061]

### Which products to upload first

- *Older (2021).* If manual entry is needed, first list the competitors' best-selling products that come from your approved supplier, then upload everything else in the background. [L1279.038]
- Products that are not in the first batch can be uploaded afterwards, in the background, once the first batch is live. [L1279.038]

### MAP and compare-at pricing

- Products should be listed at MAP as the selling price. Enter the MSRP in the compare-at price field so customers see MSRP struck through and pay the MAP. [L1285.036]
- The compare-at price should be the real price the item was offered at, otherwise the discount is misleading. For MAP-listed products, the compare-at price is the MSRP. [L1188.013, L1285.036]
- *Older (2021-22).* Pricing rule for uploads: use the MAP price list if one exists, or otherwise add a fixed markup, such as 20%, and match prices from named competitor sites. [L1396.037]

### Store-level rules

- *Older (2020).* There is no fixed maximum for products in a store. Stores with about 100 products and stores with 3,000 products both work, as long as the products are quality items from good brands in the niche. [L1277.054]
- Source C: markets for the store are set for UK, Australia and US. [L1288.002]

## Product page design and templates

### Product page structure and conversion layout

- Build the product page in this order: product title and images, a bold Buy now or Shop now button directly under the product with an Add to cart button above it, bundles, upsells and urgency elements, a short description with bullet points (more images than text, simple words), customer reviews, then the footer. [L0352.008]
- Customers land on product pages rather than separate landing pages, so optimise each product page as the main landing page and build it to convert traffic directly. [L0468.073]
- Once ads are running, send traffic to the product page rather than the homepage, because visitors can see the product and buy it immediately. [L0562.029]
- For a quick test, a minimal viable landing page is enough. Get the page live and tested first, and improve it from customer feedback after launch rather than aiming for a perfect design first. [L0552.018]
- Shopify's Dawn theme includes an instant add-to-cart button for collection pages. Enable it in the theme settings; no app is needed. [L0562.028]
- Place the satisfaction guarantee high on the page, after the key product information, so it reduces purchase risk early. Visitors close to buying often wonder what happens if the product disappoints. [L0524.015]
- Show scented or sensory products in a context that suggests the experience, such as on wood or among natural elements. Product images should be sharp and show the product in use. [L0524.016]
- Use tabs to compress long information instead of forcing a long scroll. Keep purchase options, reviews and key facts easy to reach from the top. [L0524.017]
- Add a question section below the reviews and keep the brand answering questions. This shows active support and gives visitors answers without leaving the page. [L0524.020]
- Show the offer clearly: product price, local shipping and delivery time, and bundle deals. State that stock is held in-country. [L0492.027]
- Competitor copying: copying a competitor's product page exactly can outperform a heavily edited version in some stores, with higher CTR and lower CPM. In one case, extra editing and polish reduced sales. Results vary by product. [L0660.021]
- Test each added feature (for example a sticky add-to-cart bar, a gift option or colour swatches) separately to see whether it increases sales, and check these features when comparing themes. [L0709.049]
- Include product identifiers such as SKU, UPC or part number in a dedicated section, so customers can search for the item by part number. [L0855.099]
- For fashion stores, add a size guide. Size problems were a likely cause of a chargeback on one fashion store even though a size guide was present. [L0660.019]

### Mobile product pages

- A long block of text before the product image on mobile hurts the first impression. Lead with visuals and the buy action, not paragraphs of details. [L0530.018]
- Mobile needs an adaptive layout that rearranges content for the device, not a shrunk desktop page. Keep the product name, what it does or smells like, testimonials and the quantity selector above the fold. [L0524.018]
- Check the mobile version of a page separately from desktop. Sections, images and paragraphs may display differently or be missing on mobile. Cross-check and add missing elements to the mobile layout. [L0321.036]
- When a PageFly product page differs between desktop and mobile, check each section on both versions. Where layouts differ, apply the same customisation on mobile by selecting the image and pasting the text. [L0363.031]
- Layout options in the Shopify theme editor (older, Source B 2021-22): 3 columns, 2 columns with medium image, and a further 2-column option. The 3-column layout shows as multiple columns on desktop only; mobile always stacks into one column. A two-column layout with a medium image is the traditional e-commerce layout and a sensible default. [L0370.003]

### Headline, offer and trust elements

- Standard Shopify product page (older, Source B 2021-22): the product title stays the H1 for SEO and sits at the top with price and image. The headline placed above the product description becomes the H2 and must not replace the product title. [L0416.009]
- Traditional sales page (older, Source B 2021-22): the headline is the H1 and the first element a visitor sees. The price usually comes at the end of the sales message on this page type. [L0416.010]
- Landing pages need a strong offer, several trust elements such as reviews and guarantees, and persuasive copywriting. The source refers to five key elements but does not list them. [L0637.037]
- Free gift on a product page (older, 2021):
  1. Lead the product description with text about the product, because search engines read the opening text.
  2. Below the main text, embed a large image that says the gift is free, shows the gift and states its value.
  3. The image should make clear the customer receives the gift free when ordering from the store. [L0469.040]
- Product-page graphics such as feature icons can be made by a designer and hard-coded into the site instead of installing an app. Back up the code before editing, or hire a developer on Upwork to do it (older, 2021). [L0469.041]
- Duplicated description text (the same text appears in the description and the specifications section): contact a Shopify developer. Find Shopify-certified developers on Fiverr.com or StoreTasker.com, and check spam folders for replies. [L0276.062]

### Product information settings (theme editor)

- Product Information block, recommended settings:
  - Enable sticky content on desktop: On
  - Desktop media width: Medium
  - Constrain media to screen height: On (Original)
  - Advanced settings are not needed at this stage. [L0104.005]
- Product Information subsection settings:
  - Variant Picker: Pills
  - Quantity Selector: Enabled
  - Sharing buttons: Enabled
  - Description placement: Next to Add to Cart button
  - Buy Buttons: Show dynamic checkout buttons: On
  - SKU label: "SKU" [L0104.006]
- Price on product pages cannot be hidden. When a Compare-at price is set, it automatically displays crossed-out pricing to show the discount. [L0104.007]
- The product title is pulled from the product name and cannot be edited in the theme customizer. The vendor field is dynamic and updates automatically when real supplier brands are added. [L0104.008]
- For demo stores, hide these Product Information subsections: Expiring Coupon, Estimated Delivery, Promo Image/Sale Banner, Inventory Status and Trust Badges. [L0104.009]
- Related Products section: it displays "You may also like" and lets you edit section text, layout, colours and number of products shown. For demo stores, use the eyedropper/hide icon to hide it temporarily and re-enable it later. [L0104.010]
- Stock levels on product pages:
  1. Open Online Store > Themes > Customize.
  2. Use the drop-down at the top (Home page) and choose Products > Default product > Product information.
  3. Click the eye icon next to Inventory status to hide it.
  4. Do not delete the element, so it can be restored later. [L0472.029]
- Inventory numbers can be entered in Shopify even when they are not live. A placeholder value per product (for example 10 or 100) lets the inventory status be hidden on product pages while the back end still tracks stock. [L0472.030]
- Shopify has no built-in field for product availability dates. Add the availability or backorder date as text on the product page (2020, older). [L0468.065]
- Shopify free-shipping badge: go to Online Store > Themes > Customize > Products, select a product and look for free shipping under availability. If it is not there, it may have been added to custom code or another setting, so ask Shopify support. [L0856.063]
- Product page template edits for notes: under the theme's product template, add a note near the Add to cart button. For a back-order note, insert it in the product template near that button. [L0856.068]
- PDF manuals (2022, older): upload the file to the Files section in Shopify, then add a link to it on the product page or in the description. [L0249.026]
- Product comparison table (2020, older): build a simple table with attributes in rows and products in columns. No specialised app is required. [L0245.077]
- Editing product templates: in the theme editor, the product template shows as Default product. Add, remove or reorder the variant selector, quantity selector, product recommendations and announcement bar in the sidebar, then preview before publishing. [L0600.042]
- Customising colours with custom CSS (for example the colour of struck-through MSRP prices):
  1. Ask ChatGPT or another AI tool to generate CSS for the specific change.
  2. Copy the generated CSS.
  3. In Shopify, go to Online Store > Themes > Customize > Custom CSS.
  4. Paste the CSS and save and publish. [L0255.074]
- Quantity selector: if a supplier sells in a fixed quantity, set the default quantity selector to the most common purchase amount. Base the default on what top competitors show (2021, older). [L0834.039]

### Product information: custom input, files and line item properties

- The file upload option in the theme lets customers upload files directly from the product page, for example a design or photo for a custom product. This avoids a separate app for collecting files. [L0709.020]
- Line item properties store customer input, such as an engraving name, and display it on the Shopify order. A standard supplier order usually carries only variant details, so the property must be mapped to the supplier's API to reach them. [L0709.002]
- Line item properties can be generated dynamically from Shopify metafields, which store extra product or customer data. The supplier's system must recognise the parameter it receives, or the data is lost. [L0709.003]
- Developers are still needed for complex functions such as a bundle builder, which page builders like Replo and PageFly generally cannot produce. Simple landing pages and stock themes with page builders can be enough for brands that do not focus on branding. [L0709.005]
- A simple custom engraving or name field built by a developer was roughly estimated at about $30. A complicated setup or a poorly built theme can cost much more. This figure is a rough guess, not a quote. [L0709.045]

### Descriptions and formatting

- Expandable sections in the Manhattan theme (Heading 6):
  1. Shopify Admin > Products > select the product.
  2. Highlight section headers (for example Key Features, Why You'll Love It, Specifications).
  3. Change the heading format to Heading 6 (H6).
  4. Remove divider lines between sections.
  5. Click Save. [L0104.002]
- Heading 6 creates expandable sections in the Manhattan theme. This keeps long descriptions readable and organised. [L0104.001]
- Creating expandable tabs with Heading 6:
  1. In the product description editor, highlight a section heading (for example Description).
  2. Click the formatting dropdown (currently showing Paragraph).
  3. Select Heading 6.
  4. Click Save.
  5. Repeat for other sections (Key features, Specifications).
  6. Click Preview. Sections now collapse and expand when clicked. [L0036.023]
- Heading 6 formatting with Manhattan presents long product information cleanly without overwhelming customers. It improves both usability and page digest. [L0036.024]
- Expanded state in Manhattan: the first section (the description) displays expanded by default. All other sections display collapsed and expand only when clicked. [L0036.031]
- Optional Description toggle: add a new heading labelled Description, set it to Heading 6, and place it above the main product description. [L0104.003]
- Theme customisations apply to all product pages:
  1. Shopify Admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize.
  2. Change the page selector from Home page to Products > Default Product.
  3. Make customisations, which then apply to all product pages. [L0104.004]
- ChatGPT-generated descriptions with bullet points often display with misaligned or badly spaced bullets when pasted into Shopify's description editor. Fix this by highlighting the affected text, removing the bullet formatting from the editor menu, and reapplying the bullets. [L0219.019]
- Copywriting prompt (2024):

```
Please act as a direct response copywriter. Write a product description for [product name] and/or [product URL] using this format: a headline; a one to three sentence description; a features and benefits section; and bullet points. Base every feature and claim on the supplier information provided, and do not invent specifications.
```
[L0360.018, L0360.017]

Note: the prompt above is the verbatim text of L0360.017; L0360.018 covers the custom-made product page that uses the description.

- Product description template (seasonal one-size product, 2026). Fill every bracket with facts about the product and your actual policies before publishing:

```
[PRODUCT NAME] is one of the best [PRODUCT TYPE] for [SEASON]. [MATERIAL AND FEEL, e.g. soft, warm, high quality]. [SIZE STATEMENT, e.g. one size that fits most adults, stated only if true]. [RETURN AND EXCHANGE PROMISE, only if your policy supports it]. [SHIPPING TIME, e.g. fast shipping to the USA].
```
[L0347.025]

- Product description template (core strengths and technical details). Replace the bracketed fields with product facts:

```
The [Product Name] merges durability, practical utility, and contemporary design to address [specific need or use] with reliable performance and extended product longevity.

Core Strengths:

Robust Material Composition: Fabricated from premium-grade [material] engineered for sustained use and extended lifespan.

Adaptable Use Cases: Performs effectively for [common use] while remaining suitable for [secondary uses].

Refined Aesthetic: Incorporates current design trends with a clean profile that integrates naturally across different environments.

Accessible Operation: Straightforward to use with included documentation for setup and reference.

Environmentally Responsible Manufacturing: Produced using sustainable methods designed to reduce ecological footprint.

Technical Details:

Size: [Length] x [Width] x [Height]

Mass: [Weight]

Composition: [Material Type]
```
[L0091.002]

### Expandable tabs, accordions and policy blocks

- Typical product page tabs: description, specifications, shipping info, warranty and price match guarantee. Copy the supplier's warranty text, but swap the contact email for your own. [L0808.002]
- Built-in tabs: the new Charlotte theme includes product tabs, so no tab app is needed. An alternative tab app costs about $5 per month. [L0665.041]
- Tabs built with the theme's own code (2021, older) keep the page lighter and faster. Use apps only for features too hard to code directly. [L0853.110]
- Accordion setup in Visually (Source E 2025-26): 
  - Auto-expand the details accordion on page load only for first-time visitors.
  - Leave the details collapsed by default for returning customers.
  - Implementation requires 3 clicks in Visually. [L0197.015]
- Policy blocks in the Manhattan default template (2024):
  1. Shopify Admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize.
  2. Change the dropdown from Homepage to Products.
  3. Click into the Default Product template.
  4. Click Add Block and select Collapsible Row.
  5. Drag it under the product description.
  6. Change the title to Shipping Policy or Price Match Policy.
  7. Add the content text in the editor.
  8. Choose an icon (checkmark, heart, etc.).
  9. Click Save.
  10. Repeat steps 4 to 9 for additional policies. Because this is the default template, it applies to all product pages. [L0253.006]
- Policy information (2025): to add policy information, go to the theme editor, click customise, select the collection or product template and add sections using rich text or collapsible content. Include financing, customer support, return policy and price match information as expandable sections. Drag them below the product images and save. [L0255.019]
- Accordions can hold an extra paragraph, such as a free shipping reminder placed below the description and dimensions. This is a theme-level change and usually needs the theme developer. [L0855.181]
- Tabs on product pages compress long information and keep purchase options, reviews and key facts near the top. [L0524.017]
- Duplicated descriptions in the default product layout: see L0276.062 under Product page structure.

### Supplier-specific product page templates

- Create a template per supplier:
  1. In the theme editor, click the Default product template.
  2. Click Create template.
  3. Name the template after the supplier (example: Brand B).
  4. Base it on the Default product template.
  5. Save the new template.
  6. Expand Product information.
  7. Enable and edit the Estimated delivery settings.
  8. Set a custom delivery window for this supplier (example: 7-14 days).
  9. Save changes. [L0139.009]
- Assign the template to products:
  1. Go to Shopify Admin > Products.
  2. Select a product from the target supplier.
  3. Scroll to the Theme template field.
  4. Select the correct template name for that supplier (example: Brand B).
  5. Save the product. [L0139.010]
- Product templates control estimated delivery dates, coupon display variations and other layout and content changes as needed. [L0139.011]
- Slower or custom-made products that cannot be returned: go to the theme editor, duplicate the default product page template, rename the copy (for example custom made or supplier), change the delivery estimate and returns text there, then assign that template to the affected products. [L0360.018]
- Shipping or lead-time differences (2021, older):
  1. In the theme code, duplicate the product page template.
  2. Give the copy a clear name, such as product page template followed by a number.
  3. Change the tabs or shipping-date information in the copy.
  4. Assign the new template to the products with longer shipping times. [L0671.077, L0671.214]
  
  Note: L0671.077 and L0671.214 are identical units and are merged here.
- Warranty templates (2024):
  1. Go to Shopify Admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize.
  2. Switch to Products.
  3. Click Create Template.
  4. Name the template after the supplier and warranty type (for example Supplier ABC 90-day).
  5. Click Create Template.
  6. Add Block > Collapsible Row.
  7. Rename it to Warranty Information.
  8. Add the warranty details in the content editor.
  9. Save.
  10. Repeat for each warranty duration (90-day, 1-year, 3-year, 5-year).
  11. Assign each product to the correct template from the template dropdown on the product page.
  12. Multiple templates allow different warranty information on different products. [L0253.023]
- Product page template without free shipping (2025):
  1. In the theme customizer, open the product page template.
  2. Click Default product, then Products, scroll to the bottom and click Create template.
  3. Name the new template, for example no free shipping.
  4. Change every reference to free shipping on the page (such as an offer saying free shipping) to a different offer, for example 10% off.
  5. Save and check the page for any other free shipping references.
  6. Assign this template to all products from the brand that cannot ship free.
  
  The announcement bar can still say that most products ship free. [L0857.059]
- Free-gift offer placed elsewhere on the page (2025):
  1. Go to Products and create a template based on the default product page, named for the offer.
  2. Click Add section, choose Image with text and drag it to the desired position.
  3. To add a block under the discount code, hover over the expiring coupon block, click the plus and add a block with text or custom liquid.
  4. Save, then assign the template to the products that should show the offer. [L0667.035]
- Shipping selection dropdown (2019, older): default to free curbside drop-off at $0, with paid options such as in-home delivery at $100 and second-floor delivery at $300, explained next to the dropdown. [L0663.071]
- Copy warranty text from a supplier but swap the contact email for your own. [L0808.002]

### Variant descriptions and variant selection

- Decide whether variants appear on one page or on separate product pages by checking the top two or three future competitors in the niche and copying their approach. Default to one page with variations when the leaders use variants (2022, older). [L0429.023]
- Standard and premium versions with different materials and build quality: look at how the top Google Shopping results display them, and copy their structure (one page with variants or separate pages) but write your own words (2019, older). [L0467.042]
- Bold Product Options (2019, older): an app that lets customers choose custom sizes and materials on the product page. Preset option values can update the price in real time, while free-text entry may not. Check with the app's support before relying on it. [L0467.047]
- A single Canva image of colour swatches does not let customers click a colour and add that variant to the cart. Clickable swatches need variant options built into the product, typically through an app (2025). [L0837.059]
- Set up variant-specific descriptions in this order:
  1. Create a variant metafield named "Variant description" with type Rich text.
  2. Create a snippet named "variant-description" and paste the complete corrected snippet code into it.
  3. Create a new product page template, hide the "Description" block, add a Custom Liquid block and paste `{% render 'variant-description' %}` into it.
  4. Assign the new product page template to every product that has variants. [L0621.001]
- The variant metafield "Variant description" (type Rich text) stores formatted text separately for each variant. The snippet reads it as `custom.variant_description` and outputs it with the metafield_tag filter. [L0621.003]
- Snippet code (variant-description.liquid), exactly as provided:

```
{%- comment -%}
Snippet: variant-description.liquid
Goal: Show a variant-specific description when available; otherwise fall back to
product.description.
Metafield: variants -> custom.variant_description (Rich text)
Notes:
- Scoped to this product section using section.id to avoid conflicts with quick-add or
multiple products.
{%- endcomment -%}

{%- assign current_variant = product.selected_or_first_available_variant -%}

<div id="VariantDescription-{{ section.id }}" class="rte" style="margin-top:1rem">
{%- if current_variant.metafields.custom.variant_description != blank -%}
{{ current_variant.metafields.custom.variant_description | metafield_tag }}
{%- else -%}
{{ product.description }}
{%- endif -%}
</div>

{%- comment -%}
Hidden templates (scoped by section.id)
{%- endcomment -%}
<div id="VariantDescriptionTemplates-{{ section.id }}" hidden>
<template id="DefaultProductDescription-{{ section.id }}">
{{ product.description }}
</template>

{%- for v in product.variants -%}
<template data-variant-id="{{ v.id }}">
{%- if v.metafields.custom.variant_description != blank -%}
{{ v.metafields.custom.variant_description | metafield_tag }}
{%- endif -%}
</template>
{%- endfor -%}
</div>

<script>
document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', function {
  var sectionId = '{{ section.id }}';

  // Scope to this section only
  var sectionEl = document.getElementById('shopify-section-{{ section.id }}') ||
    document.querySelector('[data-section-id="{{ section.id }}"]') || document;

  var container = document.getElementById('VariantDescription-' + sectionId);
  if (!container) return;

  var templatesRoot = document.getElementById('VariantDescriptionTemplates-' + sectionId);
  var defaultTpl = templatesRoot ? templatesRoot.querySelector('#DefaultProductDescription-' + sectionId): null;
  var defaultHTML = defaultTpl ? defaultTpl.innerHTML.trim: '';

  var descMap = {};
  if (templatesRoot) {
    templatesRoot.querySelectorAll('template[data-variant-id]').forEach(function(t) {
      descMap[t.dataset.variantId] = t.innerHTML.trim;
    });
  }

  // Try multiple robust selectors for the variant ID input,
  // scoped to this section to avoid grabbing quick-add forms.
  var idInput =
    sectionEl.querySelector('#product-form-{{ section.id }} input[name="id"]') ||
    sectionEl.querySelector('form[action^="/cart/add"] input[name="id"]') ||
    sectionEl.querySelector('input[name="id"][type="hidden"]');

  // If no variant selector (e.g., single-variant product), keep initial render
  if (!idInput) return;

  function setDescription(variantId) {
    var html = descMap[variantId] || '';
    container.innerHTML = html || defaultHTML; // fallback to product.description
    container.style.display = (container.innerHTML.trim === '') ? 'none': '';
  }

  // Initialize
  setDescription(idInput.value);

  // Listen for option changes on the section's product form
  var form = idInput.form;
  if (form) {
    form.addEventListener('change', function {
      setDescription(idInput.value);
    });
  }

  // Watch the hidden variant id input for programmatic changes (theme JS)
  var mo = new MutationObserver(function {
    setDescription(idInput.value);
  });
  mo.observe(idInput, { attributes: true, attributeFilter: ['value'] });

  // Some themes dispatch custom events from option elements; listen broadly in this section
  sectionEl.addEventListener('change', function {
    setDescription(idInput.value);
  });

  // Handle back/forward nav that changes ?variant=
  window.addEventListener('popstate', function {
    setTimeout(function{ setDescription(idInput.value); }, 0);
  });
});
</script>
```
[L0621.002]

- The snippet shows the selected variant's own description and falls back to the main product description when that variant has none. If the resulting text is empty, the description container is hidden entirely. [L0621.004]
- Hide the default Description block on the new product page template, so only the variant-description snippet displays the text. Insert the snippet through a Custom Liquid block. [L0621.005]
- Assign the new template only to products with variants. Single-variant products have no variant selector, so the script keeps its initial render and does not update. [L0621.006]
- Do not use unscoped selectors for the variant ID input or the section. Without section.id scoping, quick-add forms or several products on one page can receive wrong variant updates. [L0621.007]
- The script must update the description in three cases: when the product form changes, when theme JavaScript changes the hidden variant ID value, and when back or forward navigation changes the variant in the URL. Themes differ, so the script listens to several events. [L0621.008]
- Code copied from the PDF version of this material contains broken characters, such as curly quotes and missing operators (`||`, `?` and `:`). Replace the quotes with straight quotes and restore the operators before pasting, otherwise the snippet will not run. [L0621.009]

### Buttons and cart behaviour

Conflicting sources on what Add to cart should do:

| Source | Value given | Condition stated |
|---|---|---|
| L0268.021 (2020, older) | Customise the button to go directly to checkout instead of staying on the product page | None stated |
| L0370.004 (2021-22, older) | Theme setting Enable cart redirection sends the user to the cart after adding a product | Setting exists; no guidance on when to use |
| L0835.077 (2023) | Send one-off buyers directly to the cart after add to cart | One-off purchases; for stores where shoppers commonly buy several items, show a slider or cart option that lets them keep shopping |
| L0339.007 (Source C) | Replace Buy Now with Add to Cart, install a cart drawer app such as Slide Cart, let customers continue or check out from the drawer | Cart drawer setup |
| L0656.021 (Source C) | Shop Now button goes directly to checkout; Add to Cart followed by a separate cart page adds steps and loses buyers | Product page that needs a direct purchase path |
| L0339.008 (Source C) | PageFly template, General tab, field After Add to Cart Action: the lesson sets Go to Checkout Page, but the speaker then says it should stay on the same page | Check which value fits the cart drawer setup before publishing |

- Two purchase buttons: an Add to Cart button for customers who keep shopping, and a Direct Buy button that starts checkout straight away through a payment provider. Offering both lets customers choose between a quick purchase and continued browsing. [L0339.001]
- The Direct Buy button shows payment providers based on the visitor's device. On iPhone, Apple Pay is offered by default. On Android, it can offer Google Pay, PayPal or Shopify ShopPay. [L0339.002]
- Place Add to cart and Buy now side by side under the price, and show an express payment button such as PayPal below them. Buyers can then pay without going through the cart first. [L0600.043]
- Direct payment button setup: it needs no manual setup in the template. It is dynamic code that tells Shopify it is a direct payment button and works with the device's available payment providers. [L0339.010]
- After changing PageFly template settings: 1. Save the page. 2. Publish the page. 3. Export the template. [L0339.009]
- Quantity: the default quantity selector can be set to the most common purchase amount (see L0834.039 under Product information settings).

### Bundles, upsells and related products

- Recommendation grid (Source E 2025-26): display product recommendations on product detail pages as a 2x3 grid (2 columns, 3 rows) instead of a single-row slider showing only 2 items. Add a magnifying-glass zoom feature and label the block Curated for you, which signals data-driven logic, rather than Recommended by us, which signals manual curation. [L0197.003]
- Related Products: use the section when offering add-on products that complement the main item. [L0104.011]
- Recommendation blocks such as You may also like are common on competitor product pages. Add them once the store has enough products to recommend. [L0600.012]
- Bundles (Source D 2026), steps:
  1. Install the Kaching Bundles app from the Shopify App Store.
  2. Create a 2-pack and a 3-pack bundle offer.
  3. Generate a niche-specific ebook using Claude.
  4. Design the ebook in Canva.
  5. Add the ebook as a free bonus.
  6. Highlight the bonus on the product page.
  7. Test and track the change in average order value (AOV). [L0860.017]
- Bundle settings in Kaching: Settings > Let customers choose different variants for each item. Turn it off when the product has no variants. The related toggles Hide unavailable variant options and Show variant selection for single quantity deal bar are in the same settings. [L0861.009]
- Settings > Cart > Skip cart and go to checkout directly sends customers straight to checkout after adding a bundle. It is available but not required for the setup. [L0861.010]
- A gift product that shows Out of stock or is unavailable blocks the bundle from offering the free item. Check the gift product's inventory and make sure it is available before the bundle is published. [L0861.027]
- The gift product must be in stock, or the gift cannot be offered. Attach the e-book product as the gift product and do not leave it unattached. [L0862.023]
- Do not use bundle minimums (for example minimum order 2 units) unless they make logical sense for the customer. Sell a 2-pack, such as blinds 2-pack, instead of forcing two individual units. Bundles should address genuine customer needs (2023). [L0251.050]
- Rebuy (Source E 2025-26), setup:
  1. Create the snippet `snippets/rebuyExtensions.liquid` with the code below, replacing 117326 with your own widget ID.
  
```
{% if product %}
<div data-rebuy-id="117326" data-rebuy-shopify-product-ids="{{ product.id }}"></div>
{% endif %}
```
  
  2. The `if product` wrapper makes the widget render only on product pages. Without it, the bundle would also try to load on other page types. [L0557.010, L0557.011]
- Advanced Liquid users can paste the widget div directly into the product template file instead of using a snippet. The snippet method is the simpler default. [L0557.012]
- A manually installed bundle may appear far down the product page, below other content, which is not ideal. Dynamic placement is needed to move it. [L0557.013]
- Dynamic placement (Rebuy):
  1. Open the product page in the storefront, right-click and choose Inspect.
  2. Use the element picker (top-left icon) to hover over the element the bundle should sit above, for example the next widget after the main product.
  3. Copy that element's id attribute.
  4. Return to the widget preview and expand Placement.
  5. Under LARGE SCREENS, enable dynamic placement, set Min Breakpoint to 999, leave Max Breakpoint at None, paste the selector with a leading `#` into Placement Selector, and set Placement Location to Before.
  6. Repeat the same settings under MEDIUM SCREENS and for the remaining screen sizes.
  7. Click Save.
  8. Refresh the product page a few times to confirm the change. [L0557.014]
- A CSS selector that points to an element ID must start with a hash sign, for example `#sidebar` or `#rebuy-widget-117210`. Without the `#`, the placement selector will not find the target. [L0557.015]
- Dynamic placement uses breakpoints: large screens start at a minimum of 999, and medium and other sizes are configured separately. Each size needs its own selector and placement location. [L0557.016]
- Placement location Before puts the dynamic bundle directly before the chosen target element, for example before the product recommendation carousel. [L0557.017]
- Rebuy gives each rendered widget container an element id in the form `rebuy-widget-<widget ID>`. To place one widget relative to another, use that id as the placement selector with a leading `#`. [L0557.022]

### Back order, out-of-stock and pre-order handling

Conflicting sources on out-of-stock products:

| Situation | Source | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Out of stock, keep the page live | L0197.006 (Source E 2025-26) | Keep the page live, remove Add to Cart, keep Notify me when available visible, add a Try one of these instead recommendation block below using the recommendation engine filtered by customer behaviour and purchase history. Implementation: 4 clicks in Visually. |
| Backordered 2+ weeks | L0251.041 (2023) | Keep the product live and do not pause ads. Add a note near Add to Cart: This product is currently backordered. Expected ship date: [date]. |
| Backordered indefinitely, no ETA | L0251.042 (2023) | Either remove the product from the feed entirely or set CPC to $0 to stop ad spend. Optionally set inventory to zero and add a note suggesting similar in-stock alternatives with links. |
| Out of stock, do not delete | L0274.039 (2023) | Do not delete the product. Set CPC to zero in ads, add the note Currently backordered, ETA [date] near Add to Cart, and recommend 2-3 in-stock alternatives. This keeps free organic traffic. |

- Short-term backorders: do not remove the product or pause ads. [L0251.041]
- Indefinite backorders: removing the product from the feed or setting CPC to $0 prevents ad spend. The sources differ on deleting the product, see the table. [L0251.042, L0274.039]
- Back-order note: insert it in the product template near the Add to cart button. [L0856.068]
- Pre-orders with apps (2022, older): Shopify apps can mark products as available for pre-order instead of out-of-stock. Customers reserve items for future delivery without removing stock from the catalogue, which preserves sales from demand that exists before supply arrives. [L0249.099]
- Pre-orders without an app (2023): store pre-orders are handled by direct communication with customers, not by a dedicated app. This is the more recent guidance and differs from the 2022 app-based approach. [L0855.101]
- Products with no back-order or stock status shown on the page can still hide Inventory status (see L0472.029). [L0472.029]

### Page builders: PageFly, GemPages and Builder.io

- PageFly: a Shopify app for building high-conversion product pages with template import and drag-and-drop customisation. The free version lets you publish one page while editing unlimited unpublished pages. Install it from the Shopify app store, then go to Pages > Product Pages. [L0321.001]
- Importing a PageFly template:
  1. Install PageFly from the Shopify app store.
  2. Go to Pages > Product Pages.
  3. Click Import.
  4. Click Select File.
  5. Upload the downloaded template file.
  6. Once uploaded, duplicate the template with a unique product name.
  7. Begin editing images and copy.
  8. Check the mobile version separately.
  9. Hide all default Shopify theme sections by clicking the eye icon next to each.
  10. Publish the PageFly page. The default Shopify theme must stay hidden so only PageFly displays. [L0321.002]
- To hide the default Shopify product page so PageFly appears alone: go to the product, find the default theme section showing product information, and click the eye icon to hide it. Repeat for all non-PageFly sections. Only the PageFly section should be visible. [L0321.003]
- Changing page colours to match the brand in PageFly:
  1. Identify your brand colour, from existing branding or from ChatGPT suggestions.
  2. Locate the first icon or element with the old colour.
  3. Click the element and open the colour picker.
  4. Enter the brand colour as a hex code or RGB value.
  5. If several identical elements exist, copy the style of the updated element and paste it onto the others to apply the change across the page. [L0321.025]
- Layout options: for custom sections with text and images side by side, use Layout > Row and Column options. Common layouts include 2-column, 3-column and 2-row by 3-column. Drag paragraph, image or GIF elements into columns. No custom coding is required. [L0321.026]
- Time to build a high-converting product page: 30 minutes to 1 hour once you know the template and the ChatGPT copywriting workflow. First builds may take longer. [L0321.035]
- Slideshow section:
  1. Drag a Slideshow component into the page section.
  2. Below the slideshow, drag a Paragraph component for descriptive text.
  3. To the side or below the text, drag an Icon component.
  4. Add product images to the slideshow.
  5. Fill the paragraph with feature or benefit descriptions.
  6. Select and style the icon to match the brand.
  7. Repeat for multiple images. PageFly allows this multi-component grouping; default Shopify themes do not. [L0321.046]
- Page builders such as PageFly, Gem Pages and Builder.io allow complex landing page layouts that Shopify themes often cannot provide. PageFly is described as smoother to work with, Gem Pages can be buggy but has good support, and Builder.io is aimed at ecommerce. A page builder that goes down can take the store down with it, so use them carefully. [L0387.017]
- Shopify 2.0 free themes can handle about 75 percent of the landing page customisation described in the lesson. A customisable theme is needed for the rest, and page builders are an option for the remaining elements. [L0387.018]
- GemPages: a custom page builder for branded product and landing pages, with highlighted areas, slider testimonials, bundle package blocks and a mobile-friendly layout. PageFly was also mentioned, but its features were not confirmed in that source. [L0871.018]
- Page builders named for landing pages are PageFly and GemPages, which can build custom product pages. Templates can be imported and images and text replaced. For simple products in home decor, jewellery or fashion, a normal theme is enough. [L0637.035]
- Landing-page build with PageFly (2026): PageFly creates multi-column grids with image, review and rating rows without code. Free Shopify themes such as Debutify or Sense can build basic landing pages but offer less flexibility for custom sections than a dedicated builder. [L0916.031, L0916.032]
- Build a landing page from a pre-built PageFly template:
  1. Open the template in PageFly.
  2. Insert your product into the template.
  3. Replace the product images with your own.
  4. Replace the copy with your product's copy, keeping the benefit lines and badges already in the template.
  5. Keep the included sections for brand comparison, FAQ, reviews and the 30-day money-back guarantee, and check they match your product. [L0916.033]
- Copying an existing page template from a page builder such as PageFly, changing the images and rewriting the copy is a quick way to build a landing page for a new product. PageFly offers a template library, and a plan with a number of page slots was mentioned at around $20 per month. [L0552.015]
- Free-theme vs. page builder: for simple products, a normal theme can be enough, while complex landing pages need a builder. [L0637.035, L0387.018]
- Free-theme split test: run ads for two days on PageFly, then two days on the Source F theme, and compare which performs better. Some products favour PageFly and others the Source F theme. Both are free. [L0773.010]
- Free-gift and offer blocks in PageFly-based pages are handled in the template; see the free-gift procedure under Descriptions and trust elements. [L0469.040]

### Testing and choosing a page approach

- Split-test landing pages and themes with equal ad time per variant before choosing one. [L0773.010]
- Test each added feature (sticky add-to-cart bar, gift option, colour swatches) separately. [L0709.049]
- Copy a competitor's page structure where it works, but measure. Heavy editing can reduce sales. [L0660.021]
- Preview a product page after saving: click Preview at the top, check the customer view (product image, name, price and description), then return to the editor to make changes. [L0036.022]

### Editing the product page and template structure

- In the Shopify admin, go to Sales channels > Online store > Themes and click Edit theme to open the theme editor. [L1192.009]
- In the theme editor, select Homepage at the top, then Products, then the Default product template. This template applies to all products unless another template is assigned to them. [L1192.010]
- Path to the product page settings: Online Store > Themes > Edit theme > choose Products > Default product. Changes made on the default product template apply to all other products in the store. [L1189.006]
- In the Manhattan theme, go to Online Store > Themes, click Customize, and switch the page dropdown at the top from Homepage to Products. All product page layout options are then controlled through the default product template. [L0976.001]
- To reach the section settings on the Default product template, open the section list with the small arrow next to the section name, then click Product information. This is where the sticky media, SKU, price, variant, quantity and buy button options are configured. [L1189.026]
- Do not edit the Default product template unless every product page should change, because it is assigned to all products by default. [L1116.013]

### Template strategy

- Start with only the default product template. Add more product templates later, only when product pages must look different, for example by supplier or for specific products, and assign each template to the matching products. [L0976.002]
- Give each product its own page template so its content, such as collapsible tabs, can be edited without changing the other products. Sharing one template across products causes every edit to spread to all of them. [L1013.005]
- Source D describes one product detail page being reused for a group of 4 to 10 related products, such as books in the same genre or handyman tools. The related products can be launched together or one after another on the same page layout. [L1013.001]
- Separate product page templates let different products have different layouts, such as a quote request page, a free gift offer, a bonus product or a product page without a discount code. Assign the template per product rather than changing the default for all products. [L1287.028]
- Product page templates can differ in more than the delivery window, since any section of the product page can be edited separately for each template. [L1192.029]
- A newly created product template has zero assigned products by default, and it only affects products that are explicitly assigned to it. [L1192.026]
- A product keeps using the default template until a template is assigned to it, so preview each product after assignment to confirm it shows the correct delivery window. [L1192.028]
- Before editing collapsible tabs or other sections, confirm the template selection. Changes made in the wrong template spread to all products that share that template. [L1013.010]
- If a product is left on Default product, any change made in the product template in the theme editor also changes the main product page and every other product using that default template. Always assign the product's own template before editing it. [L1013.009]
- In the theme editor's product template dropdown, a label such as "Assigned to 1 product" shows how many products use the selected template. Before editing, confirm that each individual product template is assigned to exactly one product, so changes stay isolated. [L1013.020]
- Check the Theme template field whenever a product page does not show the blocks you expect. One product can get its own layout while the others keep the default. [L1188.048]

### Creating and assigning product templates

Source D procedure, repeated for each product that needs its own page:

1. In Shopify admin, go to Online Store > Themes and open the live theme with Edit theme.
2. In the theme editor, open the page selector and choose the Product page.
3. Scroll down in the product section and click Create a template.
4. Set the new template to be based on Default product, and name it after the product, for example dollar-apocalypse.
5. Refresh the browser page so the new template appears in the template list.
6. Open the matching product in the Shopify admin, find the Theme template field under Product organization, and select the new template instead of Default product.
7. Click Save in the product admin.
8. Return to the theme editor, open the product using its template preview, make your content changes, and click Save.
9. Repeat steps 2 to 8 for every product that needs its own page.

[L1013.006]

- The template assignment is made in the Shopify product admin under Product organization, in the field Theme template. The default value is Default product, and the value must be changed to the product's own template, otherwise the product keeps using the shared default. [L1013.007]
- In the Product organization section of a Shopify product form, the Theme template field lists Default product and any custom product templates. Select the product's own template there and save the product, otherwise it keeps using the shared default template. [L1013.018]
- To preview a product with its own template in the theme editor, open the product URL and add the parameter view followed by the template name, for example /products/product-handle?view=template-name. The theme editor then shows that template so its sections can be edited. [L1013.008]

Source A procedure (2025), for a supplier or a single product:

1. Go to Online Store > Themes and click Customize on the theme.
2. Click the page selector at the top, which shows Homepage, and choose Products.
3. Open Default product to see the template assigned to all products. Leave it unchanged unless every product should change.
4. Scroll to the bottom of the product template list and click Create template. Name it (only you see the name). It is based on Default product and duplicates it.
5. Add content: hover over a section and click the plus button, or use Add section and pick a block such as Image with text. Upload an image, enter the text, and keep or delete the button.
6. Click Save.
7. Go to Products, open the product, look at the bottom right to see which template it uses, select the new template, and click Save.

Repeat for each supplier or product that needs its own content. [L1116.012]

Source A procedure (2025), for a product template with a descriptive name:

1. Go to Online store and open Customize for the theme.
2. Open the Products section, where the default product page template is shown.
3. Click Create template and give it a descriptive name, such as product page customer image.
4. Add the sections you want, for example an image with text, and click Save.
5. Open a product, find the Theme template field, which shows default product page, and select the new template.
6. Repeat for as many or as few products as you need.

[L1287.027]

Supplier template procedure (Source A):

1. In the theme editor, open the template dropdown at the top and select Default product, then click Products.
2. Scroll to the bottom of the list and click Create template.
3. Name the new template after the supplier, and base it on the default product template.
4. Click create. The editor now shows the new template name at the top, and any changes apply only to products assigned to it.
5. In the Product information section, open estimated delivery and change the window to the supplier's real delivery time, such as 7 to 14 days.
6. Click Save.

[L1192.025]

Assignment procedure (Source A):

1. In the Shopify admin, go to Products and open the product that belongs to the supplier.
2. In the theme template setting, select the supplier's template instead of the default product template.
3. Click Save.
4. Click Preview and confirm the estimated delivery dates changed, then scroll down to check the theme template name shown for the product.

[L1192.027]

### Product page block structure

- The theme editor's Product Details section holds the product-page blocks, such as Title, Price, Add to Cart, Payment icons, Trustpilot Rating, Custom Text, Product FAQ, Customer Review, Video Carousel and Collapsible Tabs. Each block can be reordered, removed or added with the Add block and Add section buttons. [L1188.005]
- Collapsible tabs on a product page are edited in the Product Details section of the product template in the theme editor. This section sits in the template's section list alongside Cart Drawer, Scrolling Features Bar and Store FAQ. [L1013.021]
- Add block options in the Manhattan product page editor include image, text, shipping text, trust badges, countdown timer, collections list, collapsible row, custom liquid, pop-up, product ratings, complementary products and related products. Add them only when needed, since the theme already includes the most common features. [L0976.036]
- In the Manhattan product page editor, blocks you add can be removed with delete, while built-in elements you do not need are removed with hide. A hidden element can be shown again at any time by reversing the toggle and saving. [L0976.046]
- After hiding a product page block in the theme editor and saving, reload the live product page to confirm the block is gone. Scrolling without reloading can still show the old version. [L1189.028]
- The shipping text block on a Shopify product page holds your shipping policy wording, such as an expected delivery window like seven to 10 days. It is placed in the product sidebar by default, and it is optional for a demo store because it is not needed for supplier approval. [L0976.044]
- The collapsible row block has a title, row content that can link to a page such as a shipping policy page, and a selectable icon. Create the linked page first, or link it temporarily to an existing page. [L0976.040]
- The related products block shows products related to the one being viewed. Its layout and contents can be customized, it is recommended to keep it on, and it can be hidden at any time. [L0976.043]
- The related products block has a default heading of "You may also like". The heading, size, number of products and colour scheme can be changed. To hide it, hover over the block and click the eyedropper icon; click the eyedropper again to bring it back. [L1189.007]
- Related products are most useful when the store has add-on products customers might want to buy together. For a demo store without such products, hiding the related products block is acceptable. [L1189.008]
- A sidebar that changes with the product is not available in standard apps or themes as far as the coach knew (older, 2018). It requires custom code, and you build a separate template for each product category. [L1109.040]

### Product information section settings

- Product page sections are edited in a sidebar that opens when you click Product information. Breadcrumbs show the path the visitor took to reach the page; keep them on, and their colour and style can be changed in the same place. [L0976.003]
- The product title and the displayed price cannot be edited in the product page sidebar. Leave those as they are, since changing the title is not recommended anyway. [L0976.017]
- The product title and price are dynamic fields in the Shopify theme and cannot be customized in the theme editor. Changes to them must be made in the product data itself. [L1189.011]
- Keep the SKU number displayed on the product page, because it is pulled from the product data and helps with SEO once the store has live products. [L0976.018]
- SKU field: the default label is "SKU". The label can be renamed, but it is recommended to keep it as SKU. The field is used to show the product's SKU number. [L1189.012]
- The vendor text block is dynamic and pulls the vendor name entered for each product, so new supplier names appear automatically. Keep it on; it can be bolded or given extra text, but that is not necessary. [L0976.016]
- Vendor field: it shows the brand name and carries a dynamic icon, so it automatically displays each product's real brand once live products are uploaded. The demo default text is the brand name of the demo products. [L1189.010]
- In Theme settings you can edit the price label text and turn on currency codes, which show a code such as USD next to prices. Show currency codes only when you sell in several currencies; a store selling to a single country and currency does not need them. [L0976.011]
- Price field: it cannot be customized and should not be hidden. If a product has a compare-at price and a regular price, the compare-at price is shown crossed out above the regular price. [L1189.013]
- The variant picker has two display options: dropdown, a menu for choosing a variant such as a colour, and pills, small buttons showing each variant name such as sizes or colours. Both work; the lesson used pills. [L0976.022]
- Variant picker: set the display style to "pills" for product variations. No other change is needed. [L1189.015]
- Keep the quantity selector on so visitors can order several units from the product page; it can be hidden if you do not want it. [L0976.025]
- Quantity selector: keep the style set to "buttons". [L1189.017]
- The estimated delivery block shows customers how long delivery should take. For a demo store, hide it with the eyedropper icon. [L1189.016]
- Inventory status can be hidden in a demo store, because stock levels are added later. [L1189.020]
- Social sharing buttons: keep them turned on so visitors can share product pages on social media. [L1189.022]
- The description block can be placed at the bottom of the page or on the side, directly under the add to cart buttons. The side option shows the description, key features and specifications in the sidebar. [L0976.029]
- Description block: no changes are needed. It can be placed on the side next to the add-to-cart buttons, which is the preferred placement, or below the product. [L1189.021]
- Keep the dynamic checkout buttons on, which show the payment methods enabled in the store, such as PayPal or Apple Pay, directly on the product page. Turn off the gift card option if you do not sell gift cards. [L0976.026]

### Media and images

- Set Desktop media width to medium and keep Constrain media to screen height on. Media fit has two options, original and fill; choose whichever looks better for your image sizes, and the lesson kept original. [L0976.005]
- Product information section, sticky media settings: turn on "Enable sticky content on desktop" so product images stay in place while the visitor scrolls the text on the right. Set "Desktop media width" to "medium" and turn on "Constrain media to screen height" with the value "original". [L1189.009]
- Set Desktop layout to thumbnail carousel, which shows a scrollable row of thumbnails below the main image when a product has several images. This setting is recommended. [L0976.006]
- Image behaviour on hover or click has three options: nothing, zoom (magnifies on hover) and pop-up (opens a larger view). Zoom suits high-resolution photos; low-resolution or AI-generated demo images look poor when zoomed, so choose the option that fits your image quality. [L0976.007]
- For the mobile layout, set the mobile image behaviour to none for AI-generated, low-resolution demo images, and set the mobile media display to show thumbnails. [L0976.008]
- To adjust image zoom on product pages, go to Shopify admin > Online Store > Themes > Customize. Change the dropdown from Homepage to Product page, and then use the left navigation to set the zoom options. [L0965.060]
- Product image behaviour in the theme editor: under Online Store > Customize > Products > Default product, change the image settings (for example how media fits) and save, then check the result on the store. [L0971.051]
- Images added to the product description should scale automatically for both desktop and mobile. If they look wrong on one device, check how the image was uploaded, because a correctly uploaded image resizes itself. [L0970.060]
- Lazy loading on product variant images can stop images from loading. If this happens, contact the page builder support (PageFly in this case) and report that images are not loading on the page while they work elsewhere. [L0950.029]
- Avoid putting videos in the product media, because they slow down page load times. If you do use a video, the loop-after-end option restarts it automatically once it finishes. [L0976.009]
- Short animated GIFs for product pages can be made by cutting a 1 to 3 second segment from an existing video ad and exporting it as a GIF. Keep these GIFs under the speed limits for images (around 800 KB) to avoid slowing the page. [L1259.066]
- Add GIFs or short demos to the product description so customers understand how the product works. Visual explanation shortens the path to purchase. [L1171.032]

### Product descriptions and collapsible tabs

Making a collapsible description section without code (Source A):

1. In the product editor, highlight the heading text in the description, for example Specs.
2. Open the dropdown above the description box in the rich text editor, which by default shows Paragraph.
3. Select Heading 6, which turns the highlighted text into a Heading 6.
4. The heading becomes the visible top tab, and all content below it up to the next chosen heading sits inside the collapsible section.

[L0921.005]

- If you do not know code, use the rich text editor for headings and collapsible sections instead of editing the HTML. When a store suddenly looks broken, check first whether recently pasted code caused it. [L0921.008]

Creating expandable sections (Source A, 2025):

1. Type the section title in the product description, for example Shipping, followed by its text.
2. Highlight the title text.
3. Open the format dropdown and choose Heading 6.
4. Make the title bold to match the other titles.
5. Click Save and preview. The title is now a collapsible section.

Repeat for each section. Description is expanded by default. [L1116.015]

- Only text formatted as Heading 6 becomes its own collapsible section. Plain text stays inside the default Additional info section. [L1116.016]

Changing an existing description section into an expandable block (Manhattan theme):

1. Open the product in the Shopify admin and go to the description editor.
2. Select the heading of the section you want to collapse, e.g. Key features, and change its format from Heading 3 to Heading 6.
3. Save the product.
4. Repeat step 2 for every other section heading that should collapse, e.g. Why you'll love it and Specifications.
5. Delete the horizontal divider lines that were created between the sections, then save again.
6. Open the product page preview to check the result.
7. If the description intro has no heading above it, add a heading such as Description and format it as Heading 6 so it becomes the first expandable block.

[L1189.001]

- In the Manhattan theme, any text set as Heading 6 becomes an expandable and collapsible block that controls everything beneath it until the next Heading 6. Each block that should collapse needs its own Heading 6 heading. [L1189.002]
- The first Heading 6 block on a product description is expanded by default, so the visitor sees at least one section open. Plan the first section so the most important text is visible without a click. [L1189.003]
- The editor view can look broken after a heading is changed to Heading 6, but the product page shows it correctly. Always judge the result in the saved product page preview, not in the editor. [L1189.004]
- Leftover horizontal divider lines between description sections make the collapsible layout look messy. Delete them and save again before previewing. [L1189.005]
- Build three collapsible tabs for the product page, usually labelled How it works, What's inside and Satisfaction guarantee. Each tab should answer one buying question, such as what the buyer gets, how it is used and what protects the purchase. [L1188.007]
- Place detailed product content such as description, ingredients, how it is made and what is in the box inside collapsible drop-down menus. This keeps SEO-rich text on the page without overwhelming the customer. [L1225.032]
- Product page sections such as measurements, shipping, returns and price match are fine in moderation, and four to five dropdowns or headers is not too many. [L0970.078]
- Product description order: set up the product description and features in that order. The description goes first because Merchant Center reads it first, and the features follow for extra keywords. [L0964.091]
- Use the supplier's product description as a starting point, but check that it is complete and accurate before publishing the product page. [L1171.031]
- Write unique product sales copy for each product rather than using supplier copy. Add size, colour and variant charts, and offer downloadable PDF files such as warranty terms and installation instructions. [L0920.025]
- Shopify's built-in AI suggestions for product descriptions should not be accepted blindly, because they do not follow a product page SEO approach. Use them only as a starting point and optimise the result yourself. [L0966.064]
- Write the product map first, then fill the product description field in the Shopify product editor from it. Starting from a blank description box with no guide is what causes writer's block, so the map gives you the material to fill that field. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1160.019]
- Starting a product description for a test store (procedure):

1. Open the description of a proven competitor product.
2. Copy its structure, such as the sections and their order.
3. Rewrite the wording and improve it for your own store, keeping the structure that the competitor uses.

[L1172.047]

- Build specification tables with Shopify's native editor first. Third-party apps can slow the site, so use custom HTML only if the native editor cannot produce the table. [L0970.065]
- Embed a supplier video in a product description by copying its embed code, typically from YouTube, and pasting it into the description. No developer is needed for this step. (Older, 2021.) [L0968.043]
- Bullet points in the product description that wrap onto a second line can break their indentation in Shopify. A line of CSS from the Shopify forum can fix the layout. [L0966.063]
- Standard Shopify variants share one product description, so a description that differs by colour will not change when the customer selects another variant. Solving this needs an app or custom code, so test each variant in the preview before launch. [L1287.030]

Older tab setup with the Easy Tabs app (Source B, 2021-22):

1. Open the Easy Tabs app and start the free trial if prompted.
2. Under Standard tabs, click Configure default tab, which builds standard tabs from the product description.
3. Choose the tab title for the default tab (for example Description) or keep the text before the first heading as separate text above the tabs.
4. Click Next, then choose the text below the tabs or select Don't need text below the tabs.
5. Click Next and check the live preview.
6. Under Static Tabs, click Add Static Tab, enter the title and content for each tab (shipping and why buy from us at minimum), and click Save static tab.
7. Open Settings and adjust Layout (padding and colours), then click Save.

[L1300.014]

- Standard tabs are built from the product description: each heading tagged as H5 becomes a tab title, and the content after that heading becomes the tab content. The app splits the product description into separate tabs this way. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1300.015]
- Text placed before the first heading can either become a default tab with its own tab title, such as Description, or stay as separate text above the tabs widget. Choose one option deliberately so the first thing a shopper reads is clear. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1300.016]

### Variants, buttons and add-to-cart

- Use Shopify's default variant buttons for colour options. They show the colour name and change the product image on click. True colour swatches require custom code or a swatch app. [L0966.062]
- Native Shopify does not change the add-to-cart button colour based on the selected variant. This needs a third-party app and is not a standard feature. [L1116.019]
- Variants shown in the video must be set up as real product variants in Shopify, because the shopper selects a variant in the video and checks out through the store's native checkout. A variant that is not configured in the store cannot be sold through the video. [L0959.011]
- The add to cart button text changes to "view cart" after the button is clicked; keep that setting on. Avoid heavy customisation of the buttons before supplier approval, because little is needed at that stage. [L0976.028]
- Buy buttons settings: turn on "Show dynamic checkout buttons". Keep the two options below it off. "Customise add to cart" and "Enable add to cart icon" are optional; if turned on, the button colours can be changed under the buy buttons settings. [L1189.018]
- The Buy with Shop Pay button only appears on a store when Shopify Payments is enabled for that store. Demo or test stores without Shopify Payments will not show it. [L0972.019]
- Model the product page structure on the top two or three competitors selling the same product. If they use one page with variants, use one page with variant options; if they use separate pages per size, do the same. Google Merchant Center can still submit each variant as its own product. [L0970.163]

### Cart behaviour

- The cart type options are dropdown confirmation, slide-out cart, pop-up modal, redirect to the cart page, and skip cart and go directly to checkout. Dropdown confirmation is a suitable default when starting out, and the other options can be tested later. [L0976.014]
- After add-to-cart, most stores send shoppers straight to checkout. Keep them on the product page only when customers are likely to add several items. [L0970.075]
- Sticky add-to-cart bar, Manhattan theme: go to Online Store > Themes, click Customise next to Manhattan, open Product and the default product page, click the Buy buttons block, switch off "Sticky add to cart", and click Save. [L0972.017]

### Product page layout and conversion

- About 90-95% of store traffic lands on the product page, and only about 5-10% goes to the homepage or collection pages. The product page is therefore the most important page to optimise. [L1217.038]
- For a niche store with many similar products, a single-product landing page usually converts better than a collection page, because it removes choice friction and continues the emotional journey from the ad. Variants can cover near-identical products. [L1158.029]
- Pre-fill the selector with the most popular choices so about 80% is already chosen and the customer keeps about 20% agency. This reduces the feeling of filling out a form from scratch. [L1225.021]
- For a variant selector with many options and product cards, start with a drop-down rather than a pop-up. A pop-up is harder to use for a complex choice and can hide the options a shopper needs to compare. [L1225.092]

Product page order (Source E):

1. Show the product title.
2. Show a short two to three line description that explains the product.
3. Show reviews.
4. Show the variant or option selector in a compact form, on one line where possible.
5. Place the add-to-cart button above the fold, with the price visible near it.

[L1225.027]

- Below the product box, add landing-page style sections that tell the brand or product story, such as multi-column, image and text, rich text or video sections. These can be built with Shopify section tools. [L1225.033]
- Map a product page to the proven layout in this order: a hero with title bar, brand name, rating line, product name, price with savings label, three checkmark benefit bullets and Add to Cart with payment icons. Then add a See it in action area with 3 to 6 product videos, 3 customer testimonials, feature blocks, a 6-question FAQ, and 3 collapsible tabs. [L1188.006]
