- Keep the product page simple: product title, short benefit bullets, price and a few proven blocks. Switching on every section the theme offers adds mobile load and more copy to maintain for each product. [L1188.052]
- Before publishing, check the product page on mobile as well as desktop: confirm the price, Add to Cart button, carousel, collapsible tabs and FAQ all display, and that font sizes and spacing are readable. [L1188.046]

Template for mapping a product page (paste the data and attached screenshots below it):

```
I am giving you the product page template I want (hero with title bar, brand name, rating line, product name, price with savings, three checkmark bullets, Add to Cart with payment icons, See it in action section with three image placeholders, What our customers say with three testimonial cards, two feature blocks with headline and body, a Shop Now button, six FAQ questions, three collapsible tabs titled How It Works, What's Inside and Satisfaction Guarantee, a floating review bubble above the collapsibles, and a newsletter section). Using the product data and images I provide, fill every element of this template exactly, keeping the same order and the same number of blocks. Do not invent statistics, review counts, sales figures, testimonials, guarantees or claims that are not in the data I provide; if a field has no data, write [MISSING: field name] instead. Mark any figure you took from the data with its source. Testimonials must be rewritten from real customer reviews I provide and must not be fabricated. Write the output as clean copy with headings per section, ready to paste into the theme editor. Product name: [PRODUCT NAME]. Price: [PRICE]. Compare-at price: [COMPARE-AT PRICE]. Product images and screenshots: [ATTACHED]. Product data and reviews: [PASTE DATA].
```

[L1188.031]

Procedure for building the page from a marketplace listing:

1. Open the product page on the marketplace listing and select all text with Ctrl+A.
2. Copy the selection with Ctrl+C.
3. Open a new Claude chat, paste the copied listing data, and attach the reference screenshots.
4. Paste the page template prompt below the data and send it.
5. Check every price, review count and sold count in the output against the live listing and the current price.
6. Copy each output section into its matching block in the theme editor and save.

[L1188.054]

- AI-generated product page copy often carries stale numbers copied from an earlier product snapshot, such as old review counts, old sold counts or old prices. Before publishing, re-check every number against the current product data and the live price. [L1188.033]
- Video Carousel block: separate switches for "Enable autoplay" and "Show navigation dots", and the pagination dots can be placed below the carousel. Autoplay can distract from the buying decision, so test it on mobile before keeping it on. [L1188.050]

### Reviews, trust and FAQ blocks

- Customer review block settings: Avatar image, Review content, Reviewer name, Rating, Star colour and size, Background, Text colour, Drop shadow, and carousel settings for "Enable carousel mode", "Show multiple reviews in a swipeable carousel", and Navigation dots placement. Set each reviewer's name and avatar to match the gender of the reviewer so the review looks real. [L1188.010]
- Trustpilot rating block: set the Rating Text (for example Excellent), the rating value, and the Trustpilot Star colour. The lesson's advice was to use Trustpilot's official green when showing its badge, because it is their brand colour. [L1188.011]
- Product FAQ block options: the "Use product description for question" toggle fills answers from the product description instead of manual answers, and FAQ Appearance covers padding, margins, border radius, border colour and border style. Store FAQ in the footer or sections can use Background Image Mode and Desktop Layout Options such as centring the FAQ when there is no image. [L1188.009]
- Keep the review stars or review block in a visible place. Older method (2019): moving review stars or a review block under the product title in a Shopify theme:

1. Before editing code, check the review app's settings, since many apps can show ratings directly under the price or title without code changes.
2. If the app has no such option, open the theme's code editor and open the product.liquid file.
3. Select all of the file's code, paste it into a document, and save it as a backup.
4. Find the block that renders the reviews; the code differs for each review app.
5. Cut that block and paste it directly below the title markup.
6. Save and check the product page; if it breaks, paste the backup back and save.

[L0960.001]

- Each brand's warranty should appear on every product page for that brand. Describe its scope clearly, such as parts only, so buyers know what they get. (Older, 2020.) [L0961.050]
- Warranty and product claims: show the real product condition so buyers do not expect a finished item. [L0961.048]
- The Image with text block in a product template can carry an image, a brand name, a short brand description and a button. Keep the button and link it to a collection that holds that brand's products, or delete it and keep only the text blocks. [L1116.059]
- The Source A Shopify theme includes an ETA feature that shows the estimated delivery date on the product page. The ETA can update in real time. [L1130.021]

### Assembly, installation and supplier-specific pages

- For products that need assembly, state clearly on the product page that assembly is required and give an estimated assembly time, such as four hours. Show the real product condition so buyers do not expect a finished item. (Older, 2020.) [L0961.048]
- Products requiring professional installation should state on the product page that they must be installed by a professional. Customers typically hire a local installer; offering installation as a service means finding nationwide installation companies and pricing them, which is more manual work. [L0971.076]
- For a custom feature such as an extra multi-line text field under the product title, you usually need a small amount of extra code. Hire a Shopify developer on StoreTasker to build it. [L0965.061]
- Product Options is a Shopify app that adds customisable option fields, such as size, colour or personalisation inputs, to product pages. [L1007.001]
- Product pages use separate tabs named Description, Shipping and Price match. For products from non-MAP suppliers, hide the Price match tab on the product page and list the excluded brands at the top of the general price match policy page. (Older, 2020.) [L0967.050]

### Video on product pages

- Path to add a video to a product description: go to Products, open the product, and use the Description editor's code view (the </> button) to paste the script. Add a short paragraph such as an explanation of the shoppable video, then click Save. If you do not want the snippet in the description, use a Custom HTML snippet block instead. [L0959.007]
- LyveCom embed options: the Widget tab offers the embed code in two steps, Step 1 Copy the code and Step 2 Paste the code on any page of the website. The Embed option (direct embed) is the alternative to the floating widget and is placed where the video should appear, such as a product description. [L0959.004]
- The widget snippet copied from the LyveCom dashboard has this structure. The key name for the language setting was hard to read in the source screenshot, so verify it against the copy from the dashboard before publishing.

```
<script src='https://dashboard.lyvecom.com/widget/widget.js'></script>
<script>
LyveComWidget.mount({
  account: 'YOUR_ACCOUNT_ID',
  stories: true,
  lang: 'en',
  position: 'left'
});
</script>
```

[L0959.005]

- The snippet can be placed in a product description, the Shopify page builder, a Shogun builder, or any block that accepts an HTML snippet. The floating widget suits homepage greetings and new collection launches, while the product-page embed suits engagement on a single product. [L0959.008]
- Show the See it in action section with 3 to 6 short videos that show the product in use, and include them in the product page carousel with navigation dots. Upload the video files directly in the theme block rather than linking to outside pages. [L1188.008]
- Video placement, reconciled: the main media gallery should avoid video because it slows page load [L0976.009], while the See it in action section is the place for 3 to 6 product videos [L1188.008]. Use the gallery for images and the dedicated video section for demonstrations.

### Bundles and upsells

- Each product should have its own unique add-on option, and add-ons should come from the same supplier where possible to simplify shipping. (Older, 2022.) [L1092.049]
- Google Shopping submits the bundle price as the product price. Use add-on options on the product page instead of default bundles, so the listed price stays lower while average order value still rises. [L0970.080]
- Shrine theme supports bundles where a customer can choose different variants, such as size or colour, of the same product in a bundle. It does not upsell a different product, so a hoodie bundle can offer another colour or size but not a t-shirt. [L1173.018]
- PageFly is recommended for stores with one product plus an upsell, because that product is communicated most. It is not recommended for stores with many products. [L1131.028]

### Page builders (PageFly)

- PageFly is a page builder used to build advertorial pages. It offers templates for blog-style layouts that can be customised to look like a native editorial article. [L1131.027]
- Pagefly is used as the page builder for the store pages (homepage, product page, About us, Contact us), and Ryviu is used to add product reviews. [L1288.004]
- Whether to build a PageFly landing page depends on the store: a general store selling multiple items with offers like buy two, get the third free may not need one, while testing one product at a time in a general store does benefit from a PageFly landing page. [L1212.039]

Advertorial page structure:

1. Start with the problem the ad showed, for example a pet losing energy.
2. Educate with facts about the problem and why it happens.
3. Suggest general solutions the reader could try on their own.
4. Introduce your product at the end as the shortcut.
5. Add a clear call-to-action button, such as "Choose your package", that links to the offer page.

[L1131.029]

### Back orders

Back-order delivery note, Source A (2019, older):

> This product is currently on back order until [date]. You can place your order now and pay a deposit of [amount], and we will ship it to you as soon as it arrives.

[L1069.022]

Showing a different delivery note on one product (for example a back order with a long ETA):

1. Go to Online store > Themes > Customize.
2. Open the product page dropdown and choose Products.
3. Click Create template and name it, for example "ETA example two months".
4. In that template, hide the estimated delivery section and add a Text block under the Buy it now button.
5. Save.
6. On the product itself, scroll to Theme template and select the new template, then preview it.

[L1285.031]

Back-order text (template):

```
Please note, this product is back ordered with an estimated arrival of [date]. You can still place your order, and we will confirm the shipping date by email after purchase.
```

[L1285.032]

- Place a back-order ETA note directly beside the add-to-cart button, not lower on the page, so shoppers see the delivery delay before they buy. [L1285.104]

### Out-of-stock and discontinued products

- Do not delete a product page when the product is discontinued or unavailable. Set the inventory to zero so no one can add it to the cart. Add a section under the add-to-cart button that says the product is currently unavailable and links to similar in-stock products in the same price range that ship fast. If the supplier replaces the product with a new model, add a prominent note on the old page naming the new model, with a link and a statement that it is in stock and ships fast. [L1287.019]

Discontinued product with a replacement:

1. Keep the old product page live with inventory set to zero.
2. Create a new product page for the replacement version and submit it to shopping campaigns and free listings.
3. Add a section below the cart button on the old page saying it was discontinued, with a link to the in-stock replacement, so existing SEO traffic is not lost.

[L1285.033]

Replacement note (template):

```
This product has been discontinued. Our updated version, [Replacement product name], is in stock and ready to ship: [link]. You may also like these in-stock options: [Product 1], [Product 2], [Product 3].
```

[L1285.034]

Discontinued product with no replacement:

1. Set its inventory to zero on the store.
2. Exclude it from shopping campaigns and organic listings.
3. Keep the page and add a section saying it has been discontinued, with two or three similar in-stock products customers might consider. This keeps the SEO traffic and gives visitors somewhere to buy.

[L1285.035]

### Pre-orders

Pre-order model when stock is unavailable, for example before Chinese New Year:

1. Change the Add to cart button on the product page so it reads "Pre-order".
2. Add a description explaining that the product sold out due to demand and when shipments restart, for example in two to three weeks.
3. Take payment now and ship when stock arrives.

[L1214.012]

- To change the button label to Pre-order, edit the button text in the page builder or theme editor if you use a page builder or a current theme. On an old legacy theme, the label has to be changed manually in the Shopify theme settings. [L1214.013]
- Changing the pre-order button text on the Shopify theme (older theme method, admin path as given in the source):

1. In the Shopify admin, open Online Store and then the theme you use (check the exact label in your theme editor).
2. Open the theme settings and the language editing area, and find the button text for buy now.
3. Change the button text from "buy now" to "pre-order now".
4. Note that this changes the button for the whole store, not just one product, so check every product page afterward.

[L1017.026]

- For a pre-order product, make the status clear on the product page by starting the product description with a large red line such as "pre-order now". Keep the setup manual and avoid installing extra apps, because each app can slow the store down. [L1017.027]
- Pre-orders can sell well, especially in fashion, where many products are bought before stock arrives. Do not drop the model based on claims that pre-orders do not work. [L1214.014]

### Product page as the main conversion page

- About 95% of paid-ad visitors see only the product page, while only 3 to 5% visit the home page or collection page, so most design and offer effort belongs there. [L1410.030, L1412.048]
- The product page shows most product details and can be customized heavily through the theme. A collection page displays every product assigned to that collection. [L1326.017]
- Before traffic is sent to a product page, it should include product images or videos, customer reviews and an FAQ section. [L1324.006]
- Prioritize the landing page over checkout tweaks. A strong landing page builds the trust that brings buyers to checkout. Shipping information and similar details are common checkout blockers, to be addressed after the page works. [L1363.031]
- Every extra page or click between the landing page and checkout lowers conversion and increases frustration and abandonment. Shorten the path to purchase wherever possible, because making shoppers browse longer does not help when attention spans are short. [L1522.006]
- (Older, 2021-22) Copy belongs on product pages, longer sales pages built with funnel tools such as ClickFunnels or Leadpages, collection and category pages, and marketplace listings on Amazon or eBay. Shopify is named as the standard example of a product-page setup. [L1460.008]
- When traffic comes from TikTok, build the store mobile first. The lesson estimates that nearly all TikTok buyers shop on a phone, so the desktop layout can receive less attention. [L1412.014]

### Page structure and conversion elements

- Top of the product page: product image, product name, USP, star reviews, then four or five benefit-driven bullet lines. Lower sections repeat the pattern: a main benefit headline, up to two lines of text, then an image or GIF. [L1345.046]
- Add a FAQ section for problem-solving products, since almost every buyer has questions. [L1345.047]
- Add a risk-free or 30-day money-back guarantee section, which works well for conversion. [L1345.047]
- Remove every element unrelated to the main product, such as a "frequently bought together" block or an unrelated suggestion, because these act as friction and lead visitors away. [L1345.053]
- Show a clear size guide on every product page, and show the shape and fit of each item through mock-ups or model images. For oversized or drop-shoulder cuts, make the fit visible so buyers know what will arrive. [L1438.022]
- Provide accurate measurements for every product category, such as chest width, arm length and shoulder drop for tops, and the equivalent measures for trousers and jackets. Make them visible on the website before checkout. [L1438.023]
- Accurate sizing reduces returns because buyers already know what they are getting. Sizing errors discovered after delivery are a common cause of returns in fashion. [L1438.024]
- Spacing shows which items belong together: related items should sit closer to each other than to unrelated items. [L1462.006]

### Product description formatting

Format a product description for a short, sales-focused layout:

1. Paste the AI copy into the description editor.
2. Click Paragraph and then Clear formatting.
3. If formatting is still broken, paste the text into Google first to strip formatting, then copy it back.
4. Turn three key points into a bulleted list.
5. Click Preview and cut any text that pushes the Add to cart button too far down. [L1331.016]

### Collapsible tabs

#### Tab types and when to use them

- Product pages can use tabs for the product description, the shipping policy and, if offered, the price match policy. This lets the buyer find all the information without leaving the page. Path: product template in the Shopify theme, tabbed content section. [L1346.010]
- Standard tabs are unique for each product, and their content is written directly in Shopify's native Edit Product page. Use this option only when each product needs its own set of tabs. [L1300.018]
- Static tabs are created once in the app settings and then added to all products or to selected collections. Use them for content that is the same across products, such as shipping terms or a reviews tab. [L1300.020]
- (Older, 2021-22) Content below the tabs appears under the tabs widget on every product page of the store. If you do not need it, select Don't need text below the tabs. [L1300.017]
- (Older, 2021-22) For a new store, start with two static tabs: Shipping and Why Buy From Us. Add further tabs, such as reviews, later once that content exists. [L1300.021]
- (Older, 2021-22) Once the app is installed, tabs are enabled on product descriptions automatically. If they do not show on the product page, open the Installation section for manual steps. The Disable Tabs button turns them off. [L1300.019]

#### Adding a static tab (older, 2021-22)

1. Open the Static Tabs section in the Easy Tabs settings.
2. Click Add Static Tab; an edit window opens.
3. Enter a title for the tab.
4. Paste in the content and replace the placeholder business name and other details with your own.
5. Click Save static tab.
6. Check the list: each static tab shows its title, Assigned To (for example All Products) and Edit and Delete options. [L1300.022]

#### Layout, padding and colors (older, 2021-22)

- Under Settings > Layout, the TABS LAYOUT options are Tabs and Accordion. The live preview changes with each option, so compare them before choosing. The Tabs layout is the default and was kept in the example. [L1300.025]
- Under Settings > Layout > Padding, the example uses 12 px for Tabs Padding (Tab), 12 px for Tab Title and 12 px for Content Padding (Content Box, with values of 12 px and 10 px), and 12 px for Content. A Reset layout settings button restores the defaults. [L1300.026]
- Under Settings > Layout > Colors, Active Tab has a Background Color and Text Color, and Inactive Tab has a Background Color, Text Color and Border Color. In the example, the inactive tabs used the dark brand brown with white text, and the active tab used the brand yellow as its background. [L1300.028]
- The live preview shows every layout and color change before it is saved. Check the preview first, then click Save. [L1300.029]
- The Easy Tabs app's top navigation has Home, Settings, Installation, FAQ and More Apps. The Home page shows whether tabs are enabled on product descriptions and holds the Disable Tabs button. [L1300.037]

### Product templates for product types and suppliers

- Editing the Default product template changes every product that uses it. Create a separate template for products that need different images or sections. [L1331.031]
- Create a separate template for a new product type, such as sandals:
  1. Go to Products and create a new template.
  2. Base it on the Default product template.
  3. Assign the product to the new template in its Theme template field.
  4. Change the images and sections in that template.
  
  Editing it does not change products that still use Default product. [L1331.030]
- Show product-specific content through a custom product page template rather than by editing the main theme code files. Changing the main files can easily break the whole store, while a separate template only affects the products assigned to it. [L1419.007]
- A product that already uses another custom product template, such as a delivery-time template, loses that template when you assign a new one. Check the theme template field on the product first and note any layout you need to keep, because the new template replaces the old one for that product only. [L1419.014]

### Variant descriptions

#### When to use them

- Variant-specific descriptions are only worth setting up when the variants differ enough that one shared description does not fit them. If the variants share the same details, a single default description covering all variants works without extra setup. [L1419.001]

#### Setup procedure

Create the variant metafield definition. Setting: Settings > Metafields and metaobjects > Variants (under Metafield definitions) > Add definition. Name the definition 'variant description' and set Select type to Rich text, then click Save. Shopify creates the key custom.variant_underscore_description, so the name must match exactly, including spelling, lowercase and underscores. [L1419.002]

1. Log into the Shopify admin.
2. Click Settings.
3. Click Metafields and metaobjects.
4. Scroll to the bottom of the list and click Variants under Metafield definitions.
5. Click Add definition.
6. Enter the name 'variant description'.
7. Click Select type and choose Rich text (search for it if it is not visible).
8. Click Save.
9. Check that the Metafields and metaobjects overview now shows one definition for variants instead of zero. [L1419.003]

Enter a description for each variant:

1. Go to Products and open a product that has variants.
2. Leave the product page and open it again so the variant fields refresh.
3. Scroll down to the Variants section and expand the list.
4. Click one variant, for example 'seven foot blue'.
5. Scroll to the field named variant description and click it to open the text box.
6. Enter the description for that variant and click Save.
7. Repeat for every other variant.

To edit later, follow the same path and change the text. [L1419.004]

Create the snippet that reads the variant description:

1. Go to Online Store > Themes.
2. Click the three-dot button next to the theme you use and choose Edit code.
3. Expand the Snippets folder to see the existing snippets.
4. Click the first icon in the toolbar, New file.
5. Name the file variant_description.liquid.
6. Open the new file, paste the snippet code, and click Save.

The file name has to match the name used in the Custom Liquid block later. [L1419.005]

Create the custom product page template:

1. Go to Online Store > Themes and click Customize (not Edit code).
2. In the top dropdown, choose Home page, then select Products.
3. Open the template dropdown at the top. Most stores only show the template named default product.
4. Click to create a new template, name it 'variants with unique descriptions', and base it on the default product template.
5. Click Create template. [L1419.008]

- A newly created product template is assigned to zero products, so creating it does not change any live product page. It only takes effect on products you assign to it manually. [L1419.009]

Prepare the template layout:

1. In the left panel of the new template, hide the Description section so the default description no longer shows.
2. Hover just below the Description area until a blue line with a plus sign appears.
3. Click Add block and choose Custom liquid.
4. In the code box that opens on the right, enter: render variant description
5. Scroll down in the preview to check that the first variant's description appears, then click Save. [L1419.010]

- The Custom Liquid block displays code you type into it. The entry 'render variant description' tells it to load the snippet variant_description.liquid, so the block name in the code must match the snippet file name without the.liquid extension. [L1419.011]

Assign the template to the product:

1. Go to Products and open the product that has the unique variant descriptions.
2. Scroll down on the right side to the theme template setting, which shows the template the product currently uses.
3. Click the current template and select 'variants with unique descriptions'.
4. Click Save.

Only the products you assign change. Every other product page keeps its previous template. [L1419.012]

Test the variant descriptions:

1. Open the product and click Preview.
2. Scroll to the product details and click each variant option, for example seven foot blue.
3. Check that the description and the image change to match the selected variant.
4. Click the other variant options, such as eight foot green, and check each one. [L1419.013]

#### Naming rule

- Field names, the definition name and the snippet file name must be typed exactly. Differences in spelling or capital letters break the connection between the metafield, the snippet and the template. Copy the names from the lesson text rather than typing them from memory. [L1419.006]

### Product information settings

- (Older, 2021-22) In the Shopify theme editor's product settings: Sale badge style set to Save $10, Option style set to Selectable boxes as stated in the lesson, Display stock levels set to Hidden, and Quick shop button set to Disabled. [L1371.018]
- (Older, 2021-22) Enable color swatches in the Shopify theme product settings. Leave other product settings at their defaults when the storefront is in the early stage. [L1371.022]
- In the Shrine theme's product information settings, Active variant images filtering controls which variant images appear. The Hide other variants' media toggle only works when each variant has one media item assigned in Shopify admin under Products. If you need to hide several media items per variant, use the filtering setting. [L1331.041]

### Buttons and cart behavior

- For a simple single-product store, the main button can read 'Add to bag' or 'I want it now' and send the visitor straight to checkout. This skips the cart page, which can be a point where visitors leave. [L1345.055]
- A sticky button lets a visitor who is already convinced act immediately instead of scrolling back or leaving. Without a visible call to action, visitors who are almost ready can leave before they buy. [L1345.058]
- Case: a store's add-to-cart rate was lower than expected, and visitors who read the product but did not see a button on mobile were leaving. Action: a sticky add-to-cart button was added to the mobile product page. Result: the add-to-cart rate rose by about 25%. [L1345.057]
- (Older, 2021-22) Disable the Quick shop button in the Shopify theme product settings. Quick shop opens a popup with product details and adds JavaScript that slows the site even when customers do not click it, so customers should go to the full product page instead. [L1371.020]
- (Older, 2021-22) Show the dynamic checkout button in the Shopify theme product settings so each customer sees their preferred payment method, such as PayPal or Apple Pay, from those available on the store. Set the Add to cart button to Enable on desktop only. [L1371.021]

### Bundles and upsells

- A bundle app that requires code must be placed in the product page template. The lesson describes this as a required step when using Bundle Bear. Apps that need no code avoid this manual edit. [L1412.013]
- The rule to remove "frequently bought together" blocks from the product page [L1345.053] and the bundle app placement in the product template [L1412.013] apply to different situations: the first concerns unrelated suggestions that create friction, the second concerns a bundle offer that the product page must present. [L1345.053, L1412.013]

### Out-of-stock and zero-stock handling

#### Keeping the page live with zero stock

- For products from a failing supplier, go to the Shopify product page and turn on inventory tracking. Set the stock to zero (out of stock) instead of deleting the product, so customers cannot buy it. [L1407.009]
- (Older, 2021-22) Set a product to zero stock instead of hiding it. The product stays visible on the front end but cannot be bought, avoids indexing problems, and goes live again when stock is added. [L1414.023]
- Keeping out-of-stock product pages live keeps them indexed and receiving free organic traffic. The links on them can redirect visitors to in-stock products that still sell. [L1407.011]

| Option | Result | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Inventory tracking on, stock set to zero | Product stays live, cannot be bought, goes live again when stock is added | [L1407.009, L1414.023] |
| Hide the product (older advice) | Compared with zero stock, hiding is the option that causes indexing problems | [L1414.023] |
| Delete the product | Not recommended; the lesson says to set zero stock instead of deleting | [L1407.009] |

#### Out-of-stock note template

Place this note above the product description on every out-of-stock product page. Keep the links to 1 to 3 products with similar color, size, quality and price.

```
This product is currently out of stock with no ETA. If you're looking for something similar that is in stock and can ship within 24 to 48 hours, here are our recommendations: [link to similar in-stock product 1] [link to similar in-stock product 2] [link to similar in-stock product 3].
```

[L1407.010]

### Page builders: PageFly and GemPages

#### Choosing a builder

- Custom page builders such as PageFly or GemPages let you change background colors and layout to highlight offers more than theme settings do. Use the theme's own options first if they are enough for the highlight. [L1345.018]
- PageFly is a page builder used to create product pages in Shopify. Build one product page, duplicate it for each new product, then rewrite the copy and replace the imagery. This keeps a multi-product test store consistent without rebuilding each page. [L1428.027]
- PageFly landing page templates are used to build high-converting product pages with proven copywriting structure and trust elements. [L1436.039]
- PageFly is used to build landing pages for the store. It helps improve the store's look without needing advanced design skills. [L1439.038]

#### Duplicating a proven page

1. Import a page via Pages (import the template as in the course's import video).
2. Select the page checkbox and choose Duplicate to copy it for a new product.
3. Assign the copy to that product.
4. Duplicate a proven page and tweak it only slightly for products in the same niche, so products can be launched quickly. [L1363.032]

- PageFly gives one free page publish. Each additional page costs about 20 USD. PageFly support may reduce this for e-commerce students who plan multiple Shopify stores; the lesson reported this worked for at least one student. [L1363.033]
- When removing a PageFly element, check that it is removed on all devices (desktop and mobile), because it can remain on one device. [L1363.034]
- On a single-niche store, use global styles (font families and colour scheme) in PageFly so one change updates every page. On a general store testing many products, set custom colours on each page or element instead, so each product can get its own colours quickly. [L1363.062]

#### Workflow and drafts

- Sketch the mobile and desktop layouts in a design tool before building in PageFly. Design tools such as Canva or Photoshop let you export the images straight into PageFly. [L1462.021]
- PageFly is opened from the Shopify admin under Apps, then PageFly. Saving a page keeps the draft, but changes only go live when the page is published, so keep test pages unpublished until the layout is finished. [L1462.053]
- Test pages in PageFly should stay unpublished while you work on them. Save them as drafts and do not publish until the layout is finished. [L1462.052]

### PageFly layout system

#### Grid and sections

- Web page layouts are built from stacked rows, and each row holds side-by-side columns. Grid systems usually use 12 columns because 12 divides cleanly into both 3 and 4 columns. [L1462.001]
- Stacking many sections on mobile makes a landing page very long and tiring to scroll. Decide early which blocks can share a row with two or more columns on mobile. [L1462.004]
- Uneven grids are built by splitting a row into proportions, such as one full-width row, or a 50% column next to a section split into two 25% parts. Combining rows and nested columns creates complex layouts. [L1462.005]

#### Building a two-column product section

1. Click Plus, then Layout, and insert a two-column row.
2. Select the row and, in the General tab, turn off Fixed width so the section runs edge to edge.
3. In the General tab, turn off the row's column spacing, which PageFly adds by default.
4. Remove the row's top and bottom padding.
5. Select each column and set its background color under Styling > Background color, using a six-digit hex code.
6. In the first column, add the heading, the star rating icon, the rating text and the review images. Set images to block where they must stack, or to inline and fixed width where they must sit side by side.
7. In the second column, add the product title, the price, the description and the product images, then adjust the image widths.
8. Add inside padding on the columns, not on the section, so colored column backgrounds stay full-bleed.
9. Switch between desktop, laptop, tablet and mobile views and adjust the padding and column counts for each device. [L1462.022]

- Column background color is set under Styling > Background color, where a hex code can be typed into the field. Use the same hex code for other elements that should match the accent color, such as star icons or borders. [L1462.025]
- If a column has a background color, padding applied to the parent section pushes the colored background away from the edges. Put the inside padding on the column itself instead. [L1462.026]

#### Spacing and the box model

- In the box model, padding is the space inside an element between its content and its border, and margin is the space outside the element that separates it from neighbouring elements. [L1462.007]
- In PageFly, set padding and margin under the element's Styling tab. Enter one value for all four sides, or enter separate values for top, bottom, left or right. [L1462.008]
- In PageFly, edits made on desktop cascade down to tablet and mobile unless a smaller device has its own value. Edits made on mobile do not flow back up to desktop. [L1462.009]
- If a mobile value is left empty, PageFly applies whatever padding or margin you add on mobile to that element. To remove inherited spacing on mobile, enter 0 for that value explicitly. [L1462.010]
- Text placed directly against a border looks cramped. Add inside padding so the content sits clear of the edge. [L1462.012]
- An inner block groups several elements so one margin or padding value moves them as a single unit. Use this to center a text stack or to space a group of elements from the element above. [L1462.028]
- A common starting value for inside padding on mobile in PageFly is 15 px on each side. Adjust the value for each individual layout rather than applying it everywhere. [L1462.043]
- Excess inside padding in a narrow column squeezes text onto too many lines. Reduce the padding on smaller devices before reducing font sizes. [L1462.042]

#### Borders

- Borders are set under Styling > Border, where you can choose a color, a width and which sides get a border. Border radius rounds the corners, and the More settings option gives the radius value. [L1462.011]
- To place a border on only one side of a PageFly element, set a border width and color for that side and make the other three sides transparent or zero. This leaves a single accent line, such as a vertical bar on the left of a review or feature block. [L1462.054]

#### Display, image and text sizing

- Block elements take the full width of their container and stack on top of each other. Inline elements sit next to each other on the same line and only take the width they need. [L1462.013]
- Inline elements do not accept custom height and width values. Inline-block elements sit side by side like inline elements but accept width and height settings. [L1462.014]
- Images are inline by default, but PageFly displays them at 100% width. To place several images next to each other, set the image width yourself or switch them to inline display. [L1462.015]
- To set a fixed image width in PageFly, select the image, open the General tab, and set Enable for width to yes. Then enter a pixel value, for example 100 px for a small logo or icon. [L1462.016]
- Set PageFly image alignment under Styling to left, center or right. Setting text align to center on a row centers all text and inline elements inside that row. [L1462.017]
- Use a heading for the page title and smaller headings for sections. Set font family, bold, and size under the element's typography settings to build a clear hierarchy. [L1462.027]
- Set an embedded video to a 4:3 aspect ratio in the General tab for a shorter, wider frame. Choose a different ratio if the layout needs a taller video. [L1462.044]
- Applying display flex to an image in PageFly can crop the image when the image has a minimum width. After switching an image to flex, check the preview and adjust its width or switch it back to block if it is clipped. [L1462.055]
- A review line on a product section can be built from three inline items in one row: a small star icon, a text line with the review count, and a logo. Give the images a fixed pixel width, such as 100 px for small icons, and add a 10 px margin on each side to space them evenly. [L1462.059]

#### Positioning

- Position relative moves an element away from its original spot using top, bottom, left or right values, and the space it occupied stays reserved. Negative or positive margin, by contrast, moves the elements below it as well. [L1462.033]
- Position absolute fixes an element inside its nearest container, and that container must have position relative. Without a relative parent, the absolute element will not stay where you place it. [L1462.034]
- Position static is the default for every element. Set an element back to static to reset positioning that was previously changed, for example on mobile. [L1462.035]
- Set an element to position fixed with top 0 and left 0 to pin it to the top of the browser window. This makes a sticky navigation bar that stays visible while the visitor scrolls. [L1462.031]
- A PageFly element set to position fixed may not appear at all until it has a width. Setting a width, such as 50%, makes it visible so the position can be checked, and the width can be changed afterwards. [L1462.056]
- Use margin and relative positioning for most element placement, because they keep the surrounding layout predictable. Reserve position absolute for small elements inside a container that is already sized and set to position relative. [L1462.058]

#### Flexbox, grid and centering

- Set display to flex under the element's Styling tab to turn it into a flexbox container. Then use flex direction (row, row reverse, column, column reverse), align items, align content and justify content to control position. [L1462.036]
- Flexbox creates fluid layouts that wrap items onto the next line when they reach their minimum width. This suits forms such as name, email and submit fields, where the layout should adapt to the screen size. [L1462.037]
- Grid gives precise control over proportions and placement, while flexbox gives easier control over direction and alignment. Use grid for fixed column layouts and flexbox when items need to reverse order or center easily. [L1462.038]
- To center content vertically and horizontally in PageFly, set the container to display flex and set align items, align content and justify content to center. This replaces margin-auto and padding hacks. [L1462.039]

Reorder columns for mobile so the title appears above the product image:

1. Select the row and set its mobile columns per line to 1 so the columns stack.
2. Under the row's Styling tab, set display to flex.
3. Set flex direction to column reverse so the second column appears first.
4. Remove any column padding that is too wide for the narrow screen, then set padding of 15 px on each side as a starting point.
5. Check the layout in mobile preview and adjust the spacing. [L1462.040]

#### Mobile and device checks

- Check every device view after each change, including desktop, laptop, tablet and mobile. A layout that works on a wide screen can squeeze text into narrow columns on smaller screens. [L1462.041]
- On mobile, change review text or other inline items to block display when they spill past the screen edge. Block display forces each item onto its own line and keeps the text inside the container. [L1462.057]

#### Backgrounds and sliders

- Set a background image on a column under Styling > Background. Choose cover to fill the area, set background repeat to no repeat to stop tiling, and center the image position so the main subject is visible. [L1462.046]
- Opacity applies to the whole element, so lowering it on a background image also fades the background color set on that same element. Pre-darken the image in a design tool with a semi-transparent black overlay instead, and export it. [L1462.048]
- The PageFly slideshow element holds slides, and each slide can be duplicated after styling. The General tab controls the navigation style, the partial items display, rewind and auto play. [L1462.049]
- Showing a partial next slide in a slideshow signals to visitors that the content scrolls, which encourages them to swipe or click through the remaining items. [L1462.050]

#### Further details

- Place free returns messaging (e.g., '30-day Free Returns') as a simple text widget below the Add to Cart button at the moment of purchase decision, ensuring high visibility at the critical conversion point. Implementation: 4 clicks in Visually. [L0197.009]

- PageFly is a landing page builder used to create the sales page for the digital product on Shopify. [L0513.024]

## Discounts, offers, email capture and post-purchase



#### Further details

- Content templates must be customized and installed before traffic setup to ensure message alignment with your store positioning. [L0040.013]

- 1. Go to Shopify Admin. 2. Click Discounts. 3. Click Create discount. 4. Select Amount off order. 5. Set discount type to Percentage. 6. Set discount value to 5%. 7. Use discount code SAVE5. 8. Apply to all customers. 9. Do not set a minimum purchase requirement. 10. Do not set usage limits. 11. Do not set an end date. 12. Save the discount. [L0139.003]

- 1. Go to Sales Channels > Online Store > Themes. 2. Click Edit theme. 3. Switch page view from Home page to Products. 4. Select Default product template. 5. Expand Product information in the left sidebar. 6. Click the eye icon next to Expiring coupon to enable it. 7. Update the text to "5% Off All Orders". 8. Enter coupon code SAVE5. 9. Set expiration to 3 days. 10. Choose a theme color matching your brand. 11. Use Month/Day/Year date format for US sales. 12. Save changes. [L0139.004]

1. Log in to your Shopify Admin.
2. Click Apps.
3. Search for 'Shopify Messaging' in the App Store.
4. Select Shopify Messaging (best match).
5. Click Install.
6. Approve required permissions.
[L0147.003]

1. Open Shopify Messaging settings.
2. Click Template branding.
3. Select Customize.
4. Upload your store logo.
5. Choose your store font (default sans-serif is acceptable).
6. Keep email background and text simple.
7. Set button fill color to match your brand.
[L0147.005]

1. Go to Shopify Settings.
2. Click Notifications.
3. Set sender email to sales@yourdomain.com.
4. This address will be used for all Shopify Messaging emails.
[L0147.008]

- Access abandoned checkout automation: Shopify Admin → Shopify Messaging app → Create automation → Select template 'Recover Abandoned Checkout' → Turn on automation. Edit the workflow in Shopify Flow via Marketing → Automations → Install Shopify Flow. [L0149.004]

1. Open the Shopify Inbox app.
2. Click "Turn on chat" to open the theme customizer.
3. Choose a widget color to match your brand.
4. Change the text color and button color.
5. Select an icon (chat bubble, smiley, hand wave, etc.).
6. Keep the widget positioned in the bottom-right.
7. Customize the greeting text (e.g., "Welcome to [Store Name]" with an offer to help).
8. Save changes in the theme customizer.
9. Refresh your storefront.
10. Confirm the chat icon appears.
11. Send a test message to yourself.
[L0151.005]

- Use the Manhattan Shopify theme to create email capture pop-ups for bounced visits, and configure Shopify Email to deliver the follow-up automation. [L0154.004]

1. Log in to Shopify Admin.
2. Click Apps.
3. Search for 'Judge.me'.
4. Select Judge.me Product Reviews app.
5. Install the free plan.
[L0155.006]

- To customize review branding: Open Judge.me settings → Branding → set the primary color to match your store. This color will appear on stars in review emails and widgets. [L0155.007]

- To position the Review Widget: Go to Online Store → Themes → Edit theme → switch to a product page → find the Review Widget under Apps → drag and drop to position. Recommended placement is under the product description. [L0155.009]

- On the homepage, create a prominent box labeled 'Your cart is ready' that (1) displays the customer's current cart items; (2) shows the total cart value; (3) includes a single 'Continue to checkout' button to re-engage browsers who navigated away from the cart after adding items. [L0197.012]

- On cart pages, add a product recommendation section labeled 'You May Also Like' that (1) displays curated product options; (2) includes zoom buttons allowing customers to view product images at larger size without leaving the cart page; (3) includes add-to-cart buttons that add items immediately without navigating away; (4) rank products by best-selling items. [L0197.018]

- To configure the Charlotte theme's email popup on Shopify, navigate to the admin dashboard, go to Online Store > Themes > Customize, open the gear icon menu, and find Email Pop-up Settings. Activate the popup toggle, enable logo upload and add your store's logo, and set the popup to appear after 60 seconds. Enter your discount code, write the heading as 'wait, don't leave us', and use the body text 'enter your email below to receive a special discount from [Store-Name]'. Leave the countdown timer disabled since it's unsuitable for this campaign. Set the button copy to 'send me the discounts', adjust colors to match your site's branding, and save the changes. [L0201.004]

- The Shopify Email automation sequence for welcome messages works identically regardless of how you collect the initial email address. You can use Charlotte's built-in popup, any third-party popup app, a landing page form, or any other collection method-the downstream automation flow remains the same. You are not locked into one theme or tool; focus on collecting the email and the automation takes over from that point. [L0201.016]

- Aftership is used as a tracking website for order tracking notifications. [L0255.022]

- Shopify Email is sufficient for promotional email marketing; Klaviyo is not necessary as an additional expense. [L0255.023]

- Coupon code bar location: Shopify admin > Online Store > Theme Settings > left navigation panel > find Coupon Code Bar section > enable/disable as needed. [L0270.021]

- If the 'Recover Abandoned Checkout' template does not appear first in the Shopify Messaging app templates list when creating an automation, search for it by name using the search field. [L0282.017]

- 1. Create new Zapier Zap named 'AC to Slack' or similar. 2. Select Shopify as trigger app and choose 'New Abandoned Cart'. 3. Authenticate Shopify account and select store. 4. Confirm delay set to 0 hours. 5. Add action: send Slack channel message. 6. Authenticate Slack and select channel (e.g., #steve). 7. Insert customer fields (name, phone, order total, checkout URL) using Zapier's field insertion tool. 8. Save and activate Zap. [L0286.010]

- Use large typography for a headline announcing that customers receive a free bonus item with their product purchase. Display a photograph of the bonus product and state its retail value in dollars. Confirm that this complimentary item is automatically included with purchases made at your store. [L0314.008]

- An organic store should look professional, branded and simple, with a short purchase path. Avoid stacking pop-ups that force visitors to close windows, although a single email signup is acceptable. [L0352.007]

- For reviews on a product page, add many more reviews as view counts grow, since visible social proof is expected to improve conversion. Place the review block right after the description. [L0352.009]

- For products on an organic-only collection, use promotions to the email list and social audiences rather than paid traffic. Products under the normal ad threshold can be sold this way. [L0359.046]

- Bonjoro integrates directly with Shopify. When a new order is placed, the customer's name and email are sent to Bonjoro and appear in a daily list of thank-yous to record, so the team does not need to look up details in Shopify. [L0406.010]

- Scribeless integrates directly with Shopify and pulls the customer's shipping address from the Shopify order. The note is then packaged and mailed automatically without manual address entry. [L0406.012]

- Video thank-yous need only a webcam or phone camera with a built-in microphone. A professional microphone is unnecessary, and headphones with an inline mic can be used as well. [L0406.018]

Holiday site banner pattern: a seasonal sale headline plus a coupon code that the customer enters at checkout. Formula: [Holiday] sale, enter coupon code [CODE] to check out. The code is unique to the holiday, so you can track its sales, and the banner catches first-time visitors from paid ads.
Example 1: Winter sale, enter coupon code WINTER20 to check out.
Example 2: Summer sale, enter coupon code SUNNY15 to check out.
[L0467.044]

Setting up a holiday sale on the store:
1. Create a coupon code that is unique to the holiday sale.
2. Add the sale to the homepage image slider.
3. Show a banner strip across the whole site with the sale name and the coupon code.
4. Check that first-time visitors from Google Ads will see the banner.
5. Link the sale to a dedicated collection page with all the holiday offers.
[L0467.045]

- Create one dedicated collection page for the holiday offers and send all emails and remarketing ads there. Do not link them to the homepage or to a single product page. [L0467.046]

Create the pop-up coupon in Shopify:
1. Go to Discounts in the Shopify admin and click Create discount.
2. Select Amount off order.
3. Enter the discount code and name (example: save five).
4. Set the value as a fixed amount of $5.
5. Set the usage limit to one use per customer so the code cannot be reused repeatedly.
6. Click Save discount.
[L0476.007]

- Add a short product quiz when the catalogue is large, so visitors who feel overwhelmed by choice get a recommended product after a few questions. [L0524.019]

Create the expiring discount code in the Shopify admin:
1. Open Discounts in the sidebar of the Shopify admin.
2. Click Create discount code.
3. Enter the code name, for example save 10.
4. Choose the discount type Percentage.
5. Enter 10 as the discount value for 10%.
6. Keep all products selected and apply it to all products in all collections.
7. Leave minimum requirements unset.
8. Set customer eligibility to everyone.
9. Leave usage limits unset.
10. Leave the end date empty; a start date may be set.
11. Click Save discount code.
[L0531.011]

- Do not set an end date on the Shopify discount itself, because these codes are recycled in later cycles. The deadline is controlled through the announcement bar text instead. [L0531.012]

- All bundle items must sit in one Shopify collection, and that collection must be the Applies to target of the automatic discount. A collection that holds only part of the bundle will leave some items without the discount. [L0559.017]

- Countdown timers should not be added at launch, because they only make sense once the store is generating sales. Add them later as part of conversion work, not as a standard element of the first test. [L0600.013]

- Personalization in Klaviyo emails: use Add personalization to insert the customer's first name, last name or email address into the text. [L0607.027]

- Mistake: hiding the free gift or offer until add-to-cart on mobile, so visitors do not see the offer before the cart; state the free gift or offer on the product page itself. [L0640.040]

- Create an automatic buy X get Y discount in Shopify: Discounts > Create discount > choose Buy X get Y > name it (for example 'Buy 2 items and get the third one for free') > choose Automate discount > set the quantity condition (buy 2 items of a specific collection) > set the free item from the collection > Save discount. [L0650.024]

- Communicate a discount on a Shopify collection page: 1) Open the collection in Shopify. 2) Edit its description. 3) Add the offer text as a large headline or just above the logo. 4) Format it, for example as Heading 3 and in a clearly visible color. 5) Save the collection page and refresh the store page to confirm the offer shows. [L0650.025]

- A buy-X-get-Y offer must be checked against the margin: if the product is high-ticket (for example above $100), giving an item free may not make sense, so the calculation must always work and the store must make profit. [L0650.027]

- The cart and checkout should show the same offer as the one promised on the website and in the ads, including the discounted price and the free item, so customers see the same deal at every step. [L0650.028]

- A store with several pop-ups, especially pop-ups stacked on top of each other, pushes shoppers away. Limit pop-ups. [L0656.018]

Editing or removing a coupon bar in the Source A Shopify theme:
1. Open the Shopify admin.
2. Go to Online Store.
3. Click Theme settings.
4. Open the coupon code section and edit the offer, for example to 5% off or free shipping.
[L0664.031]

- Do not delete the coupon bar entirely, because it helps conversions. Change the offer instead, for example to 5% off or free shipping. [L0664.032]

- Judge.me is the recommended review app for Shopify because of its deep integration; reviews appear on product pages automatically and star ratings can show in Google Shopping ads. Judge.me is a Google-approved partner and its reviews show in Google Shopping. [L0665.032]

- Use a free live chat widget that only opens when a visitor clicks a small button in the corner, instead of intrusive pop-up offers; full-screen pop-ups usually look messy on laptops and are not recommended. [L0665.039]

- Upfront product pricing matters: a store needs a clear offer and a reasonable price for its products, and overpriced items without a bundle can hurt conversion. [L0678.037]

- A poor offer, such as a single item priced high without a bundle or compelling deal, can lower conversion. Test different offers and price points before scaling. [L0678.038]

Modify the store so visitors see the active offer:
1. Promote the offer in the notification bar.
2. Promote the offer in the exit intent overlay.
3. If the offer needs one (for example a bundle), create a custom product page for it.
4. If the offer needs one (for example an entered-to-win offer), create a content page that explains the offer details.
5. If the offer needs one (for example a bonus offer), edit the current product pages to show the offer.
6. If the offer needs one (for example spend X to get X), use Bold Sales Motivator to show how far the shopper is from the free bonus.
[L0757.029]

- Install a one-click upsell app such as Zipify on Shopify so that the post-purchase page shows a one-time offer before the regular thank-you page. The upsell should be a different product, not a bundle of the original, and priced at or below 14 dollars, because higher prices can hurt conversion. [L0789.030]

- Add a Messenger opt-in to the product page so visitors can subscribe to messages when they add to cart. This lets abandoned cart messages reach them through Facebook Messenger instead of only email. [L0790.015]

- Product review apps should display reviews only when you have permission. Use reviews provided by the supplier. Do not copy reviews from competitors' sites, since they can report the store and cause a suspension. [L0801.101]

- A supplier's reviews can be used on your product pages when the supplier gives permission. Suppliers often do not collect reviews themselves because they rely on retailers for marketing. [L0801.102]

- Enable product reviews and product ratings only after the store has about 50 product reviews, because some eligibility requirements depend on a minimum number of reviews. Promotions can be enabled earlier. [L0835.075]

- Use countdown timers only for real promotions with an end date, such as a monthly promotion or a Black Friday or Cyber Monday sale, and not as a permanent feature. [L0835.076]

- An order-tracking app can add an order tracking page to the store, using a code provided by the app. Look for a tracking option that does not slow down the store. [L0835.082]

- Shopify email to add SMS is coming; SMS apps are not needed for a new store at the start. [L0856.066]

Steps to produce and deliver the ebook bonus:
1. Generate the full ebook text with Claude (claude.ai) using the ebook prompt.
2. Copy the generated content.
3. Paste it into Canva and apply a simple ebook template.
4. Export the design as a PDF.
5. Upload the PDF to Shopify.
6. Deliver it automatically with every order.
[L0860.018]

1. Open the Digital Downloads app and select the product you use as the free gift (for example a product named e-book).
2. Click Add asset, then File.
3. Upload the PDF file that was generated for the ebook.
4. Confirm the file appears under Assets.
5. Open the product page and go to the Shipping section.
6. Tick Not a physical product so the order carries no shipping.
7. Click Save.
8. In the bundle, attach this product as the free gift for bars 2 and 3 and confirm it is available.
[L0861.025]

- Judge.me widget color is set in the app under Settings, by opening the review widget section and using the color picker. Choose the store's main brand color so the stars and widget match the color scheme. [L0908.016]

- A Shopify theme does not sell digital products by itself. For free bonuses, you can deliver links to PDFs or unlisted videos without an app, and paid digital products need a Shopify app that handles automated delivery. [L0934.057]

- The chat button appears in the bottom right corner of the store by default, on both mobile and desktop. The recommended button style is the plain chat icon only, which saves space and is recognizable without text. [L0948.006]

- Chat greeting example used in the store: "Hi, message us with any questions. We're happy to help." This text can be set as the welcome message in the chat settings. [L0948.008]

- On Shopify Plus, Checkout Plus can be shown in the cart and in checkout, and the shopper can change the choice in checkout, using Shopify's checkout extensibility. Without Shopify Plus, it only shows in the cart and the choice cannot be adjusted in checkout. Package protection has the same limit. [L0955.026]

- Checkout Plus is set up as a product in Shopify, so it appears on the order. Shopify Markets allows a different price per market, and the offer does not have to run in every market. [L0955.027]

- Setting up a branded tracking page with Wonderment: 1. Use the Wonderment block provided for Shopify. 2. Create a new page the same way as a collection, product or about-us page. 3. Insert the tracking block on that page. 4. Add subscriptions, upsells or cross-sells to the page. 5. Send customers to this page instead of the carrier's tracking page. [L0955.028]

- Shopify forced a migration of the thank-you and order status pages, and these pages can hold tracking blocks, upsells and cross-sells. Treat them as a sales channel instead of leaving them at the default setup. [L0955.057]

- Create a dedicated loyalty program page on the Shopify store with the program details. Link it in the footer and/or the main navigation so customers can find it while browsing. Optionally add a link to the page on product pages, though this takes space and is not required. [L0956.009]

- Use a tracking tool called After Ship for order status. Logged-in customers can see their order status on the site. [L0964.100]

- Place reviews on product pages, ideally on the products that already get impressions and clicks, because those are the pages shoppers already view. A store-wide review page is an acceptable fallback. [L0968.040]

- The review date display and review images are controlled by your review app, not by the theme. Look for date or image options in the review app's settings. [L0968.041]

- Add a personal thank-you and a point-of-contact line to the default Shopify order confirmation template rather than sending a separate follow-up email. This covers both messages in one email. [L0968.042]

- A page on a Shopify store can show product-specific supplier reviews only with written supplier permission. Enter them through the review app on the product page rather than copying or screenshotting them. [L0970.076]

- Customize and reuse supplier reviews: get the supplier's permission first, then enter the review manually in the Judge.me app as if a customer had written it. Judge.me uses no automation here. [L0971.033]

- Shopify Inbox setup for live chat: enable it in the Shopify admin; install the Shopify Inbox app to receive messages on the phone; to pause chat on the site, disable the chat box in the Shopify Inbox settings. [L0971.055]

- The built-in pop-ups, cross-selling and social proof features are meant for stores that already get traffic and sales. A demo store still being built does not need them and should not spend time on them. [L0973.001]

- A Shopify store can take sales without any customer ratings or reviews, so reviews are not required before launch. [L1002.076]

Hi [Supplier name],

We have just uploaded your products to our website and want to show our customers that they are high quality. Is it okay for us to use the reviews from your website? If you have reviews you could send us, we would appreciate it.

Thank you,
[Your name]
[Company name]
[L1069.021]

- Do not import product reviews from Amazon, because those reviews belong to Amazon. Reviews from a supplier's own website can be used after asking the supplier for permission. [L1069.023]

- Build the lead generation landing page as a Shopify page with a small amount of code, or use the free version of PageFly, a landing page builder for Shopify. The page usually sits at a store URL such as yourstore.com/freebook. [L1076.021]

- Shopify admin > Store > Theme settings is where the store's coupon code and promotion text are edited in the theme. If the option is not visible in the left navigation, check the theme settings path in the admin. [L1088.027]

- Hidden add-on products are created in Shopify but left out of every collection and kept out of search. They are shown only as upsells or add-ons through an upsell app such as Bold Upsell. [L1088.028]

- Do not set up discount banners or coupons until the store is live, sending traffic, and has products ready to sell. Before that point they have no effect. [L1110.024]

- Set the live chat app to show a greeting such as asking the visitor for their question and email address, or hide the chat widget when no one is available. [L1111.070]

- Embedded email opt-in page with Klaviyo: 1. Create a sign-up form inside Klaviyo. 2. Copy the embed code Klaviyo provides. 3. Create a new page in Shopify. 4. Paste the embed code into that page so new sign-ups are tagged and can trigger automations. [L1114.031]

- The current Shopify theme does not have a built-in opt-in code. If no email tool provides an embed form, a third-party Shopify app is needed to place a sign-up form on a page. [L1114.032]

- The automatic shipping confirmation email to the customer is only sent if the "send a confirmation to the customer" tick box is checked in the Fulfil Items popup. If the box is left unticked, the customer gets no fulfilment notice and must be contacted manually. [L1119.013]

- The Shrine theme includes a built-in quantity discount feature. Use it to offer multi-unit deals on a product test store instead of building a separate bundle page. [L1171.058]

- Expiring coupon (discount code) block in the product information section: it shows discount codes that switch on and off based on dates. For a demo store, keep it disabled or hidden; hide it with the eyedropper icon instead of deleting it. [L1189.014]

- Sale banner image block: it shows a sale message and a discount code at checkout. It is not needed for a demo store and can be hidden. [L1189.019]

Install the Q&A app on the product page:
1. Install the app from its listing and accept the permissions it requests.
2. Click Add questions and answers when the app offers to add itself to the store.
3. In the product page template, add a section to the page.
4. Choose Apps in the section list, then select Questions and answers.
5. Click Save, and check a product page on the live store to confirm the section appears.
[L1190.029]

1. In the left navigation under Template, click the arrow next to Product information to expand the section.
2. Hover over the Expiring coupon entry and click the eye icon to make it visible again, since it was hidden when the demo products were uploaded.
3. Click Expiring coupon so its settings open in the right-hand panel.
4. If the right-hand panel does not appear, zoom out in the browser until it shows.
[L1192.011]

Preparing the store for each promotion window:
1) Replace the notification bar with the current offer.
2) Swap the exit-intent overlay to show the offer.
3) Create a custom product page for a bundle, or modify the existing product page if the offer is small.
4) For an event, create a content page with the details and eligibility rules.
5) Add a progress tracker such as Bold Sales Motivator if the offer depends on a cart threshold.
6) Update the promotions page with the offer, if you use one.
[L1204.049]

- Keep one static promotions page and swap its content when a promotion starts, since this is easier to remove later. Bundles and bonus gifts should get their own page, so they can be removed without affecting the rest. Event pages that may rank in search can stay live and later become evergreen content. [L1204.051]

Closing a promotion, done manually after the window:
1) Set the notification bar back to the default offer.
2) Restore the previous exit-intent overlay.
3) Remove bundle or bonus content from product pages and return them to their default state.
4) Replace the offer on the promotions page with the default offer or the next offer.
5) Turn off progress trackers such as Bold Sales Motivator, so no shopper reaches a goal that unlocks nothing.
[L1204.052]

- Show the same holiday message on every promotional placement during the promotion: the homepage image slider, the announcement bar, and the expiring discount code section on product pages. Change them when the promotion changes. [L1224.030]

- Rebuy's Shopify Functions discounting currently supports Free Product, Order Discount and Fixed Price tiers. It works on all Shopify plans, Basic, Advanced and Plus, while Shopify Scripts require the Plus script editor. [L1231.033]

- Adding a tracking page with AfterShip on a Shopify store: 1. Install the AfterShip app from the Shopify app store. 2. AfterShip sends automated tracking emails to customers. 3. Copy the embed code that AfterShip provides and place it on a store page, which then works as the order tracking page where customers enter their tracking number. [L1281.050]

1. In the Shopify admin, open Apps and then Visit the Shopify App Store.
2. Search for talk to and open the tawk.to Live Chat app.
3. Click Install app. Shopify lists the data the app can view and manage (orders, customers, online store); approve it, because the app cannot work without it.
4. You are redirected to a tawk.to page. Click Sign Up and create a new account, then complete the standard sign-up process.
5. At the Install Widget step, do not paste the tracking code. Open the list of platforms, select Shopify, then click Done.
6. Return to the Shopify app, log in with your tawk.to account, select your widget (the name entered during sign-up) and click Use selected widget.
7. Confirm the success message and open the storefront to check that the chat bubble appears at the bottom of the screen.
8. Adjust the widget appearance in the tawk.to settings (see the widget appearance setting).
[L1298.007]

- Shopify includes built-in email marketing for follow-up emails to buyers and to visitors who leave without buying, as well as automatic order tracking so customers can follow their shipment until delivery. [L1304.005]

- Shopify Admin > Discounts lists every discount created by Functions. Product tier discounts appear with the name and type Rebuy Product Discount, and order tier discounts with Rebuy Order Discount. The admin name never matches the text shown at checkout. [L1308.010]

- All product tiers of one Smart Cart are grouped into one single Rebuy Product Discount in Shopify Admin, so adding a second product tier does not create a second discount. Likewise, all discount tiers are grouped into one Rebuy Order Discount. [L1308.011]

Create a standard order discount code in the Shopify admin:
1. Open the Discounts section in the Shopify admin. No discounts exist by default.
2. Click Create discount.
3. Choose Amount off order.
4. Select discount code instead of automatic discount.
5. Enter the code name, for example save 10.
6. Choose percentage (the usual choice) or fixed amount, and enter the value, for example 10%.
7. Under minimum requirements, enter a minimum order value such as 2000, or leave it at no minimum.
8. Under customer eligibility, restrict the code to specific customer segments if needed.
9. Set maximum discount uses: 5 total, 10 total, or once per customer, or leave it unlimited.
10. Keep the combination with other promotions turned off.
11. Keep the start date at the current date and time, or set a later start.
12. For a time-limited promotion, set an end date.
13. Click save discount.
[L1322.001]

- Shopify offers four discount types: amount off products, amount off order, buy X get Y, and free shipping. Amount off order is the type used most often for order-level discount codes. [L1322.002]

- A demo store does not need discount codes. Create them once the store is live and taking real orders. [L1322.004]

- On the Shopify checkout page, the customer sees a discount code field, enters the code, and clicks apply. In the demo, a 10% code gave a 319.60 dollar discount, which implies an order subtotal of about 3196 dollars. [L1322.012]

- A store can hold as many discount codes as needed, and all of them are listed together in the Discounts overview. This allows one-off codes for individual buyers to be managed alongside general promotions. [L1322.013]

Test a discount code on the storefront before sending it to a buyer:
1. Add a product to the cart on the store.
2. Open the cart and go to the checkout.
3. In the discount code field on the checkout page, enter the code.
4. Click apply and check the discount amount that appears for the order.
[L1322.021]

- On-site upsell positions are: (1) on the product page, shown before Add to cart; (2) on the cart page, shown before checkout (pre-checkout upsell); (3) after payment but before the thank-you page (post-checkout upsell), which inserts an extra page into the checkout flow. [L1409.004]

- Shopify checkout supports at most two levels of post-purchase upsells; after two offers the customer must land on the thank-you page. [L1410.027]

Post-purchase structure (the monster funnel), in order:
1. The customer buys the main item and completes payment.
2. Show the first upsell.
3. If accepted, show the second upsell; if declined, show the first downsell.
4. If the second upsell is accepted or declined, show the thank-you page.
5. If the first downsell is accepted, show the thank-you page; if declined, show the thank-you page.
[L1412.027]

- Shopify checkout allows at most two levels of post-purchase upsells, so you cannot show three or four offer pages before the thank-you page. Plan the funnel with a maximum of two offers plus a downsell path. [L1412.028]

- Enable a post-purchase funnel in Shopify under Settings > Checkout. Scroll down to the post-purchase page section and select Reconvert upsell and cross-sell. The page may take a while to load after the change. [L1412.029]

- Change post-purchase discounts under Discounts and shipping in the upsell app. You can switch the discount between percent and fixed amount per unit, change the timer, and edit the button text. [L1412.033]

- A gift card of 80 Swiss francs was sold, and buyers received a code by email. Around Christmas, three to five gift cards were sold, many of them were never redeemed, and that amount became revenue. Gift cards sold poorly outside peak gifting periods but can add revenue during Christmas. [L1439.037]

- Create an automatic discount in the Shopify store so that when two specific products are both added to the cart, one of them is given free. The customer does not need a code. [L1469.006]

- Do not show a review widget that states a product has no reviews. A visible zero-review signal (negative social proof) suggests nobody has bought it or that it is not good, so hide the widget until reviews exist. Most Shopify review apps allow the widget to be hidden when a product has zero reviews. [L1522.010]

## Social profiles, tracking and Google readiness



#### Further details

- To create a Facebook business page: 1. Log in to Facebook. 2. Click See more in the left navigation menu. 3. Click Pages. 4. Click Create Page. 5. Select Public Page. 6. Click Get started. [L0157.004]

- Configure basic Facebook page details: enter your Store Name (not the full URL) in the Page name field; select a relevant Category for your business type (use AI to identify the appropriate category for your niche if uncertain); set Hours to Always open. [L0157.005]

- Add Facebook contact information: enter your store URL in the Website field; enter sales@yourdomain.com in the Email field; optionally add a Phone number; do not use the Address field for ecommerce stores. [L0157.006]

- Set your Facebook page's profile picture by uploading your store logo, cropping it to show a clean symbol or icon version if the original doesn't fit well in the circular profile format; upload your store header or banner image as the cover photo. [L0157.007]

- Add a Facebook page action button: select 'Learn more' as the button label and link it to your store homepage. [L0157.008]

- Configure your Facebook page's vanity URL: navigate to Settings & privacy > Page setup > Name to establish a username matching your brand (such as facebook.com/YourStoreName); if the exact name is taken, select the closest available alternative. [L0157.009]

- Write a short Facebook bio based on your store's About Us page: use AI to draft the bio, then trim it to fit within Facebook's character limit if necessary. [L0157.010]

- To create an Instagram business account: 1. Go to Instagram.com on desktop or open the mobile app. 2. Click Create new account. 3. Enter your store email (sales@yourdomain.com). 4. Create a password and complete the setup. 5. Enter your Store Name as the full name. 6. Choose a username as close as possible to your store name. [L0157.011]

- To switch Instagram to a business account: 1. Click More. 2. Click Settings. 3. Scroll down and click Account type and tools. 4. Click Switch to a professional account. 5. Select Business. 6. Choose a relevant category (Shopping & retail is typical for ecommerce). 7. Confirm the switch to professional account. [L0157.012]

- Configure your Instagram profile: upload the same logo or icon used for your Facebook profile picture; paste your Facebook bio into the Instagram bio field, trimming if necessary; note that Instagram prioritizes mobile viewing, so some settings like the website link may require configuration through the mobile app rather than desktop. [L0157.013]

- Link your social media profiles to your Shopify store: navigate to Shopify Admin > Online Store > Themes > Edit theme > Theme settings (gear icon) > Social media, paste your Facebook Page URL and Instagram profile URL, then click Save. [L0157.014]

- Enable social media icons in your store footer: navigate to Theme Customizer > Footer settings, toggle ON the option 'Show social media icons', click Save, then preview your store to verify the icons link correctly to both Facebook and Instagram profiles. [L0157.015]

- Only authentic products from brands you have the right to sell should appear in the shop. Demonstration or invented items can trigger Google policy problems and put your accounts at risk. [L0167.002]

- Delete all demo products under Shopify Admin > Products. [L0167.003]

- Broken links and incomplete pages are a problem for Google, so a single 404 or unfinished page can cause compliance flags. [L0167.005]

- Remove every placeholder before submitting to Google: blank image boxes showing a circle with an X, 'image coming soon' graphics or text, and any unfinished section. The target is zero errors, zero placeholders and zero demo content. [L0167.006]
